# Weaving sections: a model for entrance lanes that also serve an exit

Design note written 2026-09-23 at 9738f2e (research and plan; no code). Every figure is from a named committed file; session-only figures are marked.

## 1. What a weaving section is, and why a lane-discrete merge locks there

HCM 7th ed. ch. 13 (Freeway Weaving Segments) defines a weave as two traffic streams crossing each other over a length of freeway without traffic control, created when an entrance is followed closely by an exit joined by an auxiliary lane. The controlling parameters are the short length L_S between the gore points, the configuration (how many lane changes each weaving movement *must* make — the HCM 2000 type A/B/C taxonomy, recast in the 6th/7th editions as one-sided vs two-sided with N_WL, the number of lanes from which a weave takes one change or none), the volume ratio, and the resulting lane-change density; speed is predicted separately for weaving and non-weaving streams, and capacity is reached when required lane-change density saturates.

Both MnDOT cases are one-sided ramp weaves (old type A: every weaving vehicle makes exactly one change, N_WL = 2). In `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.yaml` they are visible as an on-ramp and an off-ramp sharing an `attach_edge`: Ruth St (`on-ramp 745524613`) and the White Bear Ave exit (`off-ramp 18208090`) both on `999007700`, and T.H.52 (`on-ramp 769818012`) with the I-35E C-D exit (`off-ramp 18207598`) both on `51388891`. From the gore positions in `artifacts/demand_mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.json` the short lengths are 5,316.64 − 5,180.87 = **136 m** and 10,731.87 − 10,426.62 = **305 m** (detector-to-detector, `data/mndot/.../stations_x.csv`: 266 m and 581 m).

The lock has two layers. *Engine layer:* `microsim.networks.accel_lane_end` walks lane 0 downstream and refuses to terminate it when it feeds anything but the next corridor edge's lane 0 — the Ruth St case hits the `targets != [nxt]` branch, T.H.52 the `to_lanes != [0]` branch ("feeds both lanes of an exit piece", docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §6). Neither is a tail-length refusal: both are well inside `ACCEL_LANE_TAIL_MAX_M` = 400 m. So `_apply_merge_models` raises and every weave entrance is stuck on `lane_change`. (T.H.61 NB is the *other* branch — a 1,071 m added lane; its entrance detector `rnd_88807` has 2 lanes and the next station S1070 has 5 mainline lanes, so it is a lane addition/major merge, not a weave. It must not be counted as a weave success.) *Model layer:* under LC2013 both movements are mandatory changes against a deadline (lane end / gore). The entering vehicle waits for a gap in a lane occupied by exiting vehicles whose own mandatory change is into the lane the enterer is leaving; cooperative braking only slows the blocked vehicle; nobody creates the gap. In Treiber & Kesting's terms (MOBIL: incentive + politeness *p* + safety *b_safe*, plus a bias for mandatory changes) the exchange is symmetric and needs a two-sided incentive, which a lane-discrete deadline model does not supply. The result is a standing queue on the auxiliary lane — exactly the signature the I-24 flagship published: `artifacts/i24_lane_profile_zip.json` has the zipper family holding 17–33 % of vehicle-time in the acceleration lane at 4 km/h where the recording holds 6–8 % at 33–49 km/h, and the merge discharging ~5,880 veh/h against 6,630 observed (docs/I24_VALIDATION.md §0.11), an ~11 % deficit where GEH < 5 allows about 6 % (docs/MERGE_ROUND6_PLAN.md §1).

## 2. Three candidates, ranked by fit

**(A) `RampSpec.merge: "weave"` + `WeaveSpec`, two-sided gap acceptance — recommended.** Config (`packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py`): add `"weave"` to the `merge` literal and `weave: WeaveSpec | None` with `exit_ramp: str` (the paired off-ramp's `name`), `length_m: float | None` (else measured from the network), and `weave_params: dict[str, float]` validated against a `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` mirroring `SCRIPTED_MERGE_DEFAULTS` (`accept_gap_s`, `force_after_s`, `force_within_m`, `change_duration_s`, `lookahead_m`, `courtesy`, plus `exit_accept_gap_s`), both fields entering the config hash. Runner: `_apply_merge_models` skips termination *and* patches for `weave` (lane 0 must stay connected to the exit) and validates the pairing; `_scripted_merge_step` (runner.py:622–703) generalises to a direction argument — entering vehicles change left as today, exiting vehicles on lane 1 change right using `NEIGHBOR_RIGHT_*` gaps, with `courtesy` as MOBIL's politeness on both sides, and forcing allowed only for the exiting movement (its deadline is real). The exiting set is free: `route_by_id` (runner.py:1179) already carries `"*_off<j>"`. Trade-offs: TraCI-only, no netconvert, reuses a tested controller and its `meta.json` reporting; but it is a heuristic with new parameters and needs care that forced two-way changes do not produce collisions. **Cost ≈ 6–8 engineering days.**

**(B) Network-level split (merge piece + exit piece).** New `weave_patch_files` splitting the shared edge and re-connecting lane 0 so a merge taper dead-ends and a separate exit taper is fed from lane 1. `--ramps.guess` cannot produce this: it adds one `-AddedOnRampEdge` / `-AddedOffRampEdge` per ramp and, when both sit on one edge, leaves lane 0 continuous (the T.H.52 refusal message is precisely that). Mechanically it fits — `patch_net`, `expand_ramp_splits` and the compiled-id remapping all exist — but it forbids the exchange HCM says defines the section (a Ruth St vehicle could no longer reach White Bear Ave), deletes capacity, and pushes the same lock one edge upstream. Useful as a diagnostic bound only. **≈ 3–4 days.**

**(C) Sublane model on the weave edge.** `SimSpec.lateral_resolution_m` plus `FleetSpec.lc_sublane` / `lat_alignment` / `lc_pushy` / `min_gap_lat` already exist. Lowest fit: `SimSpec` is run-wide, not per-edge, so it changes every merge and every config hash, and I-24 excluded it (insertion 0.4299–0.4412 against the reference's 0.9349, docs/MERGE_ROUND6_PLAN.md §1; gridlock at defaults, §0.5(h)). **≈ 0.5 day** — run it *first* as the free falsifier, then discard.

**Tests (all three).** A planted-weave fixture beside `RAMP_OSM` / `SPILL_OSM` in `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_osm_ramps.py`: an on-ramp at the attach edge and a `motorway_link` leaving ~150 m downstream. Unit: `accel_lane_end` still refuses (the existing "weaving section" assertion at `test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py:434` becomes the positive detector); a new `weave_sections()` pairs the ramps and reports L_S. Run-level: both movements complete (`n_unfinished == 0`, off-ramp arrivals ≈ planned, `meta["n_collisions"] == 0`), determinism (`test_microsim_determinism.py`), a `tests/golden/merge_weave.json` beside `merge_scripted.json`, and schema rejections (weave on an off-ramp, unpaired exit, unknown `weave_params`).

**MnDOT validation, staged with deciders fixed now.** (1) Local 35-min peak slice, one seed: both weave entrances deliver ≥ 95 % of demand *and* their exits arrive within 5 % of planned. (2) Full 4-hour single seed: departed fraction ≥ 0.9 against round 2's 0.4062 [0.3911, 0.4210] of 34,367 planned (`round2_gridlock_record.json`); T.H.52 (peak 1,419 veh/h) rises from 2,078/5,047; Ruth St holds at 995/995; T.H.61 NB (2,196 veh/h) is *expected not to move* — it is the lane-addition case. (3) 20 seeds, ≈ 2,780 s wall per replicate on an n2-standard-32 at ≈ $1.55/h (docs/MERGE_ROUND6_PLAN.md §2), i.e. about one machine-hour, ≈ $1.6; both earlier rounds together cost ≈ $3.3.

## 3. The first two-day slice

*Day 1 — schema and geometry.* `packages/microsim/microsim/networks.py`: factor the lane-0 walk inside `accel_lane_end` (networks.py:646) into one traversal serving both the refusal and a new `weave_sections(net, chain, ramps) -> list[WeaveSection]` (paired on/off ramp, shared edges, L_S). `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py`: `RampSpec.merge` gains `"weave"`; add `WeaveSpec`, `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` and the `model_validator` rejections; docstrings citing docs/CONTRACTS.md §2. Add the `WEAVE_OSM` fixture and the schema/geometry tests.

*Day 2 — the controller.* `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py`: `_apply_merge_models` (runner.py:215) skips step (1) and (2) for `weave` and validates the pairing with a message naming the edges; a `weave_states` setup block beside `scripted_states` (runner.py:1259) using `route_by_id`; `_weave_step` from `_scripted_merge_step` with a direction argument; the call site at runner.py:1501; a `weave_sections` block in `meta.json` beside `scripted_merges` (runner.py:1715) carrying `n_entered/n_changed_in/n_changed_out/n_forced/n_unfinished/wait_s_mean`. Then the 200-s fixture run and the 35-min MnDOT peak slice locally. Contract text in docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 and a CHANGELOG entry land with the code.

## 4. What evidence would show the model is right

1. **I-24 is the falsification control, not the target.** Old Hickory's acceleration lane serves no exit, so a correct weave model leaves the flagship byte-identical: the discharge stays ~5,880 veh/h and the published rows do not move (§0.11). The transferable positive signature is the lane profile — the auxiliary lane must hold a small share of vehicle-time at high speed (recording: 6–8 % at 33–49 km/h), not 17–33 % at 4 km/h.
2. **MnDOT departed fractions** (`round2_gridlock_record.json`): the corridor mean rises from 0.4062 toward ≥ 0.9; T.H.52 from 0.41 and the downtown approach from 0.25 rise; Ruth St stays at 1.00; T.H.61 NB stays near 0.18 until the lane-addition case is built — a weave model that "fixes" T.H.61 NB is fixing the wrong geometry.
3. **Round-2 first-seed station speeds** (`round2_first_seed_station_speeds.json`, RMSPE 0.901, GEH pass 0.0): simulated means of 0.28–1.85 m/s at every station must be replaced by the observed *shape* — free flow at S1063–S1066 (31.83, 29.81, 30.51, 28.47 m/s), onset at S1067/S1068 (22.46, 14.16), minimum at S1069 (8.49), recovery at S97 (15.95). Round 1 (free flow everywhere, GEH < 5 on 17 % of 840 link-hours, RMSPE 93 %, mid-span throughput 1,805 [1,798, 1,812] veh/h against 2,900–3,000 observed) and round 2 (gridlock everywhere) bracket the two ways to be wrong.
4. **Pre-registered falsifiers:** departed fraction ≥ 0.9 with every station above 20 m/s = the round-1 failure recreated; `n_forced` dominating `n_changed_out` = a teleport, not gap acceptance; any `n_collisions > 0` = reject. The last-attempt result — merge waits 7.2 → 4.4 s with downstream speeds unchanged at 3.4, 1.0, 1.7 m/s (docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §6, session record, not committed) — is the precedent that a merge-side-only improvement proves nothing.

`packages/microsim/microsim/networks.py`, `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py`, `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py`, `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py`, `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_osm_ramps.py`; evidence in `docs/CONTRACTS.md` §2, `docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md` §6, `docs/I24_VALIDATION.md` §0.11, `artifacts/mndot_rounds/`, `artifacts/demand_mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.json`, `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.yaml`, `data/mndot/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul/stations_x.csv`.

Two findings for the next session: the weave pairs are already machine-detectable in the committed scenario (an on-ramp and an off-ramp sharing an `attach_edge`: `999007700`, `51388891`), and T.H.61 NB is a lane addition (two-lane entrance, five-lane station downstream), not a weave — its departed fraction is not a success criterion for this model.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3): the T.H.52 fixture, the mechanism, and a re-derivation that did not work

**Fixture and test.** `tests/fixtures/weave_th52.osm` is the T.H.52 geometry (docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §10) in the shape of `weave.osm`: ways 100–101 (3 lanes, 583 m compiled), 102 (4 lanes, 308 m compiled from 267 m of node spacing — netconvert adds the junction geometry), 103–104 (3 lanes, ~600 m), entrance 200 into lane 0 of 102, exit 201 from it. `TestWeaveRun::test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` runs mainline 4,500 veh/h with 25 % exiting and 1,400 veh/h entering for 20 min at 0.5 s, fleet defaults, seed 3 (~8 s wall), and asks for a flowing section: lane 1 over the first 60 m above 5 m/s in every 60-s window after 120 s, ≥ 90 % of the entrance's plan departed, ≤ 10 % of driven vehicles unfinished, no collision. It is `xfail(strict=True)`. At 462b731 lane 1 reads 20.2, 10.9, 2.9, 0.2 m/s in minutes 0–3 and 0.0 from minute 4; the entrance departs 81 of 466; 92 vehicles are driven, 25 change in, 18 out, 0 forced, 16,576 forced changes deferred, 49 unfinished; 3 of the 62 exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit; 583 of 1,966 planned vehicles depart; no collision. At two thirds of that demand (3,000 / 900 veh/h, 10 min) the same rules crawl instead of locking: 21.5, 18.6, 8.9, 4.3, 4.4, 2.3, 2.3, 1.9, 2.7, 4.0, 4.7 m/s.

**Mechanism, from a per-step trace on the fixture (seed 3; `_weave_step` wrapped, every `setMaxSpeed`/`changeLane`/`setLaneChangeMode` logged with `getNeighbors` gaps).** (1) The first standstill is in the *middle* through lane: at t = 55.5 s exit-bound v00016 in lane 2 at x = 228 m (just inside the last 80 m) receives `setMaxSpeed(0.0)` from the station-keeping rule — it holds `2 s0 + accept · v` behind whatever `getNeighbors` reports ahead in the lane to its right, which for a lane-2 vehicle is a lane-1 vehicle, with no floor — while its forced change is deferred because lane 1 beside it is a slow queue; through vehicle v00019 stops 0.2 m behind it and a vehicle sits at 0.0 m/s at x = 229–242 m from t = 60 to 84 s while lane 2 queues upstream. (2) Lanes 0 and 1 are coupled by speed matching in one direction only: an entering vehicle takes the speed of its lane-1 leader (`v_match`) along the whole auxiliary lane and an exiting vehicle in the zone the speed of (or a station behind) its lane-0 leader, so every disturbance ratchets both lanes down. (3) Lane 1's first stop in the section's first 60 m (t = 116 s) is exit-bound v00105 at x = 29 m braking at −8 m/s²: the exchange rule had dropped it to 4 m/s as the rear vehicle of an exchange, and entering v00337, matched to a 3.2 m/s lane-1 leader, executed its accepted change under mode 256 at 2.9 m/s directly ahead of it. Not the cause: the gap parameters (§10 probe), SUMO's cooperative model (driven vehicles run under modes 512/256 with every model-driven change off; the hard braking of through vehicles in the traces is IDM car-following behind inserted or stopped vehicles), the exit link (lane 0's queue head discharges freely).

**The re-derivation.** The weave as a two-sided zipper: (R1) an exiting vehicle drives with its own lane until the last `force_within_m`, an entering vehicle rolls down the auxiliary lane at the speed of lane 1's vehicle ahead and never below a floor; (R2) a committed changer positions itself against the target lane — first centred in the nearest gap (±2 m/s), then only clear of its target-lane leader by `2 s0 + accept · closing`; (R3) an exiting vehicle holds station behind a *driven entering* vehicle ahead in lane 0, the one place the floor is zero, so the pair never arrives abreast at the gore; (R4) every change is a one-step mode-256 execution through `_weave_force_gap_ok` — accepted gaps, or the guard alone once forcing is due — never a mode-512 request (SUMO answers one by adapting the requester's speed to a gap of its own choosing); (R5) a due forced change whose only obstacle is the target-lane follower asks that follower to open the gap. Eight forms were run on the fixture (session record; no code ships): R1+R4 alone locked all lanes by minute 3 (67 unfinished, 37,645 deferred) — the abreast-at-the-gore deadlock, entrant v00196 held by SUMO at the end of lane 0 (x = 308 m; its route continues on 103) and exiter v00019 creeping up lane 1 at the 3 m/s floor to x = 307.7 m beside it; adding R3 and matching along the lane locked by minute 3 with 0 forced changes because matching freezes the relative position (v00016 overlapped the same lane-0 vehicle by 2.5 m for 13 s); adding centring and R5 locked by minute 3 (25,542 deferred); making the floors relative (zero absolute) gave a 1–2 m/s crawl for ten minutes and a lock at minute 11 (69 + 43 forced, mean wait 104 s) — a ratchet, since each changer's reference is a changer further ahead; clear-of-leader positioning removed the lock but left lanes 0–2 at 0.3–2.5 m/s to the end (entrance 135 of 466) because entrants outside the zone were commanded 0.0 m/s beside a 1.5 m/s lane-1 queue with 20–60 m of free lane ahead; a creep floor outside the zone only gave a 1 m/s crawl throughout (entrance 207 of 466, 121 in / 175 out, waits 171 s / 120 s); rate-limiting every command (3 m/s²) and yield (2 m/s²) and adding a 0.5 s time gap to the forced guard — the shocks they remove are real (a through vehicle arriving at 30.9 m/s cut to a 6.5 m/s cap braked at −9 m/s² at t = 45.5 s; a 2 m follower gap is an emergency brake to IDM) — locked hard by minute 3 with 0 forced and 6,513 yield steps, a stopped changer and its stopped follower too close to open; letting entrants force by waiting time anywhere on the lane locked at minute 6. Applied singly to 462b731, station-keeping only behind driven entrants and the rate limits each turned the two-thirds-demand crawl into a lock (minutes 5 and 9), and together with the guard they failed `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` (12 unfinished). The runner is therefore byte-identical to 462b731 and the golden `merge_weave` unchanged.

**What the fixture establishes for the next attempt.** (a) Once changes happen only in the last `force_within_m`, lane 0 there carries entrants and exiters together — 2,525 veh/h against a lane's ≈ 2,050 veh/h at T = 1.4 s — so a jam formed at the gore never discharges; the changes must complete upstream, at speed. (b) An accepted change at the 0.6 s gap makes an IDM follower with T = 1.4 s brake at ≈ 4 m/s² (v00012, t = 40 s, −4.4 m/s² behind an entrant 24 m ahead); at 1,400 insertions/h lane 1 is compressed into platoons with no acceptable hole within a minute, which is what sends entrants to the gore. (c) A vehicle stopped at the gore cannot enter a 20 m/s stream under any guard. Together these say a saturated weave needs the through lane's *anticipatory* cooperation — a follower opening a gap at 1–2 m/s² several seconds before the changer is beside it — which neither LC2013 under the TraCI modes nor a max-speed cap provides (a cap below the current speed is an emergency brake to IDM's free term). The candidate is a virtual-leader car-following target for the cooperating follower (the entrant's projected position in the follower's own lane), with acceptance at the follower's own headway rather than a fixed 0.6 s; it is untested. Session artifacts (trace harness, per-attempt runs) were not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, second attempt): follower cooperation as a car-following target — no lock, a crawl

**What ships** (`microsim.runner._weave_step` and helpers `_idm_accel`, `_weave_veh`, `_weave_lane_map`, `_weave_choose_gap`, `_weave_command`, `_weave_cooperate`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2). The max-speed caps are gone: no speed matching, no station-keeping, no exchange rule, `courtesy` inert. Every driven vehicle keeps SUMO's own car-following under mode 512; every speed request is a one-step target `vehicle.slowDown(v, 0.0)` (duration 0 is the one-step form on SUMO 1.27.1 — a duration of one step lingers two steps, half-way then full; and under the default `speedMode` the target is clamped to the safe speed and to the vehicle's `decel`, so neither a collision nor a brake beyond b can be commanded). (1) *Gap choice*: a changer lists the target lane on the section axis (section edges, the through lanes of the corridor edge before and after, the on-ramp at negative positions) and picks, among the gaps it is in or abreast of within `lookahead_m` behind it, the nearest one whose follower F can open it at ≤ b_F (IDM towards the changer as virtual leader, F's own `tau/accel/decel/minGap/maxSpeed`), gaps it can also follow into first; the commitment is kept while F still opens it within b_F. Ranking by "least deceleration" alone was rejected on paper: the farthest follower always wins and the change is made at the gore. (2) *Cooperation*: F is driven at min(own IDM, a_F clipped at −b_F); several changers on one follower keep the lowest target. (3) *Easing*: the changer gets the same target towards its gap's leader when it would have to brake for it; only the rear one of an abreast pair has such a gap. (4) *Anticipation*: an entrant on the ramp within `lookahead_m` of the section chooses its gap, its follower cooperates and it eases before it appears on lane 0. (5) Both movements are forced in the last `force_within_m` after `force_after_s`, under the guard. (6) Acceptance = time gaps (`accept_gap_s`) + the immediate follower absorbing the changer within its b + the guard, executed under mode 256 for one step. New `meta.json` counters: `n_cooperations`, `mean_follower_decel_ms2`, `n_changer_eased`.

**Fixture numbers, seed 3, before → after.** Lane 1 over the first 60 m, minutes 2–19: 20.2, 10.9, 2.9, 0.2 then 0.0 → 12.7, 11.8, 12.4, 10.3, 5.8, 5.9, 4.9, 6.4, 6.3, 5.4, 6.2, 4.8, 5.5, 5.2, 5.4, 4.6, 4.2, 2.3 m/s. Entrance departed 81 → 225 of 466; all vehicles departed 583 → 1,386 of 1,966; driven 92 → 334 (in 25 → 122, out 18 → 210, forced 0 → 2, deferred 16,576 → 0, unfinished 49 → 2); exit-bound vehicles reaching the section that exit 3 of 62 → 273 of 280; collisions 0 → 0; 11,320 follower cooperations at a mean 0.13 m/s², 6,588 changer easings. `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` passes (lanes 0 and 1 at 19–21 m/s, waits 2.4 s, 0 unfinished); golden `merge_weave` regenerated (mean travel time 97.1 → 70.1 s, σ_v spatial 7.59 → 4.57 m/s, throughput 1,429 → 1,658 veh/h, waves 1 → 0, fuel −9.6 % (102.51 → 92.71 ml/veh-km), config hash unchanged). The strict `xfail` stays.

**What was tried and rejected on the fixture (session record, same seed).** Without the abreast tie-break, an exiter 4.5 m ahead of an entrant and the entrant each took the other as their gap's leader and both braked at b to a standstill (t = 55–70 s at x ≈ 85–90 m). Without ramp anticipation, a through vehicle at 28.7 m/s met an entrant appearing 7 m ahead at 19 m/s, overshot to 8 m behind, and the pair rode abreast to the gore where SUMO's lane-end braking took the entrant to 0 and the follower tracked it down (t = 30–45 s). Without easing anywhere (the task's literal rule), abreast pairs never resolve and every entrant reaches the gore, where the cooperating follower stops behind it and the guard refuses the forced change: 33,876 deferred, 56 unfinished, every lane at 0 from minute 3; a bumper-to-bumper guard did not change that. Easing off on the ramp only: 3–4 m/s throughout. `lookahead_m` 60: 4–5 m/s. An "exiter yields" tie-break instead of "rear yields": 3–4 m/s. No entrant forcing / bumper guard: byte-identical to the shipped form (neither binds).

**What stops it now — a crawl equilibrium at the section entry, not a lock.** Per-lane flows (veh/h at mean speed), window 120–360 s: x = −50 m lane 1: 705 @ 8.5 m/s while lanes 2, 3 carry 1,455 @ 16.8 and 1,440 @ 18.8; x = 20 m lane 0 (entrants): 570 @ 12.1, lane 1: 1,110 @ 11.4; x = 290 m: 1,140 / 735 / 1,065 / 1,380; x = 500 m (three lanes): 720 / 1,155 / 1,425 — a total discharge of 3,300 veh/h against 5,900 of demand. Window 600–1,200 s: x = −50 m: 522 @ 2.5, 960 @ 3.2, 1,152 @ 4.8; x = 20 m: 438 @ 4.4 / 804 @ 4.2 / 822 @ 4.2 / 1,050 @ 5.8; x = 150 m all lanes ≈ 13 m/s; x = 290 m ≈ 18 m/s; total ≈ 2,500 veh/h. The section itself accelerates from x ≈ 20 m on; the choke is at x = 0–20 m, where the lane-1 followers cooperating for entrants and the entrants easing for lane-1 leaders settle both lanes at the slowest element (each is an IDM target towards a vehicle in the other lane, bounded at b but with no floor), and exit-bound vehicles in lanes 2–3 then stop at the end of edge 101 for SUMO's strategic change into the crawling lane 1 (t = 400 s: 101 lane 1 at 0.2 m/s 13 m before the section, lane 2 at 0.7 m/s at 29 m), which spreads the queue to every lane. The combined stream at the entry (1,500 veh/h in lane 1 + 1,400 entrants) exceeds one IDM lane's ≈ 2,050 veh/h at T = 1.4 s, so a cooperation that zips them at the gore cannot carry it; the exchange that would (exiters vacate lane 1 upstream, entrants take the holes, net lane-1 flow ≈ 1,775 veh/h) needs entrants to spread along the 308 m and through vehicles to leave lane 1 — under a crawl SUMO's LC2013 sees no speed gain and nobody moves left (x = −50 m: 21 vehicles in lane 1, 6 and 5 in lanes 2 and 3 at t = 200 s). Candidates for a third attempt: a target-lane occupancy term in the gap choice (prefer the hole an exiter is leaving, defer the change while lane 1 is denser than lane 2), a floor on the cooperation target tied to the lane's flow, and a cheaper cooperation (F opens to the comfort boundary, not to full IDM spacing, then lets its own model finish after the change).

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, third derivation): through traffic vacates the weave lane upstream — lane 1 flows, the entrance does not

**The rule** (`microsim.runner._weave_vacate_step`, `_weave_vacate_lanes`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["vacate_ahead_m"]` = 150 m; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2). The second derivation's finding was structural: entering (1,400 veh/h) plus exiting (~1,100) exceed one IDM lane through the exchange, and the choreography that carries a real weave needs the *through* vehicles out of the weave lane before the section — which LC2013 does not do under a crawl. This attempt adds exactly that and nothing else: a through vehicle (not bound for the paired exit) on the lane of the corridor edge before the section that feeds section lane 1 (`_weave_lane_map`: on the T.H.52 fixture, lane 0 of edge 101), once within `vacate_ahead_m` of the section start, is asked **once** to move one lane left, `vehicle.changeLane(vid, lane_to, duration)` under mode 512 (`LC_MODE_SCRIPTED_SAFE`: every model-driven change off — no speed-gain, keep-right or cooperative change of SUMO's own — the request executed when SUMO's safety check on the target lane's leader and follower gaps passes, the vehicle adapting its speed to reach such a gap and the follower informed as for any urgent change). The request lives for the travel time to the section start at the speed when asked (floored at the 3 m/s creep), so SUMO may execute it anywhere in the remaining window. The original `laneChangeMode` is restored when the vehicle is seen in the target lane (`n_vacated`), or when the request has expired or the vehicle has reached the section still in the weave lane (`n_vacate_refused`); a request still open at the hand-back is ended with a one-step stay in the current lane — it is by lane *index*, and on the section's edge (one lane more, the ramp's) the same index is the weave lane, so a live request pulled vacated vehicles back in (found on the first grid; the numbers below are after the fix). A vehicle that cannot change stays and behaves as before. The window is truncated to the edge before the section (on the fixture 282 m, so 300 m and 500 m are the same run); `vacate_ahead_m = 0` is byte-identical to the second derivation. The cooperative-follower rules are untouched; a vacating vehicle may still be a chosen gap's follower.

**Why the default is 150 m and not a fitted value.** The HCM 7th ed. ch. 13 weaving segment's influence area extends 500 ft (152 m) upstream of the entry gore: that is the stretch over which the field says the weave shapes upstream driving, and the through driver's pre-positioning belongs to it (the advance guide sign is farther, ½ mile, but the change itself is made near the section). 200 m did better on this one seed (below); it has no source, and the response is not monotone, so the default is the standard's number.

**Why mode 512 and not 768 or the weave's own acceptance.** Mode 768 (the same safety check, no speed adaptation) executed 5 of 159 requests at 150 m and 13 of 177 at 300 m: a 1,470–1,530 veh/h target lane at 18–20 m/s rarely holds a gap that clears both secure gaps at once, and without adapting speed the vehicle never reaches one. The weave's own acceptance (0.6-s time gaps on both neighbours, the follower absorbing within its b, the guard, one-step mode 256) executed 8 of 159 (300 m) and 14 of 165 (150 m) with lane 1 at 4–6 m/s and the entrance at 198–214 — the through vehicle is the *rear* of most pairs on a dense lane 2. A short request (`change_duration_s` = 2 s, once on entering the window, mode 512) executed 30 of 218 at 150 m (windows pass, entrance 268) and 10 of 137 at 300 m with a **lock** from minute 11 (0.0 m/s, 44 unfinished, entrance 124) — rejected; these three were measured before the hand-back cancel and are session record.

The rule does what it was derived to do: lane 1 upstream of the section drops from 705 to 300–600 veh/h, the crawl at the section entry is gone in most minutes (10–13 m/s against 4–6), the exit-bound vehicles that reach the section still exit (268 of 272 at the default), no driven vehicle is left unfinished and nothing collides; the section's discharge at x = +290 m over 600–1,200 s is 3,666 veh/h against 3,150 (still far from the 5,900 of demand, 1,342 of 1,966 planned vehicles depart against 1,386 — the mainline queue reaches the insertion edge in both). The response to the window is not monotone on this seed (200 m passes every minute, 150 m and 300 m each dip below 5 m/s for one or two minutes, 100 m once) and the fleet's default keep-right eagerness (1.0; the I-94 fleet ran 0 until the 2026-09-24 regeneration of docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §11, which wrote the onboarding default 1.0 into `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.yaml`) works against it by refilling the lane; neither is a basis for tuning. The strict `xfail` stays on the entrance criterion: 240–322 of 466 at every window against 419 required.

**What stops it now — the ramp, not lane 1.** A per-step probe of the ramp (`_weave_step` wrapped; mode 512, 150 m, taken before the cancel fix) reads the entrants' mean speed by 20-m bin along the 210-m ramp, 120–360 s: 3.5, 2.8, 2.7, 2.7, 2.8, 4.5, 6.7, 8.5, 9.7, 10.6, 11.2 m/s — a 3 m/s queue over the first 100 m, accelerating from the point where the anticipation zone begins (`lookahead_m` = 120 m before the section start = ramp position 90 m) to 11 m/s at the gore; ramp travel time 42 s mean, none stopped at the gore; the ramp's link is a major link (`state="M"`, no yielding). The speed targets given to ramp vehicles over the run: 845 as *changers* (the easing rule — the entrant driven towards its chosen gap's leader in lane 1), 412 of them in the 80–100 m bin at a mean −1.5 m/s² (close to b), and 232 as *followers* opening a gap for an exiter on the section (the lane-0 listing places the ramp at negative positions). The head of the ramp queue is therefore the easing at the zone's entry: each entrant arriving at 11 m/s brakes for a lane-1 vehicle beside or just ahead of it, the queue behind it stands at 3 m/s, and insertion at the ramp's start is blocked (317 of 466 depart). Lane 1 at the entry is no longer the constraint (930–990 veh/h at 9–11 m/s at x = −50 m against 1,470 at the second derivation). That rule belongs to the second derivation and was out of this attempt's scope; the candidates for a fourth are an easing floor or a leader-distance threshold on the ramp (no easing towards a gap leader that is behind the entrant's own front, or farther than the gap the entrant needs), and deferring the exiters' choice of a ramp vehicle as follower until the entrant is on the section.

**Bookkeeping.** `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` and every other weave test pass; `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` keeps its strict marker with the new numbers in its reason and docstring. Golden `merge_weave` regenerated for the rule (`weave.osm`'s edge before the section has a lane feeding section lane 2, so the rule is live there): throughput 1,657.9 → 1,661.5 veh/h, mean travel time 70.09 → 69.51 s, p90 86.9 → 85.1 s, σ_v spatial 4.57 → 4.34 m/s, σ_v temporal 3.52 → 4.04 m/s, fuel 92.71 → 92.87 ml/veh-km, changes in/out/forced 20/13/2 → 18/17/1, waves 0 → 0, config hash 436cd4ec9e5d unchanged (the key is hash-neutral unless set). Session artifacts (measurement script, ramp probes, the per-variant runs) were not committed.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, fourth derivation): the entrant side — the ramp's anticipatory easing and cooperation are load-bearing; only the feasibility check ships

**What was set out to be derived.** The third derivation's ramp probe read the entrants' queue at 3 m/s over the ramp's first 100 m and attributed it to the second derivation's easing rule at the anticipation zone's entry (845 easing targets on ramp vehicles, 412 of them at the zone's entry bin) and to exit-bound vehicles taking ramp vehicles as cooperating followers (232). The fourth derivation therefore proposed three entrant-side rules: (1) a changer is eased towards its gap's leader only when (a) it is abreast of or ahead of the gap's follower and (b) dropping in behind the leader by the section end needs no more than its comfortable deceleration `b`, and never while it is still on the ramp upstream of the gore; (2) an exit-bound vehicle changing into lane 0 never asks a ramp vehicle upstream of the gore to slow; (3) measure. Each rule was implemented (session variants on `_weave_cooperate`, one switch per rule) and measured on `tests/fixtures/weave_th52.osm` at seed 3, the surviving candidates at seeds 4 and 5 as well. Rule (1a) is implied by the gap candidates of `_weave_choose_gap` (a follower's front is behind the changer's rear, so the changer is always ahead of it) and adds nothing; with consecutive-vehicle gaps a changer is in at most one gap, so "move to the next gap downstream" has no candidate to move to, and the alternative — keep the own car-following speed and wait for the follower — is what the rule reduces to.

**What ships** (`microsim.runner._weave_easing_ok`, called from `_weave_cooperate`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2): rule (1b) alone. With `t_a = remaining / max(v_c, creep)` the time to the section end at the changer's speed (for an entrant on the ramp, the ramp to the gore plus the section), the leader L holding its speed and a constant deceleration over `t_a`, the drop the changer needs is `d = (s0 + accept · v_c) − s_l` (its accepted gap behind L's rear; `≤ 0` when already clear) and the deceleration that achieves it `a_req = 2·(d + (v_c − v_l)·t_a)/t_a²`; the changer is eased (IDM towards L as before, clipped at `−b`) only when `a_req ≤ b`. Far from the section end the requirement is a few hundredths of a m/s² and the second derivation's easing is unchanged; in the last tens of metres, where only a brake beyond `b` or a stop beside L would still position the changer, it keeps its own speed and the change waits for the follower's cooperation or the forced mode. The easing on the ramp and the ramp follower's cooperation for an exiter stay as the second derivation had them. **The check binds rarely and its effect is within seed noise**: at seed 3 the eased vehicle-steps fall 2,512 → 2,120, the entrance departs 325 instead of 317 and every minute is above 5 m/s (was one below); at seed 4 two minutes fall below 5 m/s (4.9, 4.0; was none) and the entrance reads 307 against 311; at seed 5 the run is unchanged to the counter (eased 1,872 → 1,868, entrance 295). The `merge_weave` golden passes unchanged (the check never binds on `weave.osm`). It is kept because it states a bound the easing should always have had — no positioning brake that cannot complete — and because the "ease only when needed" variant below, the one lead that moved the entrance criterion, is one condition on top of it.

**What was measured and rejected.** All runs 20 min at 0.5 s, fleet defaults, the third derivation's `vacate_ahead_m` = 150 m; lane 1 over the section's first 60 m per minute 2–19 (count of minutes at or below 5 m/s in brackets); entrance departed of 466 planned and all vehicles of 1,966; driven vehicles unfinished; forced changes; collisions; through vehicles vacated / refused; entrants' mean speed by 20-m bin over the ramp's first 100 m, 120–360 s; one-step speed targets given to vehicles on the ramp over the run, as changer / as follower (this probe counts the target a vehicle finally receives on a step, so the third derivation's 845 / 232 — an earlier probe, before the hand-back cancel — read 1,419 / 255 here); per-lane flows at x = −50 m (section lanes 1 / 2 / 3, veh/h @ mean m/s at the first sample past the cross-section), 600–1,200 s. The "ease only when needed" variants require `0 < a_req ≤ b` (no easing for a leader that opens the gap by itself within the horizon); the "minimal constant-deceleration" variants command `−a_req` instead of IDM towards L.

**What the table says.** (a) *The easing on the ramp is not the head of the ramp queue; it is the entrant's positioning.* Without it (rows "no easing on the ramp") the entrant arrives at the gore abreast of its gap's leader and does at −b on lane 0's first metres what it used to do on the ramp, its cooperating lane-1 follower tracks it down, and both lanes settle at 3–5 m/s in every minute with the whole ramp at 1.7–2.6 m/s — the second derivation's crawl equilibrium at the section entry, stronger. The third derivation's reading of its ramp probe was wrong on this point: the entrants at the zone's entry are braked for lane-1 vehicles passing them, but taking that brake away moves the queue to the gore rather than releasing it. (b) *The ramp vehicle's cooperation for an exiter is what resolves the crossing pair before the gore.* Without it (rows "no ramp follower") seed 3 flows in every minute and seed 5 nearly so, but seed 4 locks from minute 13 (16 unfinished, entrance 204): the exiter commits to a gap whose ramp follower is not asked to open it, the entrant arrives beside it at speed, and the pair — each wanting the other's lane, the entrant now the exiter's cooperating follower and the exiter the entrant's leader — stops. (c) *Easing only when the drop is needed* (no brake for a leader that opens the gap by itself within the horizon) is the one lead that moves the entrance criterion — 400 and 394 of 466 at seeds 3 and 5 (86 %, against 419 required), 1,553 of 1,966 vehicles departing against 1,352, lane 1 above 5 m/s in every minute, 21 forced changes — but it locks at seed 4 from minute 12 (20 unfinished): more entrants reach the section faster and the section, denser, reaches the same stopped-pair state; it was measured with the ramp follower off, and the combination with the follower on is untested. (d) A follower that opens only to a 1.0-s headway instead of its own T gains nothing (396, 48 forced) and one that opens to 0.6 s locks by minute 5 at seed 3 (an IDM follower absorbing a 0.6-s gap brakes at ≈ 4 m/s², the plan's earlier finding (b)); commanding the minimal constant deceleration instead of IDM towards L locks by minute 4 at every horizon (a receding-horizon target that never completes the drop: at 60–200 m the changer rides beside its leader to the gore). (e) The feasibility check alone never locks at three seeds and changes nothing a seed does not.

**Candidates for a fifth derivation.** The "ease only when needed" condition together with a resolution of the stopped-pair state it reaches at seed 4 — the pair of an exiter and an entrant abreast, each the other's leader, which the second derivation's rear-yields tie-break does not cover once both are stopped (a stopped changer's `a_req` is small and positive at any distance, so the check never releases it): a release rule (the changer stopped beside a stopped leader for longer than `force_after_s` gives up the gap and takes the next) or the forced mode extended to that state. Before that, the same variants at seeds 4 and 5 with the ramp follower on. The runner's `n_forced` rising from 1 to 21 under the "needed" rule is the entrants arriving unpositioned at the section end; it is a symptom, not the cure. Session artifacts (the probe with its variant switches, the per-variant JSONs) were not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, fifth derivation): easing only when needed, with the ramp follower on, and a stopped changer–follower pair released

**What ships** (`microsim.runner._weave_easing_ok`, `_weave_pair_release`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["pair_release_s"]` = 2.0 s; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2). (1) The fourth derivation's lead: a changer is eased towards its gap's leader only when the drop is *needed* as well as feasible — `0 < a_req ≤ b` — so a leader that opens the gap by itself within the horizon (faster than the changer, or already clear by more than the accepted gap) gets no brake from the changer; the ramp follower's cooperation for an exiter stays on (the fourth derivation showed removing it locks seed 4). (2) A stopped changer–follower pair is released: a driven changer X and the follower F of its committed gap (`veh[X]["target"]`, any vehicle) whose front is within one vehicle length — the longer of the two — behind X's rear on the section axis, both below the creep speed (3 m/s) for more than `pair_release_s` (`t − since > pair_release_s`, `since` the first step the pair stood), are released: the one farther from the section end (more section ahead of its front; ties, impossible since F is behind X, by the vehicle id string with the greater one yielding) yields for the step — it is given no speed target in either role, makes no request if it is itself driven, and its own gap commitment is dropped and re-chosen next step — while the other executes its change under the normal acceptance, or under the forced mode at once when within `force_within_m`, guarded against closing only (`_weave_force_gap_ok` with the `s0` floor dropped: both are below the creep speed, the floor is a comfort margin at speed, and mode 256 still refuses an overlap). The default 2 s is two human reaction times of ≈ 1 s (Treiber & Kesting 2013, ch. 12, the human driver model's reaction time; the IDM has none): a pair standing longer than the time in which each could have reacted to the other once is not resolving by itself; not a fitted value. New `meta.json` counter `n_pair_releases` (each pair once per release; a pair that breaks up and stands again counts again), also in the API schema and the sweep summary.

**The lock, from the per-step trace (seed 4, "needed" alone, `_weave_step` wrapped, every `changeLane` / `slowDown` and `getNeighbors` logged for the pair).** Exit-bound v00875 enters the last 80 m in lane 1 at t = 888 s with its committed gap's follower v01769 in lane 0 1.3–1.5 m behind its rear; both decelerate together to the lane end (3.5 m/s at 896 s, 0 at 902.5 s, v00875 at 308.4 m of 308 — held by SUMO because lane 1 does not continue on its route), v01769 held 0.0 m behind it (SUMO's neighbour gap; 1.8 m by lane position) by v00875's own cooperation command every step (IDM towards a virtual leader at a zero gap, clipped at −b). v01769 is **not** an entrant: it is an exit-bound vehicle that changed into lane 0 earlier, was handed back (mode 1621) and is simply the nearest vehicle behind v00875 in the target lane. The gap cannot open — F cannot back up, X cannot move on — and the forced change is refused for ever (the guard's `s0` floor, and SUMO reporting the pair overlapping). The task's release rule was written for an *entrant–exiter* pair (the fourth derivation's description of the no-ramp-follower lock); implemented literally it fires on other pairs (6 at seed 4, 11 at seed 5) and leaves the lock untouched, with the guard's floor kept or dropped alike (identical runs). The pair is therefore defined by the commitment, not the movement: X and the follower of its committed gap. Under that form the follower, farther from the section end, yields, moves on under its own car-following, and the changer at the lane end drops in behind it and exits. The entrant–exiter pair the fourth derivation described is the same state with F a driven entrant and is covered.

**Measurements (fixture `weave_th52.osm`, 20 min, fleet defaults, the third derivation's `vacate_ahead_m` = 150 m; the fourth derivation's probe, recomputed for every row this session).** Lane 1 over the section's first 60 m per minute 2–19 (count of minutes at or below 5 m/s); entrance departed of 466 and all vehicles of 1,966; driven vehicles unfinished; forced changes; collisions; through vehicles vacated / refused; entrants' mean speed by 20-m bin over the ramp's first 100 m, 120–360 s; one-step targets given to ramp vehicles, as changer / as follower; per-lane flows at x = −50 m over 600–1,200 s (**this session's definition**: vehicles counted by the lane of their first trajectory sample past x = −50 m within the window, veh/h @ mean speed of those samples — the fourth derivation's column read 1,020 @ 9.6 for lane 1 of the same run where this reads 432 @ 9.1, so the column is comparable within this table only); pairs released.

**What the table says.** (a) Rule (1) with the ramp follower on does at seed 3 what it did without it (395 against the fourth derivation's 400) and locks seeds 4 and 5 later than without it (minute 16 against 12–13) — the follower delays the pair state, it does not remove it; the seed-5 lock also collides ten times in the jam (four rear-end pairs in lane 2 at x ≈ 123–129 m, re-reported while overlapping), which the release removes with the jam. (b) The released pair is not the entrant–exiter pair of the fourth derivation's description; a release of that pair alone is inert. (c) With (2), no seed locks, no driven vehicle of a jam is left unfinished (2–6 of 452–501), no collision, and the entrance departs 389–395 of 466 (84–85 %) at every seed against 295–325 before — still short of the criterion's 419. The section's cost is in `n_forced` (20–40 against 0–5): the changers that the "needed" condition leaves unpositioned force their change at the section end, and the released changers force at once. (d) The ramp's first 100 m still queue at 4–5 m/s (was 3–4): the entrance criterion is now an insertion-rate question at the ramp's start, not a lock or a crawl of lane 1, and the remaining candidates are on the ramp side — the follower's cooperation for an exiter reaching down the ramp (the "targets on ramp, follower" column: 379–645 targets) and the anticipation zone's entry speed.

**Bookkeeping.** `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` keeps its strict marker with the new numbers; `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_does_not_lock` pins seeds 4 and 5 (every minute above 2 m/s, ≤ 10 % of driven vehicles unfinished, no collision, entrance ≥ 80 %, at least one release); `TestWeavePairRelease` covers the release in the fake harness (the timer, the standing-time parameter, a moving or separated pair, a driven yielder); two `TestWeaveEasingFeasibility` cases flipped for the "needed" condition. `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` and every other weave test pass. Golden `merge_weave` regenerated for the two rules (both bind on `weave.osm`): throughput 1,661.5 → 1,687.5 veh/h, mean travel time 69.51 → 69.82 s, p90 85.1 → 85.4 s, σ_v spatial 4.34 → 4.28 m/s, σ_v temporal 4.04 → 3.94 m/s, VMT 169.64 → 169.62 veh-km, VHT 1.779 → 1.767 veh-h, fuel 92.87 → 93.08 ml/veh-km, changes in/out/forced 18/17/1 → 17/20/1, exits 33, collisions 0, config hash 436cd4ec9e5d unchanged (the key is hash-neutral unless set). ## 2026-09-24 (block 3, sixth derivation): the bound is the ramp's own queue, its head the easing for a leader beside the entrant

**The trace** (fixture `weave_th52.osm`, seeds 3 and 4, fifth derivation a6c0296; `_weave_step`, `_weave_command` and `_weave_choose_gap` wrapped, every entrant followed from its planned departure to its change; session harness, not committed). (a) *Insertion.* The entrance's backlog grows from minute 3 to 71 / 68 vehicles at the end (the 466 − 395 / 392 never inserted); the depart delay grows linearly to 187 / 180 s; the ramp inserts ≈ 20 per minute (1,185 veh/h) against 23.3 planned. SUMO refuses the `departPos="base" departSpeed="avg"` insertions while the ramp's first 20 m hold a vehicle at 5 m/s (no `--max-depart-delay`, so nothing is discarded: the backlog is the whole shortfall). (b) *The ramp.* 210 m travelled in 36–39 s mean (free flow 10.5 s); mean speed by 20-m bin over 120–360 s 5.1, 4.5, 4.2, 4.0, 4.0, 4.7, 5.5, 6.0, 6.3, 6.4, 6.1 m/s at 51, 72, 76, 77, 76, 64, 55, 51, 49, 50, 25 veh/km — a congested IDM equilibrium (the equilibrium gap at 4.3 m/s is 8 m; the static flow 1,189 veh/h) over 20–100 m, then a platoon stretching at 0.05–0.26 m/s² (not the free 0.6–0.7) to the gore; headways crossing 110 m and 200 m 3.1 s at both = 1,150–1,160 veh/h. No command touches a ramp vehicle before position 80 m (the anticipation zone starts at 90 m: `lookahead_m` 120 m before the section start). (c) *The gore.* Speed at the gore 8.5 / 7.6 m/s mean (p10 5.2 / 4.4); 0.0 s stopped on the auxiliary lane; the change a median 5–6 s after the gore at a median 47–52 m (p90 305–307 m: 7 entrants reach the last 80 m, none is forced); acceptance passes on 22 / 18 % of auxiliary-lane steps, the failing condition the leader time gap on 38 %, the follower's on 32 %, the follower's comfort on 2 %, the guard never; lane 1 within ±100 m of the gore carries a median 2.4–2.6-s time gap at 8–9 m/s, 59 % of its gaps long enough for both accepted gaps. So neither the acceptance, nor the forced mode, nor a queue at the gore bounds the entrance: the ramp does, and its flow is the ramp queue's, 1,150 veh/h. (d) *The head of the queue.* A pure-IDM platoon with the fleet defaults (T 1.4 s, a 0.73 m/s², b 1.67 m/s², s0 2 m) discharges from standstill at 2.32-s headways (1,553 veh/h) and from a 4.3 m/s queue at 2.21 s (1,630 veh/h) — above the demand — so the 3.1 s is imposed. The one-step targets given to ramp vehicles over the run: as changer (the easing towards the gap leader) 1,322 / 1,276, as follower (for an exiter) 390 / 677; 251 / 257 of 395 / 392 entrants receive at least one, and those lose 4.85 / 4.80 m/s of speed to them on average (p90 10.5 / 12.1). The easing's geometry on the ramp: the gap leader's rear within 5 m of the entrant's front on 63 / 70 % of the eased steps and *behind* it (overlapping) on 34 / 38 %, the entrant faster by a median 0.27 / 0.15 m/s (p90 1.9 / 1.2), the raw IDM term against that leader a median −19 / −41 m/s² clipped to −b (mean command −1.34 / −1.38 m/s², −1.68 when overlapping) while the deceleration the positioning needs (`a_req`, horizon ramp + section ≈ 50 s) is hundredths of a m/s². Each event costs ≈ 2.5 m/s and 8 s of recovery at the IDM's 0.2–0.4 m/s², the platoon behind copies it, and the platoon's head is held at 5–8 m/s: a vehicle beside the entrant is not a leader the car-following model can follow (its gap term is undefined at `s ≤ 0`), and the "needed" condition of the fifth derivation is met by any leader a hair slower.

**What ships** (`microsim.runner._weave_cooperate`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2): an entrant still on the ramp (`road in ramp_edges`) is **not** eased towards a gap leader that overlaps it (`s_l < 0`, the leader's rear behind the entrant's front); it keeps its own car-following speed, the follower's cooperation and the commitment are unchanged, and it drops in behind the leader — or passes it — on the section. Everything on the section is as the fifth derivation had it.

**What was measured and rejected (seed 3 unless stated; session variants by monkeypatch, then the shipped condition in the runner).** Applied on the ramp *and* the section, every softening of the −b easing locks the section by minute 4–5 (lane 1 at 0.0 m/s to the end, 33–59 driven vehicles unfinished): no easing towards an overlapping leader (entrance 151), easing only when the entrant cannot pass the leader within the horizon — `(v_c − v_l)·t_a < len + s_need` — (163), both (128), the minimal constant deceleration over a 1-s horizon (143). The −b resolution of an abreast pair on the section is load-bearing (second derivation). On the ramp alone none locks: the overlap rule 414 / 412 / 407 / 415 of 466 at seeds 3–6 (harness form, `remaining > section length`), the pass rule 419 with four minutes of lane 1 at or below 5 m/s, a 1-s / 10-s constant-deceleration horizon 404 / 399, a relative-speed positioning (at most 1 m/s below the leader) 388, both rules together 397 with six minutes at or below 5. The boundary of "beside": the leader's rear less than 2 m ahead of the entrant's front 422 (every minute above 5 — the one form that met the whole criterion, at this seed), less than 5 m 430 with two minutes at or below 5, less than the accepted gap 401 with five; in the runner with each vehicle's drawn `s0` (1.7–2.3 m) as the boundary the same rule read 395 / 401 / 423 at seeds 3–5 with 3 / 6 / 0 minutes at or below 5 — the response is chaotic at the few-metre level, and the overlap boundary (`s_l < 0`) is the physically unambiguous one and the most consistent across seeds, so it is what ships.

**Measurements (fixture `weave_th52.osm`, 20 min, fleet defaults; the fifth derivation's probe recomputed for every row).** Lane 1 over the section's first 60 m per minute 2–19 (count at or below 5 m/s); entrance departed of 466 and all vehicles of 1,966; driven vehicles unfinished; forced changes; collisions; through vehicles vacated / refused; entrants' mean speed by 20-m bin over the ramp's first 100 m, 120–360 s; one-step targets given to ramp vehicles, as changer / as follower; per-lane flows at x = −50 m over 600–1,200 s (vehicles by the lane of their first trajectory sample within 40 m past the cross-section, veh/h @ mean speed of those samples; comparable within this table only); pairs released; the ramp's headway crossing 110 m after 120 s.

The speed the commands take from a commanded entrant on the ramp falls from 4.85 to 3.5 m/s; what remains are the eased steps with the leader just clear of the entrant (0–5 m), which the `s0`-boundary variant removed at the cost of seed noise. (b) The cost moves to lane 1 at the section start: the abreast pair that used to resolve on the ramp (the entrant at −b) now resolves on the section (the rear one at −b, or the lane-1 follower absorbing), and lane 1 over the first 60 m falls to 4.4–5.0 m/s in three minutes at seed 3, 4.6 / 4.3 in one at seeds 4 / 5, 3.7–4.9 in four at seed 6; pairs released 14 / 0 / 16 / 18 (was 0 / 9 / 1); nothing locks, no collision, 2–6 driven vehicles unfinished. (c) The entrance criterion (419) is met at seed 5 (420) and not at seed 3 (411); the lane-1 criterion (every minute above 5 m/s) is not met at any seed. The strict `xfail` stays. (d) The remaining bound is the same mechanism one step later: an entrant and a lane-1 vehicle at speed parity, one of which must give up ≈ 2.5 m/s at −b for the other to be positioned, on a ramp whose discharge at 5–6 m/s (2.95 s) is still below 1,400 veh/h. A seventh derivation would address the resolution itself — a gentle mutual adjustment (the entrant eases at the deceleration the drop needs, the lane-1 vehicle holds or opens at a fraction of b) — rather than which side pays; the fixed-horizon constant-deceleration form measured here (a receding horizon that completes the drop only asymptotically) is not it.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, seventh derivation): the abreast pair resolved symmetrically — measured and rejected

**The rule as derived.** The sixth derivation left the entrance bounded by an entrant C in section lane 0 and a lane-1 vehicle P side by side at speed parity, resolved by one of them braking at −b while the other holds (C eased towards P as its gap's leader when P is the front one; P absorbing as the follower of C's gap once C's rear clears it), lane 1 paying through the follower tracking C. The seventh derivation resolves the pair symmetrically: a *pair* is C and the nearest lane-1 vehicle whose front is within one vehicle length (the longer of the two) of C's and whose speed is within 1 m/s of C's, with the offset still needed `need > 0` — `len_P + s0_C − (x_P − x_C)` with P ahead (an exact tie by the id string, as `_weave_choose_gap`), `len_C + s0_P − (x_C − x_P)` with C ahead. Over a horizon τ (`weave_params["pair_tau_s"]`, default 3 s = twice the IDM's desired headway T = 1.4 s, the time in which two drivers side by side each re-establish one headway between them) each side adjusts by half: the rear one towards `v̄ − need/(2τ)` and the front one towards `v̄ + need/(2τ)`, `v̄` the pair's mean speed, each approached at no more than b/2 per step and never below the creep speed (3 m/s); the −b easing towards the partner is not asked for while the episode lasts, the gap commitment is kept, the episode ends at `need ≤ 0` (the rear one's front one length plus its minGap behind the front one's) or when the partner leaves the lane, and the normal acceptance then executes the change. Each pair counted once per episode (`n_pair_adjustments`, session only).

**What the runner can command (measured before the rule was written).** The task assumed the front one's adjustment could be a `slowDown` towards a higher speed. On SUMO 1.27.1 with the default `speedMode` it cannot: the influencer clamps a `slowDown` / `setSpeed` target at the vehicle's safe speed, and for the IDM that is the model's own next speed — an IDM vehicle near its desired speed (v0 = 12 m/s, v ≈ 11, own acceleration 0.104 m/s²) asked for v + 0.42 m/s each step accelerated at 0.095, 0.086, 0.078 m/s² (the model's own decay), `setSpeed` the same; only `speedMode` 30 (the safety check off, which CLAUDE.md §3.3 forbids) gave 0.73 m/s². The one-step targets of `_weave_command` are therefore ceilings — the runner can ask a vehicle to hold or drop back, never to open ahead by a speed target — and its docstring, which had said a higher target "would make the vehicle accelerate faster than its own model", is corrected. The front one of the pair was let open ahead by its **own** model instead: its `tau` scaled to half for the episode (`setTau`, restored at the end; a driver sorting out a side-by-side merge accepting a shorter headway to their leader for those seconds), its acceleration then the IDM's own, bounded by `accel` (0.73 ± 15 % m/s², about b/2) and by the pair's target as a ceiling, SUMO's safety check on for both.

**Two forms and their variants (session harness, monkeypatched in the worker; the implementation was in the runner and is reverted).** *Receding*: the relative-speed target re-read from the current offset each step, `need(t)/τ` — the offset then decays exponentially and `need ≤ 0` is reached only asymptotically (the form the sixth derivation had already named as "not it"; measured first, by mistake). *Fixed*: the relative speed `need₀/τ` fixed at entry so the pair separates in τ. Variants of each: no headway concession (`tau` untouched); pairs with the entrant behind only; with the entrant ahead only; the −b easing kept beside the pair's commands (the rear one's ceiling is above the easing and loses); horizons 1.5, 2, 2.5 and 6 s. Columns as the sixth derivation's table: lane 1 over the section's first 60 m per minute 2–19 (count at or below 5.0 m/s at one decimal — the criterion is > 5, so 5.0 fails; the sixth derivation's paragraph counted the same seed-3 row as 3, strictly below); entrance departed of 466 and all vehicles of 1,966; driven vehicles unfinished; forced changes; collisions; through vehicles vacated / refused; per-lane flows at x = −50 m over 600–1,200 s (vehicles by the lane of their first trajectory sample within 40 m past the cross-section, veh/h @ mean speed; lanes 0 / 1 / 2 of the upstream edge); pairs released; pairs adjusted.

**What the table says.** (a) The receding form is what the sixth derivation predicted: the pairs never close (the exit condition is asymptotic), the entrants ride beside their partners to the last 80 m and force (42–43 forced against 22–24), lane 1 dips in 5–11 minutes, the entrance loses 36–67 vehicles at every seed; every decomposition of it is worse still, one locks seed 4 and two collide. (b) The fixed form is a real rule — at seed 3 the section flows better than under the sixth derivation on every lane-1 measure (1 minute at or below 5 against 4; 6 forced against 22) — but it costs the entrance (392 against 411) and, decisive, it is less robust: at seed 7 it locks the section from minute 12 (217 of 466, 38 driven vehicles unfinished) where the sixth derivation departs 424 with every minute above 5 m/s; its entrant-behind-only variant locks seed 5. Over seeds 3–8 the entrance means are 411.8 (sixth, no lock at any seed) against 361.7. (c) The horizon: 1.5 / 2 / 2.5 / 3 s read 388 / 421 / 401 / 392 at seed 3 and 386 / 386 / 365 / 368 at seed 4 — the few-metre chaos the sixth derivation recorded, not a trend. τ = 2 s meets the whole criterion at seed 3 (421 ≥ 419, no minute at or below 5, 2 of 477 unfinished, no collision) and would keep the no-lock test's bounds at seeds 4 and 5 (386 ≥ 373), but it departs 362 / 408 at seeds 6 / 7 and locks seed 8 with a collision (mean 352 over seeds 3–8): removing the marker on it would be choosing the horizon by one seed. (d) The headway concession is not what hurts (the fixed form without it reads 409 / 390 / 384) and not what helps (the concession alone, with the −b easing kept, reads 380 / 348 / 399 with a collision at seed 5): the front one of a pair in a queue has no room to open — its acceleration is its leader's gap, not its headway — so the symmetric split degenerates into the rear one braking at b/2 instead of b, which is the softened easing the sixth derivation had already measured as locking. (e) What survives: the fact about speed targets (they are ceilings, `_weave_command`'s docstring corrected) and the observation that the sixth derivation's response over seeds 3–8 is 397–424 with 0–4 minutes at or below 5 m/s and no lock, meeting the whole criterion at seed 7 — seed noise around a rule that is 5–25 entrants short, not a pass.

**Bookkeeping.** The runner is unchanged: the rule, `weave_params["pair_tau_s"]` and the `n_pair_adjustments` counter were implemented, measured and reverted, so neither the API schema nor the sweep summary gains a field (a counter no run writes would be a claim). `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` keeps its strict marker with the sixth derivation's numbers and `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_does_not_lock` its pins; their docstrings record this derivation. Golden `merge_weave` unchanged (nothing in the runner's behaviour changed; the golden test was re-run). Session artifacts (the probe, `probe_th52.py`, with its variant switches by label and the 60 runs under it, and the two `slowDown` probes) were not committed.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, exit-side derivation): the corridor locks at the gore's end, not the entrance — exit priority and the give-up

**The corridor's evidence (a 35-min slice of `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul_weave_slice.yaml` on a cloud VM with the sixth-derivation runner, one seed, session record).** Departed 0.848 (the second derivation gave 0.892 on the same inputs), one collision, and the first standstill at minute 11 at x = 10.60–10.70 km in lanes 0 and 1 — the **end** of the T.H.52 section (entrance 769818012 on edge 51388891, the exit 18207598 at 10.73 km), not its start. The section's counters: entered 114, exited 88, reached-section-exiting 133, forced 1, forced deferred 8,971 vehicle-steps, unfinished 21, vacated 131 / refused 24, pair releases 88. So the exit movement (lane 1 → the auxiliary lane) stalls at the gore's end while derivations three to seven optimised the entrance.

**The corridor's flows at that weave.** From the scenario's series at its 600–900 s step (the peak; the standstill forms in that window): the upstream `inflow` 1.0956 veh/s = 3,944 veh/h propagated through the fourteen upstream ramps' `exit_fraction` and `inflow` series in corridor order arrives at edge 51388891 as 1.3663 veh/s = **4,919 veh/h**; off-ramp 18207598's `exit_fraction` is **0.2122** (1,044 veh/h exiting); on-ramp 769818012's `inflow` is 0.3922 veh/s = **1,412 veh/h**; the section edge has four lanes (three through + the auxiliary) over 305 m, as the fixture. The other steps read 4,318–4,919 veh/h arriving, 0.168–0.212 exiting, 1,268–1,412 entering. Against the fixture's 4,500 / 25 % / 1,400 the corridor carries more through traffic (3,876 against 3,375 veh/h over three lanes) and a slightly smaller exit stream. `TH52_CORRIDOR_DEMAND` in `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` carries the peak's numbers; `TestWeaveRun::test_th52_weave_at_corridor_demand_exit_side` runs them for 20 min at seed 3 and judges the exit side: ≥ 90 % of the exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit (`n_exited / n_reached_section_exiting`), lane 1 over the section's **last** 60 m above 5 m/s in every 60-s window after 120 s, ≤ 10 % of driven vehicles unfinished, no collision.

**At 3f47446 (the sixth derivation's runner) the fixture reproduces the corridor's state transiently.** Seed 3: lane 1's last 60 m read 15.0, 16.3, **4.0, 3.7**, 11.5 m/s in minutes 2–6 and 11–19 m/s after; 96 trajectory samples of lane 1 stopped within the last 20 m in minute 4 (14 in minute 5) and 7 + 8 of lane 0 beside them; 303 of 307 exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit; 436 driven (223 in, 212 out, 17 forced, 125 deferred, 1 unfinished), 4 pairs released, no collision; the entrance departs 418 of 470. Seeds 4 and 5 never stall (last 60 m ≥ 12.9 / 11.8 m/s, 308 of 313 / 312 of 320 exit). So the corridor's flows alone do not lock the fixture — the corridor adds the heavy population, the arrival lane distribution and 35 minutes of growing demand — but the state the corridor stays in, the fixture passes through, and the trace of that passage is the mechanism.

**The trace (seed 3, `_weave_step` wrapped; every exit-bound driven vehicle inside the last 80 m logged with its `getNeighbors` gaps, the acceptance and the guard; session harness, not committed).** Exiter v00251 enters the zone in lane 3 at t = 263.5 s with 76.7 m left at 12.3 m/s — three changes to make in 80 m — reaches lane 1 with 7 m left at 3.8 m/s (271.5 s) and stops at the gore's end (x = 308.4 of 308.4, 0.0 m/s from 279.5 s to 301 s; SUMO holds it there because lane 1 does not continue on its route); v01707 and v01708 stop behind it in lane 1 for 25 s and the queue reaches 300 m upstream by t = 300 s. Over those 22 s: (a) its front is ahead of every auxiliary-lane front, so the abreast rule of `_weave_choose_gap` ("a gap whose leader's front is behind the changer's is not one the changer is in") discards **every** gap, the only survivor being the open road behind the frontmost lane-0 vehicle, whose follower is beside the exiter (`s_f < 0`) — `target` is `None` on most steps and nobody is held; (b) lane 0 streams past at 5–11 m/s (v01709, v00192, v01710, v01712, v00130, v00201 — exit-bound vehicles already in lane 0, and entrants), each overlapping the exiter in turn, and the due forced change is refused by `_weave_force_gap_ok` on the leader side (an overlap, 41 steps), the follower side (an overlap or a follower closing within 0.6 s, 42) or both (26): 67 deferred steps for this vehicle; (c) a follower finally chosen (v00130 at 291.5 s, 1.4 m behind at 1.1 m/s; then v00201) is driven towards the exiter's rear as its virtual leader and comes to rest at ≈ its own `minGap` behind it — a gap the guard's `s0` floor (`g_foll > s0`) never passes — until the fifth derivation's release lets it go after 2 s, whereupon it moves on beside the exiter (a leader-side overlap again) and the next one is held: lane 0 crawls at 0.7–1.4 m/s through the cycle (v00130, v00201, v00204, v00207, v01714, v00290) and the exiter gets in at 301.5 s when a gap happens to clear on both sides. (d) No exiter ever continues on the mainline (`n_missed` 0): a vehicle stopped at the lane end stays there — on the corridor these are the 21 unfinished and the 8,971 deferred steps, and the 88 releases are the cycle of (c). What was not the cause: the acceptance gaps (the exiter is stopped, `s0 + 0.6·0 = s0`), the vacate rule (176 vacated, 89 refused, as at the fixture's demand), the entrance (418 of 470 departed).

**The rules (ship; `microsim.runner._weave_choose_gap`, `_weave_step`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2).** (1) *Exit priority.* An exit-bound changer whose forced change is due (`force_after_s` after entering the last `force_within_m`) keeps as a candidate the gap behind a target-lane vehicle *beside* it — L's front behind the changer's front but ahead of its rear — with `a_c = inf` (it does not ease towards a vehicle it is ahead of; it waits for it to clear); its follower F holds whatever F's IDM says (the commitment is kept while F is behind the changer's rear — F brakes at no more than its `b` either way), and F is driven towards a virtual leader one changer `minGap` behind the changer's rear (`s_F − s0_c`), so it comes to rest `s0_F + s0_c` behind, a gap the guard accepts. The entrants and lane-0 exiters behind the gore thus hold while the exiter drops in; the pair-release asymmetry (the one farther from the end yields) already favours the exiter. (2) *Give-up at the gore's end.* An exit-bound vehicle halted (below `HALTING_SPEED_MS` = 0.1 m/s, SUMO's own halting threshold) still owing its change with no more than `exit_giveup_m` = 5 m (one vehicle length; a new `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` key, hash-neutral unless set) of section ahead, and with no change to request that step (review, 2026-09-24 block 3: the acceptance and the forced guard are read before the give-up; with the give-up first, a halted exiter whose follower had come to rest at the priority's gap was rerouted on the very step both gaps cleared), is rerouted through — `vehicle.changeTarget` to the corridor's last edge — handed back at once and counted in `n_missed` and the new `n_missed_exit`; it is never held by SUMO at the end of a lane its route does not continue on. The `n_missed_exit` counter is in `meta.json`, `WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut` (null for an older meta) and the sweep summary's `WEAVE_FIELDS`.

**Measurements (fixture `weave_th52.osm`, 20 min, fleet defaults; the probe of the sixth derivation with the exit side added).** Entrance departed of the plan; exit-bound vehicles that exit of those that reach the section; lane 1 over the section's first 60 m, minutes at or below 5.0 m/s; lane 1 over the last 60 m, minimum minute (count at or below 5); trajectory samples stopped (< 0.5 m/s) within the section's last 20 m, lanes 0–1; driven vehicles unfinished; forced; forced deferred (vehicle-steps); exits given up; pairs released; collisions. Rows at the corridor's demand (4,919 / 0.2122 / 1,412 veh/h, of 470 planned entrants) and at the fixture's (4,500 / 0.25 / 1,400, of 466). macOS record (CI note below).
