**What the table says.** (a) At the corridor's demand the exit side is clean at every seed under the shipped rules: lane 1's last 60 m never at or below 5 m/s, no trajectory sample stopped in the section's last 20 m (was 125 at seed 3), 97–98 % of the exiters that reach the section exit, no exit given up, nothing collides; the exit-side test passes at seed 3 without a marker. (b) The priority's moment matters for the entrance: from zone entry it costs 4–18 entrants at seeds 3 and 5 (407 / 402 against 411 / 420) and doubles the forced exits (40 / 38 against 22 / 23) — an exiter that holds its lane-0 follower for the whole zone brakes the entrant stream 80 m early; from the due moment (4 s into the zone, the same instant the forced change is allowed) it reads 419 / 414 / 419 against 411 / 412 / 420 — no cost on the mean, within the seed spread of 397–424 the seventh derivation recorded — with fewer minutes of lane 1's first 60 m at or below 5 m/s at seed 3 (2 against 4). (c) The give-up is a backstop, not the fix: no exit is given up at any seed under the shipped form (the priority gets the exiter in), and gated on distance alone it took one exit that a rolling vehicle still made. (d) The entrance criterion is met at no seed by a margin: 419 against 419.4 at seeds 3 and 5 is the criterion's rounding, not a pass, and two minutes at 5.0 m/s at seed 3 fail the strict lane criterion; the marker stays. (e) What the fixture cannot show: the corridor's lock does not resolve by itself, the fixture's did after 4 releases, so the 21 unfinished and 8,971 deferred of the corridor are the mechanism above sustained — the next corridor round (VM, the same 35-min slice, 4 seeds) is the test of these rules, and the counters to read are `n_missed_exit` (should be small: exiters getting in by priority, not giving up), `n_forced_deferred` (should fall by an order of magnitude) and the first standstill's position.

**Bookkeeping.** `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` keeps its strict marker with these numbers; `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_does_not_lock` keeps its pins (seed 5 releases 14); `test_th52_weave_at_corridor_demand_exit_side` is new and unmarked; `TestWeaveExitPriority` (fake harness: the held gap behind the vehicle beside the exiter, the kept commitment, the due moment, the change once the beside vehicle has cleared, the halted give-up with `changeTarget` and both counters, `exit_giveup_m = 0`); `TestWeavePairRelease` moves its stopped pair from 0.5 m to 6 m before the end (a halted exiter 0.5 m from the end is now given up before it can be released) and `TestWeaveExitBookkeeping` its hand-back case from 5 m to 10 m; the API and sweep tests carry `n_missed_exit`. `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` and every other weave test pass. Golden `merge_weave` regenerated (the priority binds on `weave.osm`): mean travel time 70.83 → 71.00 s, p90 85.45 → 85.45 s, σ_v spatial 4.274 → 4.276 m/s, σ_v temporal 3.944 → 3.945 m/s, VMT 169.630 → 169.629 veh-km, VHT 1.7681 → 1.7685 veh-h, fuel 93.02 → 93.00 ml/veh-km, throughput 1,687.5 veh/h, changes in/out/forced 17/20/1 and exits 33 unchanged, collisions 0, config hash 436cd4ec9e5d unchanged. Session artifacts (the exit-side probe with its trace, the per-variant runs) were not committed.

### 2026-09-24 (block 3) — CI note: the capacity fixture is platform-sensitive

The GitHub Actions runner (Linux, the eclipse-sumo 1.27.1 wheel) fails the
no-lock test at seed 4 on every commit since the sixth derivation while the
same code passes on the macOS laptop that produced the tables above: Linux
seed 4 gives entrance 331 of 466 (macOS 412), `n_entered` 417, changed in /
out 184 / 228, forced 45, and a lane-1 minute at or below 2 m/s — a lock.
Seed 5 passes on both. The fixture at capacity is chaotic near the
criterion, so a platform's floating-point differences in SUMO (or a
tie-break in the rules that depends on exact equality) change the outcome;
the seed-4 case is marked a non-strict expected failure until the review of
derivations three to six says which. Every fixture number in this document
is therefore a macOS record; the corridor rounds run on Linux VMs and are
the numbers that count.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, the upstream-entrance fixture): the corridor's last 850 m in one fixture — the head is the weave's approach, with or without the 40648744 entrance

**Why.** docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §11a puts the head of every I-94 WB queue in the corridor's last 850 m — the 40648744 entrance (9.88 km; a plain `lane_change` merge onto a guessed 102 m acceleration lane, 9.944–10.198 km, nothing driven) feeding the T.H.52 weaving section (769818012 → 18207598 on edge 51388891, 10.33–10.72 km) 230 m after the lane's end — from the warm-up on in all 19 running seeds, and §11b (cloud, 4 seeds, first hour) places the first standstill at minute 11 at 10.25–10.30 km in lane 0, between that lane's end and the gore, with lane 1 following from minute 12 at 10.20 km; with the entrance on the `scripted` merge it comes at minute 14, otherwise the same. `weave_th52.osm` (the section alone) passes its corridor-demand test; it has no such entrance. `tests/fixtures/weave_th52_upstream.osm` is the local twin of the stretch: three through lanes; E1 (way 300) onto the added lane of way 110 (netconvert 101.9 m, lane 0 dead-ends at its end with no patch, as `--ramps.guess` gives the corridor); way 111, three lanes, 230.1 m (edge 40648738's 229 m); the section (way 102, four lanes, 308.3 m) from E2 (way 200) to X (way 201) as in `weave_th52.osm`; 583 m of approach and 617 m after. The vacate rule's window (150 m before the section start, truncated to the edge before it) lies inside way 111, as on the corridor.

**Flows (`TH52_UPSTREAM_DEMAND`, tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py).** The same 6000 s step of `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul_weave.yaml` (07:10–07:15) that `TH52_CORRIDOR_DEMAND` uses, propagated in the scenario's ramp order: the entry's 1.0956 veh/s through the thirteen ramps before 40648744 arrives at that entrance as **3,793 veh/h**; on-ramp 40648744's `inflow` is 0.3 veh/s = **1,080 veh/h** (`artifacts/demand_mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.json`: `method: conservation`, no detector; 889 veh/h over the day, 1,148 in 07–08); so **4,873 veh/h** arrive at the section (`TH52_CORRIDOR_DEMAND` says 4,919 from an earlier propagation; the 46 veh/h are within the artifact's carried residuals); on-ramp 769818012's `inflow` 0.3922 veh/s = **1,412 veh/h**, off-ramp 18207598's `exit_fraction` **0.2122**. The plan's exit draw applies to every vehicle passing X, so 70 of E1's 360 and 99 of E2's 470 are exit-bound too (route ids `on0_off2`, `on1_off2`). 20 simulated minutes at seed 3, step 0.5 s, the fleet defaults (as every fixture table here: `lc_strategic` 1.0, `lc_keep_right` 1.0, IDM — not the corridor's 5.0 / 0.0 / EIDM population), full demand from t = 0 with an empty network, so the queue forms at once and "minute" is not the corridor's minute; positions are. Wall-clock 3–4 s per run (libsumo).

**The test.** `test_th52_with_upstream_entrance_at_corridor_demand` asserts the exit-side test's criteria (no collision, ≥ 90 % of the exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit, lane 1 over the section's last 60 m > 5 m/s in every 60-s window after 120 s, ≤ 10 % of the driven unfinished) plus E1 departing ≥ 90 % of its plan and lane 1 over the last 60 m of E1's acceleration lane > 5 m/s in every such window. It fails and is marked `xfail(strict=True)` with the numbers below.

**Both variants at seed 3** (`meta.json`, trajectories at 2 Hz; x along the chain, the acceleration lane at 583–685 m, way 111 at 685–915 m, the section at 915–1,223 m; "first standstill" = the first 50 m × 1 min bin with ≥ 20 samples below 0.1 m/s; "< 2 m/s bins" = mean speed below 2 m/s over ≥ 20 samples, the §11b criterion, in the 230 m stretch only):

| | E1 `lane_change` (the corridor's) | E1 `scripted` |
|---|---|---|
| departed, all / mainline / E1 / E2 | 1,624 of 2,094 (0.78) / 972 of 1,264 (0.77) / **225 of 360 (0.63)** / 427 of 470 (0.91) | 1,634 of 2,094 (0.78) / 1,052 of 1,264 (0.83) / **176 of 360 (0.49)** / 406 of 470 (0.86) |
| first standstill (any lane) | minute 0, x 650–700 m (the acceleration lane's end) | minute 0, x 600–650 m (the acceleration lane) |
| < 2 m/s bins in the 230 m stretch, minutes 0–7 | lane 1 at 0–100 m into the stretch from minute 2 (0–150 m in minutes 3–4 and 7); lane 0 never inside the stretch, only on the acceleration lane and upstream; lane 2 at the stretch's first 50 m in minutes 3–5 | lane 1 from minute 6 (0–100 m into the stretch; 0–150 m in minute 7), lane 2 minute 7; lane 0 only on the acceleration lane |
| weave counters: entered / changed in / out / forced / deferred / unfinished / vacated / refused / pair releases / exited of reached | 414 / 209 / 203 / 23 / 248 / 2 / 201 / 59 / 15 / 283 of 291 | 431 / 225 / 202 / 15 / 17 / 4 / 198 / 59 / 7 / 281 of 286 |
| weave waits in / out, s | 4.8 / 13.3 | 6.3 / 13.0 |
| scripted merge (E1) | — | entered 153, changed 150, forced 150, unfinished 3, wait 28.7 s mean / 38.1 s p90 |
| E1 on the acceleration lane: time, merge position | 21.4 s mean, 47.5 s p90; 46 m mean, 69 m p90 | 28.4 s, 37.9 s; 35 m, 41 m |
| lane 1 over the acceleration lane's last 60 m, by minute (2–19) | 3.7, 3.9, 2.7, 5.2, 3.0, 4.7, 2.5, 2.6, 4.0, 4.8, 4.7, 2.1, 2.0, 3.0, 3.2, 5.2, 3.8, 4.6 | 6.6, 6.4, 5.5, 5.9, 5.7, 4.4, 5.2, 5.0, 1.5, 6.0, 3.0, 6.1, 6.2, 6.0, 4.2, 2.9, 3.7, 5.8 |
| lane 1 over the section's last 60 m, minimum minute | 3.8 (minute 11; 6.2 in 10, 7.4 in 12; 16–19 m/s in minutes 2–8) | 10.3 (minute 15) |
| flow past the acceleration lane's end by lane 0 / 1 / 2, veh/h (minutes 5–19, vehicles seen within 15 m) | 1,012 / **576** / 1,476 = 3,064 | 980 / **564** / 1,548 = 3,092 |
| flow at the section start by lane 0–3 | 972 / 1,236 / 1,068 / 1,448 = 4,724 | 996 / 1,116 / 1,084 / 1,496 = 4,692 |

| collisions | 0 | 0 |

The scripted E1 delays the stretch's standstill (minute 6 against 2) and delivers fewer of its own (0.49 against 0.63), and everything downstream is the same to within a few percent — the §11b reading (minute 14 against 11, "otherwise the same") on the fixture.

**Where the head is.** The space–lane table (mean speed, minutes 5–19, 50 m bins) for the `lane_change` variant: lane 0 / 1 / 2 (/ 3) read 0.7 / 1.9 / 8.2 at x = 450–550 (way 101, upstream of the merge), 2.4 / 1.8 / 1.8 / 9.8 at 600 (the acceleration lane, its lane 0), 4.4 / 3.2 / 2.9 / 10.5 at 650, then on way 111 **4.4 / 1.4 / 11.2** at 700, 4.1 / 1.4 / 12.3 at 750, 5.4 / 3.2 / 13.3 at 800, 7.5 / 6.6 / 13.5 at 850, and in the section 8–14 m/s in every lane from 900 on. The slowest lane of the whole network is the **middle lane of way 111 over its first 100 m, at 1.4 m/s**; a mainline vehicle spends 48.6 s in lane 1 over the first 80 m of way 111 (16.7 s in lane 0, 6.5 s in lane 2), and lane 1 carries 576 veh/h past the acceleration lane's end while lanes 0 and 2 carry 1,012 and 1,476. That crawl runs back through the acceleration lane's edge (lane 2 of way 110 = the same lane, 1.8–2.9 m/s) and way 101 (1.9 m/s), and the standstill map fills the approach from minute 5. The section itself is not the bottleneck by speed: all four lanes at 8–14 m/s, the exit side clean (283 of 291, no sample stopped at the gore's end, 15 pair releases).

**Per rule.** Three isolating runs on the same fixture and seed (the diagnostic script is a session record, not committed; every number is from `meta.json` and the trajectories):

1. *E1's merge onto lane 0 with the weave 230 m downstream.* Lane 0 of way 111 runs 42–62 veh/km at 4.1–5.4 m/s carrying 1,012 veh/h — 237 of E1's vehicles seen on the chain in 20 min (≈ 700 veh/h) plus 120 mainline through and 52 mainline exiters who enter way 111 in lane 0; it is neither the slowest nor the densest lane (lane 1: 68–85 veh/km). **Without the weave's ramps** (E2 and X removed, E1's 1,080 onto the 3,793 alone): the merge passes 4,196 of the 4,873 veh/h (1,116 / 1,452 / 1,628 by lane), E1 departs 264 of 360 (0.73), the mainline 1,216 of 1,264 (0.96), lane 0 queues upstream of the acceleration lane at 1.2–1.5 m/s (450–600 m) and the acceleration lane reads 3.1 m/s at its start, and everything past the lane's end runs at 11–22 m/s. So the plain `lane_change` merge is itself ≈ 14 % short of its demand at this flow, but its shortfall stays at the merge; it does not reach the stretch before the gore.
2. *The vacate rule on E1's merged vehicles.* Yes, they are asked: of E1's 212 vehicles reaching the chain, 162 through ones enter the vacate window (the last 150 m of way 111) in lane 0; **125 move to lane 1 before the gore** (15.1 s in lane 0 of the window, p90 22.3 s) and 37 stay (25.9 s, p90 43.7 s); E1's 36 exiters stay in lane 0 (23.9 s), as they should. The mainline's own lane-0 through vehicles add 78 vacated and 22 stayed. The requests total 260 in 20 min (`n_vacated` 201, `n_vacate_refused` 59) ≈ 780 veh/h asked into a lane carrying 560–580 veh/h at that point, under mode 512 (the vehicle adapts its speed to reach a gap, the target lane's follower is informed as for an urgent change). **They do not stall in lane 0** (4.1–5.4 m/s over the window); it is the lane they are asked into that stalls. **With the rule off** (`vacate_ahead_m = 0`, E1 kept): lane 1 over way 111's first 80 m takes 15.8 s per vehicle instead of 48.6 s and reads 3.5–3.7 m/s at 700–850 m instead of 1.4, lane 0 falls to 1.8–2.3 m/s — and the section locks as the third derivation found: lane 1 over the section's first 60 m 1.3–3.2 m/s in minutes 5–11 and over its last 60 m 2.1–3.4 in minutes 4–7, 79 forced, 1,524 deferred, 338 pair releases, 5 exits given up, E2 departs 290 of 470 (0.62), total departed 1,543 (0.74 against 0.78). The rule moves the head from the section's end to the 230 m before its start; it does not create the shortfall.
3. *Plain car-following capacity at the combined flow.* **Without E1** (the section alone under the same 4,873 veh/h arriving, 1,412 entering, 0.2122 exiting — the corridor-demand case on this geometry): the mainline departs 1,194 of 1,624 (0.735), E2 413 of 470 (0.88), and the head is in the same place with the same numbers — lane 1 of way 111 at 1.3–1.4 m/s over 700–800 m, 47.0 s per vehicle over its first 80 m, 560 veh/h past the acceleration lane's end against 1,028 / 1,548 in lanes 0 / 2, the first < 2 m/s bins in lane 1 at 100–150 m into the stretch from minute 2 and never lane 0, **263 vacate requests** (200 vacated, 63 refused; 201 mainline through vehicles moving left in the window, 69 staying), exit side clean (295 of 303), section lanes at 10–19 m/s. The lanes of way 111 are not at car-following capacity (an IDM lane at the defaults carries ≈ 2,000 veh/h; lane 2 runs 1,476–1,548 at 11–13 m/s beside the crawl); the section's four lanes carry 4,700 veh/h at 8–19 m/s; and the crossing (1,412 in, ≈ 1,030 mainline exiters out over 308 m) is carried. What is at capacity is the entry into lane 1 of the edge before the section: ≈ 13 requested changes per minute into a 560 veh/h lane under SUMO's urgent-change cooperation, at the point where the mainline's lane-0 through traffic (263 of 831 through vehicles enter way 111 in lane 0 in this run; 75 of 628 with E1 in front, E1's 237 taking the lane instead) is asked to leave lane 0.

**Answer.** On the fixture the 40648744 entrance is not what forms the head: the head — lane 1 of the 230 m before the T.H.52 gore at 1.3–1.4 m/s, the approach queued behind it — forms identically with the entrance removed and its 1,080 veh/h added to the mainline (the same 260 / 263 vacate requests, 47–49 s per vehicle in lane 1 over the stretch's first 80 m, the same first < 2 m/s bins), and identically with the entrance on the scripted merge (three to four minutes later). The entrance's own merge is short of its demand (0.73 delivered alone; 0.63 with the weave behind it, whose queue reaches back over the acceleration lane at 0.5–5 m/s) and it puts its vehicles into the lane the vacate rule then empties, but the request count and the lane-1 crawl are the weave approach's own, made by the vacate rule's requests into the middle lane. The per-rule numbers say the third derivation's rule is now the head's mechanism at the corridor's demand: with it, the middle lane before the gore; without it, the section's end — and the total delivered is lower without it. The cloud's lane-0-first standstill at 10.25–10.30 km (§11b) is not reproduced in lane order (here lane 1 first, lane 0 never below 2 m/s inside the stretch) — the fixture runs the builder's fleet defaults and IDM, the corridor `lc_strategic` 5.0, `lc_keep_right` 0.0 and the EIDM population, which is the next thing to align before reading the lane order across. What the fixture supports as the next lever: the vacate request's target — the middle lane within 150 m — rather than the entrance's merge model.

**Bookkeeping.** `tests/fixtures/weave_th52_upstream.osm`; `TH52_UPSTREAM_DEMAND`, `_th52_upstream_config(seed, merge)`, `_lane_speed_windows(df, x0, lane)` and `test_th52_with_upstream_entrance_at_corridor_demand` (strict expected failure) in tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py; the runner unchanged.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, short sections): the 136 m Ruth St twin — the give-ups are an abreast crossing pair at the gore's end, and no safe scaling of the zone or the priority removes them

**Why Ruth St.** docs/ONBOARDING_MNDOT.md §11a–§11b: the Ruth St weave (entrance 745524613 → C-D split 18208090, 136 m, x ≈ 5.3 km) is the corridor's hardest section in every seed of the 20-seed battery (47 % of entrants forced, mean waits 130 s in / 79 s out, 50–72 k deferred forced changes per four-hour run, two "good" seeds semi-locking there) and gives up 3.3–4.2 % of its exiters in the first hour, above the battery's 2 % threshold, where T.H.52 (305 m) is within. Its flows, read from `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul_weave.yaml` (the series `artifacts/demand_mndot_i94_wb_stpaul.json` wrote — the entrance `detector_scaled` off rnd_88819, the split `detector_scaled` off rnd_88817 — the mainline the entry's `inflow` propagated through the six ramps before it in corridor order): the entrance peaks at 0.1070 veh/s = **385 veh/h** in the 3900–4200 s step (07:05–07:10; 0.1278 at 8400 s over four hours), with **4,049 veh/h** arriving and `exit_fraction` **0.0295** (131 veh/h exiting); the C-D split peaks at **0.2893** in the 9000–9300 s step (08:30–08:35; 881 veh/h exiting) with 2,824 veh/h arriving and 220 veh/h entering; the first hour proper carries 2,050–4,260 veh/h with 143–343 entering and 0.017–0.037 exiting. `RUTH_DEMAND` in `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py` carries both steps.

**The fixture.** `tests/fixtures/weave_ruth.osm`, `weave_th52.osm` with the section shortened: three through lanes, the auxiliary lane from way 200 to way 201 over 0.001150° of longitude (98 m) that netconvert makes **135.7 m** with the junction geometry (T.H.52's 267 m becomes 308.4 m the same way; the golden `weave.osm` measures 178.4 m), ≈ 600 m of approach and downstream. Criteria (`assert_exit_side`): the exit-side test's (no collision, ≥ 90 % of the exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit, lane 1's last 60 m above 5 m/s in every 60-s window after 120 s, ≤ 10 % of driven vehicles unfinished) plus ≤ 2 % given up (`n_missed_exit / n_reached_section_exiting`) and the entrance departing ≥ 90 %.

**What a 136 m section does under the rules (trace, `_weave_step` wrapped; the corridor's fleet at the exit peak, seed 3; macOS record on the runner of 01dd5ec).** 116 exiters were driven; 114 entered the section in lane 1 (2 in lane 2; the rest of the 290 reached the section already in the auxiliary lane, 101_0 → 102_0 being connected, or are ramp-to-ramp draws) at a mean 16.6 m/s (p10–p90 9.6–21.6). Median time in the section 1.2 s: 69 of 116 changed by acceptance in the 56 m before the zone. The rest entered the 80 m zone 3.5 s after the section at a mean 11.3 m/s (5.7–15.9) — the zone is 59 % of the section and the cooperative stretch before it, 56 m ≈ 3.5 s, is shorter than one accepted gap opened by a follower at ≤ b from abreast (≈ 3.6 s, ≈ 55 m at 15 m/s) — and were due (`force_after_s` 4 s) with 26–40 m and ≈ 3 s left at 8–10 m/s: 35 changed in the zone after the due moment (forced or by the priority), 4 before it, 8 were given up. All 8 halted at the gore's end 9–14 s after zone entry with a lane-0 vehicle beside them that was itself halted at the end of the auxiliary lane (an entrant needing lane 1: v00972 stood at 0.0 m/s from 548 s to 578 s while v00391, v00404 and v00407 halted beside it and were given up in turn), every due step refused by `_weave_force_gap_ok` on an overlap — leader 119, follower 97, both 92, closing 118 of 451 refused due steps across the run — and none with a single accepted or guard-passing step. The priority hold does its part (the follower is driven to rest one changer minGap farther back) but the vehicle *beside* the exiter is waited for (`a_c = inf`), and on a short section that vehicle is an entrant braking for the same lane end, not exit-bound traffic streaming past as at T.H.52. So the give-up is an **abreast crossing pair at the gore's end**, formed because entrant and exiter arrive together and have no room to sort out first; the give-up (the exiter rerouted through) is what breaks it, at one exit each. The vacate window (150 m) lies on the edge before the section (282 m here) and is truncated to it; it does not interact with the section's length, and the entrance departs 73 of 73 at every seed.

**The length effect alone (fleet defaults, seed 3, Ruth 135.7 m against T.H.52 308.4 m at the same flows).** 4,049 / 0.2893 / 385: forced 66 against 20, deferred 162 against 85; 3,923 / 0.1876 / 181 (the 6600 s step): forced 55 against 6, deferred 227 against 2, lane 1's last 60 m 5.2 against 15.6 m/s; 4,500 / 0.25 / 600: 1 given up against 0, lane 1 4.3 against 9.8 m/s, forced 74 against 26, deferred 478 against 55, 20 releases against 0; 4,919 / 0.2122 / 1,412 (T.H.52's corridor demand): 2 given up against 0, lane 1 3.5 m/s in 4 minutes against 9.6, forced 86 against 8, deferred 532 against 37, entrance 372 against 419 of 470. The short section forces 3–10× as many changes and defers 5–100× as many at every load.

**Measurements (the corridor's fleet, exit peak, 20 min; given up of reached / lane 1's last 60 m minimum (minutes ≤ 5) / forced / deferred / collisions; seeds 3 / 4 / 5 unless stated).** Baseline (runner of 01dd5ec): **8 / 3 / 4** of 290 / 281 / 274, 3.3 / 4.1 / 4.3 m/s (2 / 1 / 1), 33 / 15 / 17, 608 / 268 / 326, 0; the fleet defaults at the same flows: 0 / 0 / 0, 10.7 / 9.3 / 13.3, 12 / 9 / 10, 22 / 15 / 8, 0. Scalings, each binding only when `length_m < 2 · force_within_m`: (a) forced zone = the whole section, delay 4 s (with the priority from the section's start): 2 / 4 / 3, 4.9 / 4.6 / 4.2, 22 / 23 / 18, 165 / 388 / 346, 0 — and, priority left at the due moment, 2 / 2 / 2 with **4 collisions at seed 4**; delay 2 s: 4 / 2 / 2 with **29 collisions at seed 3**; delay 3.4 s (= 4 · L / 160): 5 / 5 / 2; (b) whole section, one-step delay 0.5 s for both: **1 / 2 / 0**, 5.7 / 6.5 / 10.9 m/s, 28 / 30 / 34, 146 / 225 / 94, 0 — extended to seeds 6–15: 1 / 3 / 1 / 0 / 1 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 3 / 2 given up, lane 1 down to 4.5 m/s at seeds 7 and 13, and **1 / 4 / 1 collisions at seeds 8 / 14 / 15**, all in the auxiliary lane's first 30 m (forced changes at speed under mode 256 over a follower closing within the guard's 0.6-s margin); (c) zero delay: 2 / 1 / 1 with **1 / 5 / 1 collisions**, on the arrival step (the neighbour listing of a vehicle that just crossed the junction); (d) the exit priority alone from the section's start, forced zone unchanged: 6 / 3 / 3, 3.5 / 3.0 / 2.4 m/s — it removes the exiter's early easing behind the vehicle beside it, so more pairs reach the end abreast; from the start after 1 s: 3 / 3 / 3 (seeds 6–8: 1 / 4 / 5); after 4 s: 9 / 3 / 6 with 3 collisions at seed 3; from the zone's entry with no delay: 8 / 3 / 3; (e) the priority for both movements (the entrant at the auxiliary lane's end held by its lane-1 follower one minGap farther back, the T.H.52 exit-side fix mirrored): 7 / 2 / 2 (seeds 6–8: 2 / 2 / 4) — measured on the working tree with WP-44's in-flight vacate re-derivation, whose baseline at seed 3 is 4. What none of them can do: brake the entrant beside an exiter harder than its comfortable `b` (the influencer clamps a `slowDown` target at the vehicle's `decel` under the default `speedMode`, CLAUDE.md §3.3), which is what a driver on a 136 m weave does; and an earlier last resort is a forced change at speed, which mode 256 does not make safe.

**Decision.** No scaling ships. `microsim.runner._weave_short_section_rule` returns the fixed `force_within_m` / `force_after_s` for every section and the flag `short` (`length_m < 2 · force_within_m`), which `meta.json["weave_sections"][i]["short_section"]` carries so the battery can name its short sections; its docstring records the derivation and the table above. The test module keeps the fleet-default cases green at both demand points and seeds 3–5, marks the corridor-fleet exit peak a strict expected failure at seeds 3–5 with the numbers, and the entrance peak at seed 4 (2 of 41 given up, 4.9 %) a non-strict one — under the vacate rule being re-derived beside this work (WP-44) that seed passes and the exit-peak seeds still fail. Golden `merge_weave` (178.4 m, not short) reproduced and every T.H.52 test keeps its outcome with these hunks on 01dd5ec (the working tree's golden and exit-side failures at the time of writing are the vacate re-derivation's, not these). Next, if the give-ups are to be removed rather than named: a speed-aware forced guard (the follower's braking distance rather than `s0 + 0.6 · Δv`) that would make an earlier last resort safe — it changes T.H.52 and the golden, so it is a derivation of its own — or a yield rule for the entrant beside an exiter that does not need a brake beyond `b` (it must be decided before the pair is abreast, in the ramp's anticipation window).

**Bookkeeping.** `tests/fixtures/weave_ruth.osm`; `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py` (`RUTH_DEMAND`, `CORRIDOR_FLEET`, `ruth_config`, `lane1_last60m_windows`, `assert_exit_side`, `TestShortSectionRule`, `TestRuthStWeave`); `microsim.runner._weave_short_section_rule`, the `rule` entry of `weave_states` and `short_section` in the meta; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (short sections). Session artifacts (the trace harness, the per-variant runs) not committed.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, the vacate rule re-derived): the fleet aligned, a form that never asks the target lane to brake, a bound on the asks — measured, the form not made the default

**Why.** The upstream-entrance section above ends on two open items: align the fixture's fleet with the corridor's before reading the lane order across, and the vacate request's target — the third derivation's rule asks ≈ 780 veh/h of through vehicles into a middle lane carrying ≈ 560 veh/h under mode 512, where SUMO's informed-follower cooperation brakes the lane-1 follower for every request, and that lane is the head of the corridor's queue at 1.3–1.4 m/s. This block does both and derives the rule again so that it never asks lane 1 to brake. Every number below is from `tests/fixtures/weave_th52_upstream.osm` under `TH52_UPSTREAM_DEMAND` (or the fixture named), 20 simulated minutes, step 0.5 s, on this laptop (macOS; the CI note on platform sensitivity applies); the measurement script is a session record, not committed; the tables read `meta.json` and the 2 Hz trajectories. "Stretch" = lane 1 over the whole 230 m of way 111; "accel end" = lane 1 over the last 60 m of E1's acceleration lane; "gore" = lane 1 over the section's last 60 m; minutes 2–19; flows are distinct vehicles within 15 m of a cross-section over minutes 5–19 — here 20 m past the acceleration lane's end onto way 111 (the earlier section's "past the acceleration lane's end" bins straddled the 110/111 boundary; on way 111 the third derivation's numbers read 1,024 / 576 / 1,492 veh/h by lane at seed 3).

**1. The fleet aligned.** `corridor_fleet_block()` (tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py) reads the `fleet` block of `scenarios/mndot_i94_wb_stpaul_weave.yaml` (EIDM, heterogeneity 0.15, `idm_calibration: artifacts/idm_i24_capacity.json`, `lc_strategic` 5.0, `lc_strategic_ramp` 1.0, `lc_keep_right` 0.0) and `test_th52_with_upstream_entrance_on_the_corridor_fleet` runs the two-entrance fixture on it, strict `xfail` beside the default-fleet test, which is kept as it is. Seed 3, the third derivation's runner (before anything below):

Seeds 4 / 5 on the corridor fleet: E1 266 / 254, E2 404 / 393; at seed 5 the section's **end** locks from minute 16 (gore 2.9, 0.1, 0.0, 0.0 m/s, 23 unfinished, 2,963 deferred, 38 releases) — the third derivation's runner locks the T.H.52 gore's end on the corridor's fleet at one seed in three, which the default fleet never showed. Reading: the corridor's fleet delays the middle lane's crawl by five minutes (`lc_keep_right` 0 keeps through traffic out of lane 0, so 167 vacate requests instead of 260) and leaves it in the same place, lane 1 still first and lane 0 only at minute 15 — the cloud's lane-0-first standstill (§11b) is not the fleet's doing either. What the alignment adds is the seed-5 lock, a corridor-fleet finding the default-fleet fixtures cannot see.

**2. The rule re-derived** (`microsim.runner._weave_vacate_step`, `_weave_vacate_gap_ok`, `_weave_vacate_bound_veh_h`, `_idm_desired_gap`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["vacate_no_follower_braking"]`, `["vacate_max_veh_h"]`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 dated block). Two things, both selectable, both hash-neutral unless set:

* **The gap-conditioned form** (`vacate_no_follower_braking = 1`): a through vehicle in the window is evaluated every step, nearest the section first, against the target-lane gap it is in — the weave's own time gaps to the leader and the follower (`accept_gap_s` = 0.6 s) **plus** the follower's IDM desired gap at its current speed and approach rate, `s*(v_F, v_F − v_c)` (Treiber, Hennecke & Helbing 2000; CLAUDE.md §3.1), so the follower needs no braking beyond its free-road term — and asked only on a step when the gap accepts, `changeLane(vid, lane_to, step_s)` under mode **768** (SUMO's safety check, no speed adaptation), one request per accepting step, the hold set at the first request and restored at the target lane or the section. A vehicle never offered a gap keeps its own model and is counted once in the new `n_vacate_skipped_no_gap`; `n_vacate_requests` counts requests in vehicle-steps. The third derivation's form (asked once, mode 512, the follower informed) stays as `0`, the default.
* **The bound** (both forms): vehicles first asked in the last 60 s are limited to `vacate_max_veh_h`; a positive value is the bound, `0` (the default) the target lane's spare capacity, `VACATE_LANE_CAPACITY_VEH_H` = 2,050 veh/h (one IDM lane at the fleet defaults) less the flow that lane carried into the window over the same 60 s, floored at zero. Not a fitted value.

**3. Measured.** The two-entrance fixture, seed 3, both fleets (E1 / E2 departed; vacated / refused / skipped / requests; releases; forced; stretch minimum after 120 s and minutes above 5 m/s; accel end minimum; gore minimum; exited of reached; unfinished; collisions):

Seeds 4 / 5: default fleet — third derivation E1 240 / 218, E2 426 / 429; shipping form 227 / 207, 411 / 407 (skipped 11 / 11, stretch never above 5 m/s after minute 3); gap-conditioned 208 / 196, 321 / 307 with 159 / 148 releases and **one collision at seed 5**. Corridor fleet — third derivation 266 / 254, 404 / 393 (the seed-5 end lock above); shipping form 253 / 240, 382 / 390 (skipped 4 / 3, no lock at seed 5: gore never below 12.9 m/s, 3 unfinished, 2 releases); gap-conditioned 266 / 242, 329 / 338 (no lock either, 15 / 10 releases). The gap-conditioned form's request-to-change ratio does **not** invert on the default fleet (3 of 38 requests at seed 3, 4 of 55 at seed 4, 4 of 5 at seed 5) and does on the corridor fleet (19 of 30, 25 of 26, 32 of 35), because on the default fleet the candidates reach the window at 2–6 m/s behind E1's merge while lane 1 runs at 10–25 m/s: the follower's desired gap at a 10–20 m/s approach rate is 60–100 m, which a 1,100–1,600 veh/h lane never holds, and the few gaps that pass are refused by SUMO's own 768 check. With vacating gone the section's end locks as the third derivation found "without the rule": 128–159 pair releases, 57–72 forced changes, E2 307–321 of 470. The weave's within-b acceptance in place of the desired-gap term (the follower absorbing at ≤ b, still under 768) was also measured at seed 3: 45 requests, 2 changes, E2 300 — the same collapse; not shipped.

**The other fixtures, the shipping form against the third derivation, seeds 3 / 4 / 5** (`weave_th52.osm`): at the corridor's demand (`test_th52_weave_at_corridor_demand_exit_side`) the entrance 386 / 398 / 384 of 470 (was 419 / 428 / 434), exit 299 of 306 / 305 of 312 / 302 of 311 (was 300 of 310 / 307 of 315 / 317 of 325), the gore never below 11.9 / 7.5 / 8.7 m/s (was 9.6 / 12.9 / 12.3), 150 / 147 / 162 vacated, 66 / 71 / 66 refused, **19 / 17 / 17 skipped by the bound**, 3 / 1 / 2 unfinished, no give-up, no collision — the test passes; at the fixture's demand (`test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows`, `…_does_not_lock`) the entrance 406 / 386 / 393 of 466 (was 419 / 414 / 419), lane 1's first 60 m at or below 5 m/s in 1 / 1 / 0 minutes (was 2 / 1 / 1; minimum 5.0 / 5.0 / 5.6), 6 / 3 / 4 unfinished, 2 / 0 / 4 releases, 18 / 4 / 15 skipped, no collision — the strict marker keeps its place with the new numbers, the no-lock pins hold (seed 4 with no release; `min_releases` for seed 5 is 1 against 4); the gap-conditioned form there: entrance 309 / 305, gore 3.4 m/s and first 60 m 1.3 m/s, 66 / 245 releases, 9 / 17 unfinished — it fails both. The moderate fixture (`test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl`) is **byte-identical** in all three seeds (10 / 5 / 7 vacated, 10 / 16 / 11 refused, none skipped). Golden `merge_weave` regenerated: throughput 1,687.5 veh/h, changes in / out / forced 17 / 20 / 1, exits 33 and the hash 436cd4ec9e5d unchanged; mean travel time 70.998 → 70.816 s, p90 85.45 → 86.27 s, σ_v spatial 4.276 → 4.294 m/s, σ_v temporal 3.945 → 3.947 m/s, VMT 169.629 → 169.653 veh-km, VHT 1.7685 → 1.7683 veh-h, fuel 93.00 → 93.03 ml/veh-km, collisions 0. Attributed: with `vacate_max_veh_h` set far above the flow the old fuel total is reproduced to the last digit, so the drift is the spare-capacity bound delaying an ask by a step when a burst of through vehicles fills the minute's budget (7 vacated / 14 refused / 0 skipped either way).

**Decision.** The gap-conditioned form ships as an option and not as the default: it does what it was derived to do — no target-lane follower is ever braked for a request, and on the corridor's fleet most of its few requests execute — but at these demands it removes the vacating that carries the section, and the target (lane 1 over the stretch above 5 m/s in every minute after 120 s, no lock, both entrances ≥ 0.9) is met by no form on any seed; the strict `xfail` markers stay on both two-entrance tests. The bound ships at its spare-capacity default as specified, binding in bursts (0–19 vehicles skipped per 20 min) and moving the T.H.52 numbers by a few percent either way; the head of the queue is unchanged by it. What the tables say about the head: it is not the request count (a bound of 300 veh/h leaves lane 1 of the stretch at 2.8–3.5 m/s with 35 vacates) nor the follower's braking (no braking at all leaves the section to lock behind it); the through vehicles that need to leave lane 0 are already at 2–6 m/s when they are asked, 150 m before the gore, because the lane is queued from E1's merge and the section back, and a vehicle at that speed cannot enter a 15 m/s lane without that lane yielding. The next lever the fixture supports is asking earlier — upstream of E1's merge, where lane 0's through traffic still moves at speed — which needs the window to cross edges (`_weave_lane_map` lists only the edge before the section), or a target for the merge itself.

**Bookkeeping.** Runner: `_weave_vacate_step` (two forms, the bound, the hold guard extended to mode 768), `_weave_vacate_gap_ok`, `_weave_vacate_bound_veh_h`, `_idm_desired_gap`, `LC_MODE_SCRIPTED_SAFE_NO_ADAPT`, `VACATE_LANE_CAPACITY_VEH_H`, `VACATE_FLOW_WINDOW_S`; `weave_sections[i]` gains `n_vacate_skipped_no_gap` and `n_vacate_requests`, also in `WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut` and the sweep's `WEAVE_FIELDS` (no frontend change). Tests: `TestWeaveVacateGapConditioned` (8, fake harness: the acceptance's three terms, the per-step request under 768, the skip and the refusal, the fixed and the spare-capacity bound in both forms, the request counter), `test_th52_with_upstream_entrance_on_the_corridor_fleet` (strict `xfail`), the four T.H.52 tests' markers and docstrings re-recorded, the API and sweep tests extended.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, the cross-edge window): the vacate window measured along the chain, 500 m by default — the middle lane of the stretch is no longer the head, the queue stands on the entrance's merge and the approach, and E1 does not recover

**Why.** The previous section ends on the lever the fixture supports: ask the through vehicles to leave lane 0 *upstream* of E1's merge, where lane 0 still moves at 11–22 m/s and lane 1 has gaps, instead of in the 230 m between the acceleration lane's end and the gore, where the candidates are already at 2–6 m/s behind the merge and lane 1 is full. That needs the window to cross edges: the third derivation's `_weave_lane_map` listed only the edge before the section, and the 150 m window was truncated to it.

**Implementation** (`microsim.runner._weave_vacate_lanes`, `_weave_vacate_step`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["vacate_ahead_m"]`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 dated block). `vacate_ahead_m` is now measured along the corridor chain from the section start across as many upstream corridor edges as it reaches. `_weave_vacate_lanes(net, chain, section_edges, offsets, ahead_m)` walks upstream edge by edge and returns `{edge: (lane feeding section lane 1, lane feeding section lane 2)}`, nearest the section first, the feeding lane derived per edge by following netconvert's connections into the lane found on the edge after it — so the index shifts where a lane is added or dropped: on `weave_th52_upstream.osm` way 111 lanes 0 → 1, way 110 (E1's acceleration lane on its right) lanes 1 → 2, way 101 lanes 0 → 1, way 100 lanes 0 → 1; on `weave_th52.osm` and `weave.osm` the approach edge and the entry edge both (0, 1). The walk stops, truncating the window there, at an edge missing from the runner's offsets, at a lane with no unique feeder (a split, or two lanes merging into one) or where both lanes are fed by one lane; empty means inert (no edge before the section, a two-lane section, `vacate_ahead_m = 0`). `_weave_vacate_step` lists the window's weave lane and target lane from the step's own `results` (the section's `lane_map` is untouched, so the gap choice of the driven movements is unchanged), the target lane continuing onto the section through the section's own lane-2 listing. An open default-form request is by lane *index*: when the vehicle crosses onto a window edge whose target lane has another index still in the weave lane, the request is re-issued there for its remaining life (`vacate["lane_to"]`); a held vehicle on a junction's internal lane (`internal_links`) is left for that step. Everything else — one ask per vehicle, mode 512 by default, the gap-conditioned option under 768, the spare-capacity bound, the counters — is as the previous section left it, and **with the window set to 150 m the new runner reproduces the previous runner's committed numbers exactly** (E1 225 / E2 390, 170 / 69 / 9 / 242 on the two-entrance fixture at seed 3; T.H.52 capacity 406 / 386 / 393 of 466 with lane 1 minima 5.0 / 5.0 / 5.6 m/s; corridor demand 386 / 398 / 384 of 470, exits 299 of 306 / 305 of 312 / 302 of 311, gore minima 11.9 / 7.5 / 8.7 m/s). `meta.json["weave_sections"][i]` gains `vacate_window_edges` (the edges the window reaches, nearest first; `[]` = inert).

**The default: 500 m, stated, not fitted.** 150 m was the HCM 7th ed. ch. 13 weaving segment's 500-ft upstream influence area — where the segment's own gap search binds. A through driver moves left for a weave where the advance signage tells him to: the MUTCD (2009 ed., §2E.33, Advance Guide Signs) places the nearest advance guide sign of a freeway exit 1/2 mile (≈ 800 m) ahead of it. 500 m is an engineering choice between the two, at the task's stated value; both ends are measured below (300 / 500 / 800 m against the 150 m reference). Hash: `config_hash_payload` omits defaults and `weave_params` is a dict the scenario sets, so no committed scenario or golden sets `vacate_ahead_m` and no committed hash changes (golden `merge_weave` keeps 436cd4ec9e5d); a scenario that had set `vacate_ahead_m: 150` explicitly keeps both its hash and its behaviour.

**Measured.** Every run 20 simulated minutes, step 0.5 s, macOS (the CI note on platform sensitivity applies); the diagnostic script is a session record, not committed; numbers from `meta.json` and the 2 Hz trajectories. Conventions as the previous section: "stretch" = way 111's 230 m (x = 685–915 m), by lane, minutes 2–19; "accel end" = lane 1 over the last 60 m of E1's acceleration lane; "gore" = lane 1 over the section's last 60 m; "first < 2 m/s bin" = the first 50 m × 1 min bin with ≥ 20 samples whose mean is below 2 m/s, from t = 0, as (minute / offset into the stretch); "criteria" = the two-entrance test's (no collision; ≥ 90 % of the exit-bound vehicles that reach the section exit; gore > 5 m/s in every minute after 120 s; ≤ 10 % of the driven unfinished; E1 ≥ 90 % of 360; accel end > 5 m/s in every such minute).

**Reading.**

1. *The section is carried and the middle lane of the stretch is no longer the head.* At 500 m the gore reads above 5 m/s in every minute at every seed on both fleets but corridor seed 4 (2.9 m/s in minute 4: 402 deferred forced changes and 19 releases in one minute, 0 unfinished — a stall, not a lock), so the two-entrance test's section-side criteria pass where at 150 m the gore fell to 3.6 / 4.9 m/s. Lane 1 over the stretch is above 5 m/s in 10 / 9 / 6 (default) and 10 / 11 / 10 (corridor) of 18 minutes against 2 / 3 / 2 and 4 / 3 / 4 at 150 m, its minima 2.5 / 2.0 / 1.5 and 3.2 / 3.2 / 2.7 m/s against 1.3 / 1.2 / 1.5 and 1.9 / 1.9 / 1.8. E2 departs 417 / 405 / 419 (default; 390 / 411 / 407 at 150 m) and 358 / 374 / 344 (corridor; 364 / 382 / 390).
2. *The queue now stands on E1's merge and the approach.* E1 departs 205 / 206 / 246 of 360 (default) and 272 / 258 / 248 (corridor) — no better than at 150 m (225 / 227 / 207 and 250 / 253 / 240) — and the acceleration lane's end reads 0.2–1.5 m/s at its minimum on every run. Upstream of the stretch the first bins below 2 m/s are on E1's acceleration-lane edge (way 110) on the default fleet — the acceleration lane from minute 0, lane 1 beside it from minute 3 at seed 3 — and on the approach proper (way 101, x = 400–550 m) on the corridor fleet, lanes 0 and 1 both from minute 4. The requests are made 300–500 m before the gore where lane 1 has gaps — fewer are refused (54 / 53 / 52 against 69 / 50 / 67) — but the bound skips more (40 / 35 / 33 against 9 / 11 / 11) and the vehicles that vacate leave lane 0 to E1's merge, whose plain `lane_change` model was already ≈ 14 % short of its 1,080 veh/h alone (the previous section's per-rule run 1). Lane 0 of the stretch reads 1.7–2.5 m/s at its minimum and is the first lane below 2 m/s there in every run at 500 m (strictly first at default seed 3 and corridor seeds 3 and 5, tied with lane 1 at the others); on the corridor fleet that is a change of order — at 150 m lane 1 was first at every seed (minute 4 / 6 / 6 against lane 0 at 7 / 9 / 16), which is the cloud's §11b lane order (lane 0 first at 10.25–10.30 km) reached on the corridor's own fleet; on the default fleet lane 0 was already first under the bound at 150 m (the earlier section's "lane 1 first" was the runner without the bound).
3. *The sensitivity.* 800 m is worse everywhere: E2 falls to 364 / 348 / 413 and 340 / 337 / 328, the T.H.52 capacity fixture drops to 363 / 365 / 373 of 466 with 427 deferred and 46 releases at seed 3, and two runs collide once (the default fleet at seed 5 on both fixtures) — the asks reach vehicles at speed on the entry edge, 800 m from a gore they do not see. 300 m and 500 m are close on the two-entrance fixture (E2 421 / 403 / 397 against 417 / 405 / 419 on the default fleet, 368 / 372 / 371 against 358 / 374 / 344 on the corridor's; stretch lane 1 minima 2.1 / 1.5 / 2.1 against 2.5 / 2.0 / 1.5 and 2.4 / 3.8 / 3.0 against 3.2 / 3.2 / 2.7), and 300 m is the kinder of the two to T.H.52 alone: at the corridor's demand the entrance departs 423 / 394 / 429 of 470 against 398 / 394 / 395, seed 5 meets every criterion of every T.H.52 test at 300 m and at 500 m stalls at the gore's end for a minute (last 60 m 1.7 m/s, 534 deferred, 39 releases, 3 exits given up); at capacity seed 4 departs 388 against 365. On `weave.osm` (golden `merge_weave`, approach 470.5 m) a 500 m window reaches 30 m onto the entry edge.
4. *Decision.* 500 m ships as the stated default; 300 m is recorded as the value that suits the T.H.52-only fixtures and is not chosen over it, since the two-entrance fixture — the corridor's actual geometry — reads the same at both and the default is a physical statement, not a fit. The two-entrance tests keep their strict `xfail` (E1 and the acceleration lane's end fail at every window; the section side now passes). The next lever is no longer the vacate rule: it is E1's own merge onto a lane the rule keeps emptying — a driven merge for the upstream entrance (the `scripted` model gives E1 176 of 360 at 150 m, the previous section; a cooperative form of the plain merge at these flows has not been derived) — or the approach's demand itself, since 4,873 veh/h through three lanes with the exchange 230 m ahead is what the approach carries at 1–4 m/s here.

**The other fixtures at 500 m** (Table 2; the tests' outcomes): `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` keeps its strict marker (398 of 466, lane 1's first 60 m 3.7 and 4.4 m/s in minutes 2–3, no lock, 3 unfinished); `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_does_not_lock` seed 5 passes (389, minimum 3.2 m/s, 1 unfinished, 19 releases) and seed 4 — the platform-sensitive non-strict `xfail` — now fails on macOS too, on the 80 % entrance pin alone (365 of 466 = 78 %; lane 1 never below 4.1 m/s, 5 unfinished, nothing missed, 27 releases): its marker records that; `test_th52_weave_at_corridor_demand_exit_side` passes (297 of 301 exit, gore never below 6.5 m/s, 1 unfinished, entrance 398); `test_moderate_crossing_demand_does_not_crawl` passes; `test_two_sections_are_stepped_and_listed_upstream_first` (`weave_two.osm`, 12 entrants per ramp) had pinned `n_changed_in > 0` on both sections, and at any window ≥ 300 m section B's twelve entrants all change in by SUMO's own strategic change with lane 1 vacated 500 m back (driven-in 2 → 0 at B, 6 → 5 at A; every entrant departs, nothing unfinished or missed): the pin is relaxed to each section driving vehicles and handing every one back, and the window's edges (`["101", "100"]`, `["104", "103"]`) are asserted. The Ruth St module (`test_microsim_weave_short_section.py`) keeps its outcomes (the gate below). Golden `merge_weave` regenerated (hash 436cd4ec9e5d unchanged; the window now reaches over the 470.5 m approach onto the entry edge): throughput 1,687.5 → 1,680.0 veh/h, changes in / out / forced 17 / 20 / 1 → 14 / 19 / 2, exits 33 unchanged, mean travel time 70.82 → 69.35 s, p90 86.27 → 84.58 s, σ_v spatial 4.294 → 3.848 m/s, σ_v temporal 3.947 → 3.349 m/s, VMT 169.653 → 169.648 veh-km, VHT 1.7683 → 1.7399 veh-h, fuel 93.03 → 90.67 ml/veh-km, collisions 0.

**Bookkeeping.** Runner: `_weave_vacate_lanes` (new signature), `_weave_vacate_step` (window lanes from `results`, the re-addressed request, the junction-lane skip), `weave_states[i]["vacate_lanes"]` now `{edge: (from, to)}`, `vacate_window_edges` in the meta; `flowstate_core.config.WEAVE_DEFAULTS["vacate_ahead_m"]` 150 → 500 with its docstring. Tests: `test_lanes_walked_upstream_across_edges` (the fixture net), `test_walk_stops_where_a_lane_has_no_unique_feeder` (a fake net), `test_open_request_is_re_addressed_where_the_target_lane_index_shifts`; the harness helpers pin their own window at 150 m; the five T.H.52 markers and docstrings re-recorded; `test_defaults_and_hash` at 500. Session artifacts (the 48-run grid and its script) not committed.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, the short section at the 500 m window): Ruth St re-measured at the cross-edge default — the longer window helps the exiters, the entrance is unchanged, a 2·L cap is worse than either window, and both collisions are the exit-side acceptance

**Why.** The short-section study above was measured with the 150 m window truncated to the edge before the section; the cross-edge section then set the default to 500 m. The Ruth St twin has a 582.7 m approach (way 100, 300.5 m; way 101, 282.2 m), so the 500 m window now reaches 218 m onto the entry edge (`vacate_window_edges` `["101", "100"]`) and covers 86 % of it. Everything below is `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py`'s own configuration (`ruth_config`, `RUTH_DEMAND`, `CORRIDOR_FLEET`, the state `lane1_last60m_windows` reports), 20 simulated minutes, step 0.5 s, macOS (the CI note on platform sensitivity applies), the window set through `weave_params["vacate_ahead_m"]`; the grid script is a session record, not committed. "Empty" in the lane 1 column: a 60-s window after 120 s in which no vehicle was sampled in lane 1 over the section's last 60 m (the test's `len(windows) == 18` reads it as a failure).

**Reading.**

1. *The 500 m window does not make Ruth St worse on either count the study named; at the exit peak it helps the exiters.* On the corridor's fleet at the C-D split's peak the give-ups fall from 8 / 3 / 4 to **4 / 2 / 4** of 290 / 280 / 274 (2.8 / 1.1 / 1.5 % → 1.4 / 0.7 / 1.5 %, every seed within the 2 % threshold now), forced changes from 33 / 15 / 17 to 9 / 9 / 15, deferred forced steps from 608 / 268 / 326 to 252 / 111 / 275, pair releases from 14 / 3 / 7 to 2 / 1 / 2, waits in / out at seed 3 from 13.6 / 5.4 s to 7.6 / 3.1 s; the entrance departs 73 of 73 and 128 of 128 at every window, fleet and seed. The mechanism, seed 3 at the exit peak from the 2 Hz trajectories: the rule asks 137 through vehicles and 135 vacate (44 of 50 at 150 m; refused 1 against 6), most of them within 70 m of entering the window (the through vehicles' lane 0 → 1 changes on the approach sit at x = 100–150 m from the corridor's start, 430–480 m before the section, at 25 m/s; at 150 m they sit at 450–500 m, 80–130 m before it), so the weave lane in the section carries **0.43 vehicles per sample at 15.0 m/s against 0.98 at 9.8 m/s**, 11 through vehicles enter the section in it against 20, 187 of 240 exiters reach the section already in the auxiliary lane (101_0 → 102_0 is connected) against 132 of 236, and lane 0 of the section runs at 14.4 m/s with 2.2 vehicles per sample against 8.4 m/s with 3.6. The exiters that still cross in the section cross an emptier lane; the give-ups that remain are the abreast pair at the gore's end of the short-section study, in fewer instances. The through vehicles do not pile into the lane the entrants need — the entrants need the weave lane (section lane 1), which is the lane the window empties; the lane they are asked *into* (section lane 2) carries 1.12 vehicles per sample at 24.4 m/s against 1.24 at 22.8 m/s.
2. *What the 500 m window costs at the exit peak: an empty weave lane, and the exit-side acceptance's collision.* Lane 1 over the section's last 60 m carries 0.22 vehicles per sample: it is empty in 45 of the 108 10-s bins after 120 s at seed 3 (28 at 150 m), a whole minute (minute 4) has no sample, and the per-minute minima — 4.6 / 6.5 / 3.6 m/s against 3.3 / 4.1 / 4.3 — read on one to three vehicles each; the test's criterion counts an empty minute as a failure, which is left as it is because seeds 3 and 5 fail the 5 m/s line as well. Seed 4 collides once, at 600.5 s in `102_0` at 35.5 m: v00447 (exit-bound) enters the section in lane 1 at 23 m/s, changes into the auxiliary lane at x = 597 m (15 m in) with v00440 27 m ahead at 1.0 m/s — the head of a lane-0 queue of exiters at 1–6 m/s from 625 to 703 m — brakes at −9 m/s² and stops 1.9 m short of it; v00450 does the same three seconds later (into lane 0 at x = 600 m at 20 m/s, 22 m behind the now-standing v00447) and hits it. The exit-side acceptance is the weave's own time gap at the changer's speed, `s0 + 0.6 · v_c` = 16 m for a 23 m/s changer whose stopping distance at `b` is 59 m: it does not read the leader's speed. The same trace at the 2·L window, seed 5, 717.5 s at 39.2 m (v00539 stops 3 m/s short, v00542 hits it at 9 m/s). At 150 m the exiters make the same changes (68 of them in the section at seed 4, 38 at more than 15 m/s; 77 and 49 at 500 m) and the pair does not arrive at seeds 3–5; the longer window raises the exiters' speed in the weave lane by 1–2 m/s (mean entry speed 19.5 against 17.6 m/s) and lane 0's queue is not longer (its first 60 m below 3 m/s in 1 of 105 10-s bins against 2 of 107). The collision is not the window's: it is the exit-side lead-gap acceptance without the leader's speed — the speed-aware guard the short-section decision named as the next derivation, now with two seeded traces — and belongs with the vacate-window review (WP-49), not here.
3. *The entrance peak is seed noise around the same pair.* At 4,049 veh/h the target lane has little spare capacity, so the bound skips 149 / 155 / 150 through vehicles against 87 / 100 / 83 and fewer vacate (60 / 41 / 46 against 73 / 86 / 62); seed 3 gives up 2 of 45 at both windows (4.4 %, the pair) with lane 1's last 60 m at 2.9 m/s in one minute against 4.4, 252 deferred against 123, 7 releases against 2; seeds 4 / 5 give up 0 / 0 against 1 / 0 with lane 1 never below 15.7 m/s against 6.2 / 12.7. The fleet defaults pass every criterion at both demand points and all three seeds at 500 m as at 150 m (seed 3 at the exit peak gives up 1 of 281, 0.4 %).
4. *The 2·L cap is not justified.* The one variant measured is `vacate_ahead_m` capped at twice the section's length (271.4 m; the approach-length cap would be inert here, the approach being 583 m). It is worse than *both* windows at one seed per fleet: on the corridor's fleet at the exit peak seed 5 gives up **8 of 274** (2.9 %, against 4 at either window) with 548 deferred, 3 unfinished and a collision of the same trace; on the fleet defaults at the entrance peak seed 5 locks lane 1's last 60 m at **1.6 m/s** for two minutes with 716 deferred forced steps and 46 pair releases (0 / 2 / 0 at either window), which would turn a green case of `test_short_section_at_corridor_flows` red. Where it is better (corridor seed 3 at the entrance peak: 0 of 45, 8.8 m/s) the 500 m window's seeds 4 / 5 are as good, so it is the seed that moves, not the rule. Nothing in the table ties the window's effect to the section's length: the window acts on the approach's through traffic, the section's length on the crossing pair, and the two do not interact — the short-section study's conclusion, now measured at both windows.

**Recommendation.** `vacate_ahead_m` stays 500 m independent of the section's length; no section-length-dependent window is added, and the 2·L cap's numbers above are its rejection. What the re-measurement hands to the vacate-window review (WP-49) is the exit-side acceptance: a lead-gap term that reads the leader's speed (the changer's braking distance to a slower leader, as `_weave_vacate_gap_ok` already does for the follower in the gap-conditioned form), with the two traces above (500 m seed 4 at 600.5 s, 271 m seed 5 at 717.5 s) as its regression cases. The Ruth St markers are re-recorded with the 500 m numbers: the exit-peak marks stay strict (the give-ups are within 2 % at every seed now; what fails is lane 1 at seeds 3 and 5 and the collision at seed 4), the entrance-peak marks stay non-strict (seed 3 pays the pair, seeds 4 / 5 pass).

**Bookkeeping.** `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py` (the marks' reasons and the class docstring; no new test); this section; no runner or fixture change. Session artifacts (the 36-run grid, the trace script) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, speed-aware acceptance): the weave's gap acceptance reads the other party's speed — both Ruth St collisions gone, a third of the same family closed on the guard's follower side, no lock, later exits

**Why.** The short-section re-measurement above handed over two seeded collisions of the exit-side acceptance (Ruth St, corridor fleet, exit peak: seed 4 at the 500 m window, t = 600.5 s on `102_0` at 35.5 m; seed 5 at the 271.4 m window, t = 717.5 s at 39.2 m). Both reproduce at 8503d7c to the step and the metre (`TestExitSideAcceptance`, `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py`). The acceptance's leader side was the movement's time gap at the changer's own speed, `g_lead ≥ s0 + accept · v_c` (16 m for the 23 m/s exiter), and the forced guard's two sides the closing distance over that time gap, `s0 + accept · Δv⁺`; neither reads whether the party behind can brake for the party ahead.

**The rule (ships; `microsim.runner._weave_brake_gap`, `_weave_lead_gap_min`, `_weave_force_gap_ok`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2).** The brake distance of the party behind to the speed of the party ahead at its own comfortable deceleration `b` (SUMO's `decel`, the calibrated draw), the party ahead holding its speed: `Δv⁺² / (2·b)`. Three checks change. (1) The acceptance's leader side, both movements: `g_lead ≥ max(s0 + accept · v_c, s0 + (v_c − v_L)⁺²/(2·b_c))` — 147 m for the trace, against 27 m offered. (2) The guard's leader side: `g_lead > max(s0 + accept · Δv⁺, s0 + Δv⁺²/(2·b_c))`, so forcing cannot command a change onto a slower leader that the acceptance refuses. (3) The guard's follower side: `g_foll > max(s0 + accept · Δv⁺, Δv⁺²/(2·b_F))` at the follower's `decel` (no `s0` term: the reported follower gap already excludes the follower's `minGap`), released pairs included — the release drops the changer's `s0` floor, not the follower's braking. The acceptance's follower side (the time gap plus the follower's IDM absorption within its `b`) is unchanged; so are the vacate rule, the gap choice, the cooperation, the exit priority and the give-up. No parameter, hash-neutral. Refused at speed, the exiter eases in its lane towards the queue's speed (`_weave_cooperate`, as before) and drops in once the gap it is offered is one it can brake for.

**Why the third check.** The leader-side form alone (variant D below) removed both Ruth St collisions and left the golden, the moderate fixture and the fleet defaults byte-identical — and collided once elsewhere: `weave_th52.osm` at the corridor's demand, seed 5, t = 978.5 s at the gore's end (`102_0`, 303 m). The trace (`_weave_force_gap_ok` and `changeLane` wrapped; session record): exiter v01013, a released pair partner (`s0 = 0`) at 0.54 m/s, forced into lane 0 at 977.5 s with 10.7 m to follower v01934 at 16.13 m/s — the closing-speed bound asked `0.6 · 15.6 = 9.35 m`, the follower needed 73 m at its `b` — and was hit a second later after the follower braked at −9 m/s². The same defect on the guard's other side; it is at 8503d7c too, where seed 5 does not happen to roll it. With the follower term the pair release keeps its regime (both below the creep speed, both brake gaps near zero) and loses the fast follower.

**Variants measured and rejected (the same 30-run grid: seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness).** A: the changer's IDM desired gap `s*(v_c, v_c − v_L)` (`_idm_desired_gap`, the changer's own `T`, `a`, `b`, `s0`; the time gap as the floor) as the leader bound of the acceptance and the guard — the form the block's brief named. It is the *no-braking* gap (`s0 + v·T` at equal speeds: 35 m at 23 m/s against the 0.6 s time gap's 16 m; 263 m for the trace): it removed the collisions and locked every T.H.52 fixture. B: the gap at which the changer's IDM towards the leader asks exactly −b, `s*/√(1 + b/a − (v/v0)⁴)` (153 m for the trace, ≈ 20 m at equal speeds at 23 m/s), leader side only: no collision anywhere, but IDM's kinematic term `v·Δv/(2√(ab))` is conservative at a weave's moderate closing rates (32 m for 15 → 10 m/s where the brake distance is 10 m), so the exiters ease in lane 1 longer and the gore slows (corridor demand seed 3: 3.4 m/s in two minutes against 6.5). C: B on both sides of the guard — the follower's IDM −b gap: locks the short section (Ruth St entrance peak: lane 1 at 1.2–1.5 m/s in 8–16 minutes, 1,288–3,910 deferred) and moves the golden's mean travel time 69.3 → 73.8 s. D: the kinematic form on the leader side alone — the seed-5 collision above. E: D plus the follower side plus a *patient* give-up (a halted exiter whose gap follower is still held is not rerouted that step): the held follower at rest behind a halted exiter is the abreast-pair state the release exists for, and waiting on it is the pre-release lock — Ruth St exit peak at 0.0 m/s for the whole run, 19,253 deferred, 24 unfinished. The shipped form is D plus the follower side.

**Reading.**

1. *The collisions vanish and nothing locks.* No collision on any of the 30 runs (8503d7c: two on the Ruth St corridor fleet); the driven vehicles unfinished stay at 0–6 of 230–500 per run, lane 1 at the gore never reads a lock's 0.0 m/s, and the entrance departs 73 of 73 / 128 of 128 on Ruth St at every seed.
2. *The exiters change later, and more of them reach the gore's end still owing the change.* On the Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak the forced changes go 9 / 9 / 15 → 40 / 25 / 6, the deferred steps 252 / 111 / 275 → 1,168 / 402 / 132, the releases 2 / 1 / 2 → 32 / 6 / 0, and the give-ups 4 / 2 / 4 → 9 / 1 / 4 of 290 / 280 / 274 (3.1 / 0.4 / 1.5 %; seed 3 leaves the 2 % threshold again). The mechanism is the one the rule intends: an exiter at 20–23 m/s no longer drops in behind the auxiliary lane's queue at a 16 m gap; it eases in lane 1 behind the queue's tail and drops in near its speed, so the auxiliary lane's queue is joined from behind at the price of lane 1's speed at the gore (2.8 / 4.2 / 6.7 m/s at the minimum against 4.6 / 6.5 / 3.6) — and seed 5, which failed on lane 1 before, now meets every exit-side criterion and loses its strict marker (seeds 3 / 4 keep theirs: the give-ups at 3, lane 1 at both). At the entrance peak the same fleet improves at seed 3 (2 of 45 given up → 0, lane 1 2.9 → 6.3 m/s, 252 → 138 deferred) and pays at seeds 4 / 5 (1 of 41 given up; one minute at 3.6 m/s). The fleet defaults are unchanged within one give-up (seed 4 at the exit peak: 1 of 282) and the moderate fixture byte-identical.
3. *T.H.52.* At the corridor's demand seed 3 reads 3.8 m/s in one minute at the gore (was 6.5 at the minimum; 292 of 299 exit, 1 given up), seed 4 is unchanged (11.9 m/s, 308 of 317) and seed 5's stall shortens by a minute (1.7 m/s in two minutes, was three; 5 of 308 given up, was 3); the exit-side test at seed 3 gains a non-strict marker. At capacity the entrance departs 395 / 401 / 373 against 398 / 365 / 389, one exit is given up per seed (was none) and the no-lock pins hold at both seeds — seed 4 passes the 80 % pin on macOS again (401), seed 5 by one vehicle (373 against 372.8) — with the `n_missed` pin moved from 0 to at most 1: the exit given up is a vehicle halted at the gore's end whose lane-0 follower could not brake for it, the forced change that at the corridor's demand ended in the seed-5 collision. On the two-entrance fixtures the section side keeps passing on the fleet defaults at seeds 4 / 5 and slips to 3.7 m/s in one minute at seed 3; E1's head is untouched (205 / 206 / 246 → 216 / 210 / 256 of 360 on the defaults, 272 / 258 / 248 → 253 / 256 / 253 on the corridor fleet); both strict markers stay.
4. *The golden moves by the follower term.* Variant D (the leader side alone) reproduces the golden bit for bit — no changer on `weave.osm` closes on a slower leader beyond the time gap — and the follower side binds six vehicle-steps of the forced guard: mean travel time 69.345 → 69.500 s, σ_v spatial 3.848 → 3.940 m/s, fuel 90.67 → 90.54 ml/veh-km, the counts unchanged. Regenerated.

**What this hands on.** The exits given up rise where the auxiliary lane queues, because a halted exiter at the gore's end is rerouted on the first step no request is possible — under the speed-aware guard that step now comes while its held follower is still braking towards it, a transient of two or three seconds. The patient form (E) shows the naive fix locks; a bounded patience (wait while the held follower's speed is still falling, give up when it has come to rest and the guard still refuses) is the next thing to measure, together with the corridor round these rules were derived for (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds: `n_missed_exit`, `n_forced_deferred`, the first standstill's position, and the collision count that was the slice's one).

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_brake_gap` new, `_weave_lead_gap_min` new, `_weave_force_gap_ok` two optional decelerations, the acceptance in `_weave_step`); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_weave_short_section.py` (`TestExitSideAcceptance`, the marks' reasons); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveForceGapGuard::test_speed_aware_guard` and `::test_acceptance_refuses_a_fast_changer_behind_a_slow_leader`, the exit-priority scenario re-recorded — a halted exiter's forced request now equals its accepted one on the follower side, so the "guard alone" case is a give-up and a 14 m follower the accepted one — the marks and pins above); `tests/golden/merge_weave.json`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2; this section. Session artifacts (the measurement harness, the six grids, the seed-5 trace) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-52, bounded give-up patience): a halted exiter waits while its follower is still braking — measured on the same grid, it does not help, because the give-up is not the transient it waits for; shipped off (`exit_giveup_patience_s` = 0)

**Why.** The speed-aware acceptance above handed on one thing: the exits given up rise where the auxiliary lane queues, and the reading was that a halted exiter at the gore's end is rerouted on the first step no request is possible, which under the speed-aware guard now comes while its held follower is still braking towards the gap — a transient of two or three seconds. The naive patient form (variant E above: never give up while the follower is held) locked the short section. The task was a *bounded* patience: wait while the refusal is that transient, give up the moment it is not, never lock.

**The rule (implemented; `microsim.runner._weave_giveup_patient`, `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exit_giveup_patience_s"]`, `weave_sections[i].n_giveup_waited`; docs/CONTRACTS.md §2).** On a step on which a halted exiter within `exit_giveup_m` can request no change (neither the acceptance nor the forced guard passes), it is kept instead of given up when all of: (1) the auxiliary-lane follower reported this step is the one reported on the previous step (`st["foll_prev"]`; the speed history is one vehicle's), (2) it is behind the exiter — a positive reported gap; a vehicle overlapping the exiter is beside it, not braking towards a gap behind it, (3) its speed fell since the previous step by more than `WEAVE_GIVEUP_DECEL_TOL_MS2` = 0.1 m/s² times the step (SUMO's speeds settle at an equilibrium within hundredths of a m/s per step; one tenth of the smallest comfortable deceleration of interest, not fitted), (4) it is not at rest (`HALTING_SPEED_MS`), and (5) the wait since the first refused step (`st["giveup_since"]`, set then and never renewed) is shorter than the bound — `exit_giveup_patience_s` or the follower's braking time to rest at its own `decel` from its speed on that first step, `v_F / b_F`, whichever is shorter, so a follower already crawling earns no patience. Any other state gives up at once as before; the wait is a deferred forced request (`n_forced_deferred`) and is counted in `n_giveup_waited` (vehicle-steps). `0` never waits and is the default, for the reasons below; the key is hash-neutral unless set. The bound's default candidate was 10 s: the braking time at the fleet's `b` = 1.67 m/s² from the 16 m/s of the lane-0 follower in the seed-5 collision trace above (9.6 s) — the bound is a safety net, the deceleration condition is what ends the wait.

**Variants measured (the same 30-run grid as the speed-aware acceptance — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness; the 20f9fcb rows re-run in this session and identical to the section above).** A: the rule as stated at 10 s. B: A without the positive-gap term (2), the first form implemented — measured at 5, 10 and 20 s, which give identical runs except at T.H.52 capacity seed 3 (16 / 13 / 13 vehicle-steps waited): the bound binds nowhere else, the deceleration condition ends every wait within a few steps. C: the bound alone at 10 s, no follower condition but a follower reported — the naive form of E, capped.
