*Table — golden `merge_weave` under each variant (the hash differs only because the override is set; the default's run is the 20f9fcb one).*

| variant | hash | mean TT | p90 TT | σ_v | VMT | VHT | fuel | throughput | in / out / forced / deferred | exits | coll. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20f9fcb (patience 0, **ships**) | 436cd4ec9e5d | 69.500 | 84.126 | 3.940 / 3.449 | 169.649 | 1.7492 | 90.54 | 1680.0 | 14 / 19 / 2 / 6 | 33 | 0 |
| A: patience 10 s | 3d4b8d04580a | 69.500 | 84.126 | 3.940 / 3.449 | 169.649 | 1.7492 | 90.54 | 1680.0 | 14 / 19 / 2 / 6 | 33 | 0 |
| B: A without the positive-gap term | 3d4b8d04580a | 69.500 | 84.126 | 3.940 / 3.449 | 169.649 | 1.7492 | 90.54 | 1680.0 | 14 / 19 / 2 / 6 | 33 | 0 |
| C: bound only, 10 s | 3d4b8d04580a | 69.500 | 84.126 | 3.940 / 3.449 | 169.649 | 1.7492 | 90.54 | 1680.0 | 14 / 19 / 2 / 6 | 33 | 0 |

**Reading.**

1. *The give-ups do not fall.* Over the 30 runs: 44 given up at 20f9fcb, 48 under A, 45 under B, 33 under C; minutes of lane 1's last 60 m at or below 5 m/s (Ruth St and T.H.52 rows) 18 / 26 / 25 / 53; driven vehicles unfinished 45 / 42 / 43 / 72; forced changes deferred 5,439 / 6,128 / 9,389 / 23,541; no collision under any. A and B wait 50 and 84 vehicle-steps in total — the wait is as short as intended (1–16 per run) — and neither locks; but the runs they touch read worse more often than better: under A the T.H.52 capacity entrance at seed 4 departs 358 of 466 (401 before; the no-lock test's 80 % pin is 372.8, so A at 10 s would fail it), lane 1 there 4.7 m/s in one minute against 10.7; T.H.52 capacity seed 3 gives up 4 of 326 against 1 of 319; the two-entrance defaults at seed 5 give up 4 of 312 against 1 of 308 and at seed 4 exit 287 of 308 against 301 of 307 (lane 1 1.3 m/s); Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 5, gives up 8 of 274 against 4 (lane 1 3.9 m/s in two minutes against 6.7 in none). Its gains are the 271 m window at seed 5 (6 of 274 against 8, lane 1 3.3 against 2.1), T.H.52 corridor demand seed 5 (3 of 299 against 5 of 308, 0 unfinished against 6) and two-entrance defaults seed 3 (1 of 301 against 3). B is the same picture with a different scatter — at the 271 m window it reads 0.0 m/s for five minutes, 236 reached against 274 (a queue upstream), 4,209 deferred — its one-step waits in the overlap state (a lane-0 vehicle beside the exiter, crawling and slowing) hold the exiter a step or two longer for nothing. C, the capped naive form, gives up fewer (33) at the price of holding lane 1 for up to 10 s each time: 841 vehicle-steps waited, 18 of 52 waiters rescued, and the 271 m window at seed 5 locks as E did — lane 1 at 0.0 m/s for twelve minutes, 13,366 deferred, 20 unfinished, the entrance 63 of 73 — so a bound alone is not a bound on the lock, only on its onset.
2. *Why: the give-up is not the transient.* A per-step trace of every give-up on the 20f9fcb grid (the acceptance's two sides, both gaps and both speeds at the refused step; session record) classes the 44 as: **34** with a lane-0 vehicle overlapping the halted exiter (a negative reported gap on one side — the abreast state at the gore's end that the short-section section described, the queue creeping past a halted exiter, or two halted vehicles under `s0` either side); **6** with the follower behind at a positive gap and moving at 1 m/s or more — the braking transient the rule was written for; **3** refused on the leader side without overlap (the lane-0 vehicle ahead a metre or two off at 7–11 m/s); **1** with the follower crawling below 1 m/s at a gap under the bound. So the population the patience can act on is 6 of 44, and under A those 6 were waited on and given up anyway: the follower did not stop behind the exiter, it drove up alongside it. The trace of one (T.H.52 capacity, seed 3, exiter v00425, refused from t = 431.0 s with 41.7 m to follower v00328 at 11.54 m/s) shows the mechanism: v00328 *was* the exiter's chosen gap and *was* under the cooperation's hold on every step of the wait (commanded at its own `b`, −1.377 m/s²; its own IDM leader 66–98 m ahead, irrelevant), and it closed to −0.4 m in six seconds because stopping at `b` from 11.54 m/s needs 48.4 m and the hold caught it with 41.7. The cooperation cannot command a brake beyond `b_F` by design and the guard asks for exactly that brake gap, so the refusal was never a transient: the follower slides alongside whatever the exiter does, and the exiter is then in the overlap state and given up. The one rescue on the grid (Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271 m window, seed 5, exiter v00773 at t = 1022–1024.5 s) is the same geometry with room: 47.5 m offered to a follower at 11.59 m/s needing 40.2 m at `b` = 1.67; it braked to 8.17 m/s in 1.5 s and the change was accepted, not forced. That is the case the rule serves, and it is one in thirty runs.
3. *What this says about the give-ups.* The exits given up at the gore's end are two populations, neither of which a wait can move: the abreast state (34 of 44), which is the crossing pair the pair release exists for and the queue creeping past a halted exiter that nothing asks to yield; and the follower caught inside its brake distance (6 of 44), which is exactly the vehicle the speed-aware guard refuses to force the exiter in front of — a rescue there is a collision risk, the seed-5 collision of the section above. The lever, if there is one, is upstream of the halt: catching the follower before it is inside its brake distance (the exit priority begins only when the forced change is due, `force_after_s` after entering the zone; a hold from zone entry was measured and rejected on the entrance in the exit-side derivation), or the exiter not arriving at the gore's end at a halt at all.
4. *The golden is untouched.* The default is the previous behaviour, so `merge_weave` reproduces 20f9fcb bit for bit (the summary carries none of the weave meta's new key); set to 10 s the golden's metrics are identical too (no give-up occurs on `weave.osm`) and only the hash moves, because the override is set.

**What this hands on.** The negative result narrows the exit-side question to the two populations above. For the abreast state, the measure is whether the queue vehicle beside a halted exiter should yield — it is the gap the priority names, but the priority's hold reaches only the follower of the chosen gap, and at the gore's end the vehicle beside the exiter is the leader of that gap, driven by nothing; a rule that asks the vehicle *beside* a halted, due exiter to hold (rather than creep past) is the next thing to derive and measure, against the same lock risk as E. For the follower caught inside its brake distance, the measure is the hold's start: the distance at which the cooperation first catches the follower against its brake distance at `b`, per give-up, from the trace — if the followers are caught late because the priority is due late, the priority's timing is the lever; if they arrive at 11–16 m/s into a gap that the queue ahead has already closed, nothing at the section can help and the give-up is honest. Both belong with the corridor round (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds) the previous section named, which still has not been run since 20f9fcb.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`exit_giveup_patience_s`, default 0, docstring); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`WEAVE_GIVEUP_DECEL_TOL_MS2`, `_weave_giveup_patient` new, the give-up branch of `_weave_step`, `foll_prev` / `giveup_since` in the vehicle state, `n_giveup_waited` in the section state and `_weave_meta`); `packages/api/api/schemas.py` (`WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut.n_giveup_waited`); `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` (`WEAVE_FIELDS`); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveGiveupPatience`, the fake state and the counters test); `tests/test_api/test_runs_merge_diagnostics.py`, `tests/test_scripts/test_corridor_sweep.py` (the key lists); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the 33-key list, the WP-52 paragraph, the API section); CHANGELOG.md; this section. No golden change. The dashboard's TypeScript types list neither `n_missed_exit` nor this key (the API marks both "not shown by the dashboard"), so `frontend/` is untouched. Session artifacts (the harness, the four grids, the give-up traces) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-53, the abreast state): the vehicle beside a halted exiter is, 24 times in 34, an entrant halted at the end of its own lane — the crossing pair at the lane ends, which no local rule resolves; a bounded wait for the vehicle that is sliding past rescues the rest and costs more than it returns; shipped off (`exit_abreast_patience_s` = 0)

**Why.** WP-52 above handed on the abreast state: of the 44 fixture give-ups at 6497b2d, 34 had a lane-0 vehicle overlapping the halted exiter, and its reading was "the queue creeping past a halted exiter that nothing asks to yield" — the vehicle beside the exiter is the leader of the gap the exit priority names, and the priority's hold reaches only that gap's follower. The task was to derive a rule for that vehicle: a hold (commanded through the same cooperation mechanism, at its own `b`, SUMO's safety on), or a yield, bounded against the lock that WP-51's variant E and WP-52's variant C showed, never on a driven vehicle, never a chain.

**The trace first (session harness: `changeTarget` wrapped, every give-up's geometry read from libsumo on the give-up step — both reported neighbours with their speeds, the overlapping vehicle's position, length, leader and whether the runner drives it, the exiter's committed follower, its time in the zone).** The 44 give-ups reproduce to the vehicle and the step. They class as:

| class (the overlapping vehicle, or the refusal) | 6497b2d | A: abreast 10 s | B: abreast 10 s + braking patience 10 s |
|---|---|---|---|
| a *driven entrant* halted at the end of the auxiliary lane beside the exiter (the crossing pair at the lane ends) | **24** | 24 | 27 |
| an exit-bound queue vehicle sliding past the exiter's rear, moving (0.7–7.3 m/s, its own leader moving ahead of it) | **10** | 1 | 5 |
| an exit-bound vehicle standing beside the exiter | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| no overlap; the follower behind at 1 m/s or more, inside its brake distance | 9 | 18 | 6 |
| no overlap; the follower crawling below 1 m/s | 1 | 3 | 4 |
| total | 44 | 46 | 44 |

WP-52's 34 / 6 / 3 / 1 is the same population read coarser (its 34 "overlap" is the 24 + 10; its 6 brake-distance and 3 leader-side refusals are the 9 with a follower at 1 m/s or more behind). The decisive fact is the 24: the vehicle beside the halted exiter is, in 24 of the 34, an *entrant* — `ws["veh"]` drives it with `dir = +1`, it has no leader, it is at 0–0.4 m/s and coming to rest at the end of the auxiliary lane, whose only continuation is the exit its route does not take. Each of the pair owes the change into the other's lane; each is at the end of its lane; each overlaps the other, so neither can change across the other and neither can move on. The priority did nothing for the exiter because the entrant was already beside it when the priority became due (the pair crawled abreast through the zone; the entrant was never the follower of the exiter's gap, so the hold never reached it), and the entrant's own easing (the rear one of an abreast pair drops back at `−b`) is inert at 0.3 m/s at a lane end. The 10 are a different geometry: most often the exiter's own held follower (WP-52's brake-distance case, caught inside its brake distance at `b`) that slid past the exiter's rear at 0.7–7 m/s and, once past it, is released from the hold (it is no longer a candidate follower) and accelerates towards its moving leader; at its speed on the give-up step it clears the exiter's front — `(len_c + 2·s0_c + g_F + s0_A + len_A) / v_A` — in 1.7–8 s in 8 of the 10 (the other two are already on the internal lane beyond the section edge, at 6.9 m/s). The follower of the gap behind it is held by the priority, so the gap the exiter needs is the one that opens as the sliding vehicle clears.

**Two forms, derived.** *Hold* (H): the auxiliary-lane vehicle abreast of a halted, due exiter is commanded to stop, at its own `b` through `_weave_command` (mode unchanged, SUMO's safety on), bounded per pair, never a driven vehicle (entrant or exiter), never one already under a hold this step. It cannot open anything: a vehicle beside the exiter that stops is the crossing pair, and one sliding past cannot stop inside its brake distance — the hold already asks `−b`. It was implemented in the session harness only and measured for the record. *Yield* (A, the rule implemented; `microsim.runner._weave_giveup_abreast`, `_weave_abreast_clear_m`, `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exit_abreast_patience_s"]`): on a step on which a halted exiter within `exit_giveup_m` can request no change, and a vehicle is beside it — reported on the leader side under the acceptance's floor `s0_c + accept · v_c` (a vehicle just clear of the overlap but inside the floor is still clearing) or on the follower side with a negative gap — the exiter is kept instead of given up when that vehicle is not a driven entrant of the section (its lane ends at the gore: it never clears), is moving (`HALTING_SPEED_MS`), and would clear the floor at its current speed within the budget left, `exit_abreast_patience_s` less the time since the first refused step (`st["giveup_since"]`, the one budget WP-52's patience uses, never renewed). A halted vehicle beside the exiter, a slower one, or an exhausted budget gives up at once; `0` never waits; the wait is a deferred forced request and is counted in `n_giveup_waited` with WP-52's (one counter; the key list stays at 33). The distance reads the reported gaps with SUMO's conventions (a leader's gap less the ego's `minGap`, a follower's less its own; verified on the trace: `g_lead = −7.0` for an entrant 0.07 m ahead of the exiter's front with `s0_c = 2.07`). The bound's candidate was 10 s, WP-52's; it is a safety net, the clearing condition ends the wait. Nothing new is held: the only hold is the priority's, on the gap's follower.

**Variants measured (the same grid as WP-51/52 — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden, 29 runs; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness; the 6497b2d rows re-run in this session and identical to WP-52's).** A at 5, 10 and 20 s; B: A at 10 s with WP-52's braking patience at 10 s (the sliding vehicle *is* the braking follower a few seconds earlier, so the two waits chain: brake, slide past, clear); H at 10 s beside A at 10 s (the harness form above). A first form of B crashed on six runs — the braking patience read the follower's speed on the first refused step from a key only it wrote, and the abreast wait had opened the budget with no follower behind (`KeyError`); fixed (`_weave_giveup_patient` records the speed on the first step it reads one) and pinned (`TestWeaveAbreastPatience::test_the_budget_opened_by_the_abreast_wait_is_read_by_the_braking_patience`), B re-run.

**Reading.**

1. *Over the grid.* Give-ups 44 (6497b2d) / 47 (A 5 s) / 46 (A 10 s) / 46 (A 20 s) / 44 (B) / 46 (H); exits 5,988 / 5,974 / 5,981 / 5,981 / 5,972 / 5,981; minutes of lane 1's last 60 m at or below 5 m/s over the Ruth St and T.H.52 rows 14 / 17 / 16 / 16 / 20 / 16; forced changes deferred 5,439 / 6,035 / 5,883 / 5,883 / 6,281 / 5,819; vehicle-steps waited 0 / 44 / 53 / 53 / 175 / 45; no collision, no lock (nothing reads 0.0 m/s, the entrances depart 73 of 73 and 128 of 128 on Ruth St at every seed). The 20 s runs are the 10 s runs byte for byte: the bound never binds above 10 s, the clearing condition ends every wait; at 5 s two more exiters are refused the wait and given up.
2. *A does what it was written for and the give-ups still rise.* The sliding-vehicle give-ups go 10 → 1 and the exiters the trace named are rescued (Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 3: v00769 waits 4 s for v01001 to clear and drops in — 273 → 280 of 290 exited, 2 → 1 unfinished, 1,168 → 1,016 deferred; seed 5 gives up 3 of 274 against 4; T.H.52 corridor demand seed 5, 4 of 308 against 5). By vehicle, of A's 46 give-ups 21 are the same halted entrant as at 6497b2d, 8 the same brake-distance refusal, and **5 are exiters that were the sliding-vehicle class and are now the brake-distance class**: the exiter waited, the vehicle beside it cleared, and the follower of the gap behind it — held by the priority, but caught inside its brake distance at `b` as WP-52 traced — arrived at 4–13 m/s inside the gap and the guard refused it. The wait also holds lane 1 for its duration, and where lane 1 is the section's bottleneck the cost shows: T.H.52 at capacity seed 4 departs 386 of 466 against 401 (the 80 % pin is 372.8, so it passes) with lane 1 at the gore at 2.7 m/s for three minutes against 10.7 in none, 4 of 379 given up against 1, 519 deferred against 71; the two-entrance defaults at seed 3 depart 394 of 470 against 417 and exit 284 of 298 against 294 of 301. Three new entrant-class give-ups (v00856, v00663, v00911 at 6497b2d were entrant-class give-ups of other seeds' sequences) are the altered sequence, not the rule.
3. *B chains the two waits and pays more.* With the braking patience on beside it the exiter waits from the follower's braking through its slide to its clearing: 175 vehicle-steps over the grid, up to 25 per run, and the give-ups are back at 44 — with 16 fewer exits, 20 lane-1 minutes at or below 5 m/s against 14, 6,281 deferred against 5,439, and the T.H.52 capacity entrance at seed 5 at **372 of 466**, one vehicle under the 80 % pin (372.8), with 8 driven vehicles unfinished against 3; seed 3 at 390 against 395 with lane 1 at 2.9 m/s for three minutes; seed 4 at 398 against 401 (with 384 of 391 exited against 373 of 379 — B's one clear gain, and lane 1 at 3.5 m/s for two minutes against 10.7 in none); Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak seed 5 gives up 6 of 274 against 4 (512 deferred against 132), the fleet defaults at seed 4 2 of 282 against 1. The classes under B — 27 entrant, 5 sliding, 2 standing, 6 brake-distance, 4 crawling — say what the chained wait does: fewer brake-distance refusals (the follower is waited through its braking) and more exiters at the lane end when the entrant arrives beside them.
4. *H is inert, as derived.* Over the whole grid the hold binds on one vehicle-step (Ruth St corridor fleet, exit peak, seed 3): a vehicle beside a halted due exiter under the creep speed that is neither driven nor already held is not there to hold — the abreast vehicle is an entrant (driven, excluded by construction) or the sliding vehicle above the creep speed. H's rows are A's rows.
5. *The crossing pair is the population, and it is not a wait's.* 24 of 44 at 6497b2d, 24 under A, 27 under B: an entrant halted at the end of the auxiliary lane beside an exiter halted at the end of lane 1. No rule at the gore's end resolves it — neither can move on (SUMO holds each at the end of a lane its route does not continue on), neither can change across the other (the overlap is complete), and a hold on either is the state itself. The exiter's reroute is the one local resolution, and it is what frees the entrant too (it forces into lane 1 behind the rerouted exiter). Whether the pair should form is an upstream question: the priority's hold reaches the entrant only while it is behind the exiter's rear, and at the crawl the pair is abreast before the priority is due (4 s after zone entry; from zone entry it was measured and rejected on the entrance in the exit-side derivation).
6. *The golden is untouched.* The default is the previous behaviour, so `merge_weave` reproduces 6497b2d bit for bit (`test_golden_summary_reproduced[merge_weave]` passes; the summary carries no new key); set, the metrics are identical on every variant and only the hash moves.

**What this hands on.** The exit-side give-ups at the gore's end are now three named populations with numbers: the crossing pair at the lane ends (24 of 44 — the entrant and the exiter each at the end of its own lane, abreast), the follower caught inside its brake distance (9), and the sliding vehicle (10, rescuable by the wait above at a cost the grid does not repay). The two waits (WP-52's and this one) are measured options, both off. The lever for the crossing pair is upstream of the halt, and it is the entrant's: an entrant in the forced zone abreast of a *due* exiter could be asked to drop behind the exiter's rear while both still move (the priority's hold, extended to the vehicle beside the exiter, not only to the follower of the gap behind it) — that is the seventh derivation's geometry from the other side, and it must be measured against the entrance numbers that rejected the earlier priority (407 / 416 / 402 of 466). The other lever is the one WP-52 named for the brake-distance population: the hold's start, per give-up, from the trace. Both belong with the corridor round (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds) that neither WP-52 nor this package has run.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`exit_abreast_patience_s`, default 0, docstring); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_abreast_clear_m` new, `_weave_giveup_abreast` new, the give-up branch of `_weave_step`, `_weave_giveup_patient` reads the budget either patience opened); `packages/api/api/schemas.py` and `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` (`n_giveup_waited`'s description covers both waits; no key added, the contract stays at 33 keys); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveAbreastPatience`, the defaults pin); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the WP-53 paragraph, the key list's note, the API paragraph); CHANGELOG.md; this section. No golden change; `frontend/` untouched. Session artifacts (the harness with the trace hook and the H form, the six grids, the six give-up traces, the classification) not committed.


## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-54, the crossing pair): traced per pair, it forms with both vehicles braking for their own lane ends, and it persists because the halted entrant is not freed by the give-up — the exiter yields to it (ships); the entrant's own yield, the rule the package named, is feasible in 2 of 24 pairs and shipped off

**Why.** WP-53 above classed 24 of the 44 fixture give-ups as the crossing pair at the lane ends — an exit-bound changer halted at the end of lane 1 beside a driven entrant halted at the end of the auxiliary lane, each owing the change into the other's lane — and handed on that the pair is decided upstream and by the entrant: an entrant in the forced zone abreast of a *due* exiter could be asked to drop behind the exiter's rear while both still move, the exit priority's hold extended to the vehicle beside the exiter, to be measured against the entrance numbers that rejected the priority from zone entry (407 / 416 / 402 of 466). This package derives that rule, traces the 24 pairs from the exiter's zone entry to see whether it can bind, measures it, and measures the symmetric decision the trace points at instead.

**The trace (session harness: every in-zone exiter's auxiliary-lane neighbourhood logged after each step with the step's actual `slowDown` / `changeLane` calls; the 24 give-ups of WP-53 reproduce to the vehicle and the step at 6e7757e).** For each pair, the entrant's front relative to the exiter's (`dx`, positive ahead), both speeds, both remaining lane lengths and both `b` at the step the priority becomes due (4 s into the zone), and the *margin* of the rule as derived — whether the entrant can come to rest at its own `b` behind where the exiter's rear will be at the latest, the lane end: `rem_E − len_X − s0_E − v_E² / (2·b_E)`.

| geometry at the due moment | pairs (X ← E; fixture, seed) | dx [m] | v_X / v_E [m/s] | b_E [m/s²] | margin [m] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) the entrant already halted at its lane end — the priority became due with the entrant standing there | v01384 (Ruth St cf entr 4); v00518, v00523 ← v00981 (Ruth St cf exit 3); v00272, v00287 ← v01701 (T.H.52 cd 5); v00645, v00646, v00663 ← v00991 (Ruth St cf exit 5, 271 m) — **8** | +11.0 … +14.9 | 0.6–8.2 / 0.0–0.4 | 0.8–1.6 | −7.5 … −8.3 |
| (b) abreast at speed parity, both braking for their own lane ends (the exiter for the end of lane 1, which its route leaves; the entrant for the end of the auxiliary lane) at 0.9–2.7 m/s², more than the entrant's `b` in every case but one | v00069 ← v00947, v00070 ← v00947 (cf exit 3); v00366 ← v00970 (cf exit 4); v00906 ← v01012 (fd exit 3); v00245 ← v01701 (T.H.52 cd 5); v01400 ← v01823 (two-entrance cf 4); v00198 ← v00956 (cf exit 5, X at 15.6 m/s with 3.7 m left) — **7** | −4.1 … +8.4 | 7–11 / 6.7–8.9 (one at 15.6 / 2.6) | 0.59–2.26 | −0.7 … −21.1 |
| (c) the entrant closing from behind at 10–14 m/s, *already held at `−b_E`* as the follower of the exiter's gap (the priority's hold: `sl` recorded every step) and sliding past because `b_E` cannot shed the closing speed inside its lane (stop distance 37–128 m against 38–48 m of lane) | v00505 ← v00981, v00856 ← v01008, v00746 ← v00999 (cf exit 3); v00237 ← v00960 (cf exit 5); v00158 ← v01684 (two-entrance cf 4); v00355 ← v00969, v00636 ← v00991 (cf exit 5, 271 m) — **7** | −16.2 … −3.9 | 8–10 / 10–14 | 0.53–1.43 | −5.3 … −94.8 |
| (d) the entrant ahead and slowing, the exiter faster and unable to ease behind it at its `b` (`_weave_easing_ok` refuses: 2.25 m/s² needed against 0.81), the entrant able to stop short at its `b` | v00344 ← v00968 (cf exit 3); v00911 ← v01012 (cf exit 5) — **2** | +9.6, −1.4 | 11.7 / 7.4; 10.0 / 6.7 | 3.08, 2.97 | **+1.4, +1.4** |

Three things the table says. (1) The pair *forms* in (b)–(d) as the sixth and seventh derivations described from the other side, and the rule as derived cannot break it: at the due moment the entrant is braking for its own lane end at a rate SUMO's model sets (IDM's stop at a lane end is not bounded by `b`; 0.9–2.7 m/s² here) and so is the exiter, side by side towards the same point, and a command at `−b_E` is below what the entrant already does — `_weave_command` records a target only below the vehicle's own model, and the corridor fleet's `b` draws run from 0.53 to 3.08 m/s² (the fleet defaults 1.67 ± 15 %), so in (b) the entrant's easing is refused by `_weave_easing_ok` or inert, and in (c) the entrant *is* commanded at `−b_E` every step and still overtakes. The feasibility bound holds in 2 pairs of 24, by 1.4 m. (2) The pair *persists*: WP-53 read the give-up as "what frees both", and it does not — the entrant halted at the auxiliary lane's end stays there after the exiter beside it is rerouted, refused into lane 1 by the speed-aware guard on every lane-1 vehicle arriving at 4–14 m/s inside its brake distance, and the next exiter drives down lane 1 beside the standing auxiliary-lane queue the entrant now heads (`weave_th52.osm` at the corridor's demand, seed 5, t = 280–295 s: v00177, v00214, v00257, v00165 at rest 7 m apart behind v01701, the exiter's target hopping from one to the next, each held, none a gap), halts at the end beside it and is given up in turn. The 8 give-ups of (a) are this state; **16 halted entrants account for the 24 give-ups, one (v00991) for four, two (v00981, v01701) for three, one (v00947) for two** — v01701 halted at the T.H.52 gore's end from t ≈ 270 s to past 310 s with three exiters given up beside it, v00991 on the Ruth St 271 m window from 859 s to past 893 s with four. (3) Which side pays follows: in (a) the entrant has nothing left to give and the exiter's alternative is its reroute, so the exiter yields; in (b) neither can fall behind at its `b`; in (c) the entrant is already paying at `−b_E` and losing; only in (d) is the entrant's yield the geometry's resolution, twice.

**The rules (both `microsim.runner._weave_yield_at_ends`, called from `_weave_step` after the exiter's gap choice; commands through `_weave_command`, one-step targets clipped at the vehicle's own `b`, SUMO's safety check on; counted in `n_cooperations` and in `n_exiter_yields` / `n_entrant_yields`, the 34th and 35th `weave_sections` keys, counted only when the target binds).** *The exiter yields* (`WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exiter_yields"]`): an exit-bound changer inside its forced zone with a driven entrant ahead of it in the target lane below the creep speed (3 m/s) is driven towards a virtual leader one entrant `minGap` behind that entrant's rear — the exit priority's hold with the roles exchanged, so it comes to rest `s0_c + s0_E` behind, a follower gap the entrant's forced guard accepts — the command the smaller of IDM towards that leader and the constant deceleration that stops at the point (IDM brakes hardest last and would be clipped at `b` into an overrun), asked only while feasible at the exiter's `b` (`v_c² / (2·b_c) ≤ gap − s0_c`), re-evaluated every step, nothing to release. *The entrant yields* (`WEAVE_DEFAULTS["entrant_yields"]`, the rule the package named): while an exiter's forced change is due, a driven, moving entrant beside it — its front ahead of the exiter's rear, its rear not clear of the exiter's front by the exiter's accepted gap `s0_c + accept · v_c` — that is not the follower of the exiter's gap (that one holds already), is not commanded already this step (no chain) and can come to rest at its own `b` behind the lane end less the exiter's length and both `minGap`s (the margin above; otherwise it would be halted at its own lane end by yielding) is driven towards a virtual leader one exiter `minGap` behind the exiter's rear — `−b_E` while it overlaps, then IDM — and not while the exiter is eased towards it (mutual easing stops both, the seventh derivation). Once behind the rear it is the ordinary exit-priority geometry.

**Variants measured (the same 29-run grid as WP-51..53 — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271.4 m window, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness; the 6e7757e rows re-run in this session and identical to WP-53's).** XY: the exiter yields anywhere on the section; **XYZ: inside the forced zone (ships)**; EY: the entrant yields from the due moment (the rule as derived); EYZ: from the exiter's zone entry; XYZEY and XYEY: the combinations.

**Reading.**

1. *The exiter's yield inside the zone helps on every total and locks nothing.* Give-ups 44 → 39, exits 5,988 of 6,131 → 6,015 of 6,142 reached, minutes of lane 1 at the gore at or below 5 m/s 14 → 12, forced changes deferred 5,439 → 4,590, pair releases 218 → 169, driven vehicles unfinished 45 → 41, the entrances 5,944 → 5,973, E1 1,444 → 1,440, no collision; it binds on 89 vehicle-steps in four runs and the other 24 are byte-identical, the T.H.52 capacity and corridor-demand seeds 3 / 4 among them, so every strict marker and no-lock pin reads as before. Where it binds: T.H.52 at the corridor's demand, seed 5 — 313 of 323 exit against 296 of 308 (the section flowed, so more exiters reached it), 4 given up against 5, lane 1's last 60 m 4.1 m/s at the minimum against 1.7 (three minutes at or below 5 against two), the entrance 401 as before; the trace of the mechanism is v00272, which enters the zone at 14.1 m/s with 78.7 m left at t = 276.5 s (v01701 halted at the lane end, the four-vehicle standing queue behind it), is commanded from t = 278 s, comes to rest 4.4 m behind v01701's rear at t = 289 s (`v` = 0.07 m/s, 9.4 m of lane left) — v01701 changes at t = 289.5 s after 20 s at the lane end, the queue behind it moves, and v00272 changes at t = 295 s with 5.5 m left, where at 6e7757e it was given up at t = 295 s and v00287 after it. The Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271 m window, seed 5 — 4 given up against 8, 269 of 274 exit against 265, lane 1 4.0 m/s in one minute against 2.1 in three, 358 deferred against 814: v00646 stops behind v00991 at t = 871 s, v00991 changes at 873 s after 14 s at the lane end, v00646 at 879.5 s. The two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 4 — the entrance 380 of 470 against 351, 2 given up against 3, lane 1 4.6 against 2.9 m/s, 0 unfinished against 5, E1 252 against 256. Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 3 — 279 of 290 exit against 273 and 0 unfinished against 2, at 10 given up against 9 (one more exiter reaches the gore's end owing its change).
2. *From the whole section it is worse.* XY reads 46 given up with 43 fewer exits and 36 fewer entrants than 6e7757e: the T.H.52 capacity fixture gives up 4 / 5 at seeds 3 / 4 against 1 / 1, with lane 1 at the gore 3.9 m/s in two minutes at seed 4 against 10.7 in none, and the two-entrance defaults give up 5 / 5 at seeds 3 / 5 against 3 / 1 — an exiter braking to a stop 100–300 m out holds lane 1 for an entrant that is often freed before it arrives. The zone (80 m, the same distance at which the forced change is allowed) is the trigger that ships.
3. *The entrant's yield, the rule as derived, is not the mechanism.* From the due moment (EY) it binds on 21 vehicle-steps, five pairs in five of the 28 runs — feasible, as the trace said, only where the entrant has room and a large `b` (1.4–2.5 m/s²) — and the grid reads 36 given up with one fewer exit, four fewer lane-1 minutes at or below 5, 1,036 fewer deferred. Of the five pairs two do what is derived: the two-entrance defaults at seed 4 (v01769 beside v00379 at 6.8 / 7.8 m/s with 38 m left drops back for 2 s, v00379 changes 1 s later) and the Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 4 (v00979 beside v00481 at 7.8 / 6.9 m/s with 44 m left drops back for 2.5 s, v00481 changes 2.5 s later and v00979 follows it into lane 1 half a second after); one slows an entrant that then takes the lane-1 gap *ahead* of the halted exiter (271 m window, v00972 ahead of v00388 by 7 m); two command an entrant crawling at 0.2–2.3 m/s beside an exiter standing in a lane-1 queue (Ruth St exit peak seed 3, v00985 beside v00563 halted 78 m from the end; the two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 4, v01688 beside v00170 at 0–4.7 m/s, which changes 18 s later) — the rest of the change is the sequence moving, as the seventh derivation described. On top of the exiter's yield it returns nothing (XYZEY: 39 → 39 given up, 6,006 exits against 6,015, the entrances 5,936 against 5,973, E1 1,478 against 1,440). From the exiter's zone entry (EYZ) it binds on 120 vehicle-steps and locks the Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271 m window: lane 1 at 0.0 m/s for seven minutes, 186 of 218 exited (the queue reaches upstream), 12 unfinished, 3,121 deferred — an entrant asked to fall back at `−b` beside an exiter that has 80 m to go stops the auxiliary lane behind it and forms the queue the give-up then heads. Shipped off (`entrant_yields` = 0).
4. *The classification after the rule.* The crossing pairs fall 24 → 17 and the halted entrants behind them 16 → 12; the two rescued chains are v01701's (three give-ups → one, v00245's at the pair's forming) and v00991's (four → two). The 17 that remain are the forming events of (b) and (c) — one halted entrant per give-up in 9 of the 12, v00947's and v00991's two each — plus v00981's four, up from three (Ruth St exit peak seed 3: the exiters arriving beside it enter the zone at 16–17 m/s and need 90 m to stop at their `b` against the 70 m the zone offers, so the yield is refused as infeasible and they halt beside it). The sliding, brake-distance and crawling classes are unchanged within one.
5. *The golden is untouched at the default.* No exiter on `weave.osm` meets a halted entrant; `merge_weave` reproduces 6e7757e byte for byte (`test_golden_summary_reproduced[merge_weave]` passes, hash 436cd4ec9e5d); set, the hash moves and the metrics do not.

**What this hands on.** The crossing pair has two halves and the shipped rule takes one: an entrant halted at the auxiliary lane's end is now freed by the next exiter that can stop behind it at its `b` inside the zone. The other half — the forming event, (b) and (c) above — is the pair braking abreast for the same point and an entrant arriving at 14–16 m/s onto a lane it cannot stop in at its own `b`; those are not resolved by any yield at the lane ends, and the numbers say where to look: the entrant's arrival speed on a short auxiliary lane (the sixth derivation's ramp throttle lets it in at 14–16 m/s; the corridor fleet's `b` draws reach 0.53 m/s², at which 14 m/s needs 185 m), and the exiters that enter the zone at 16–17 m/s and cannot stop for a halted entrant in 70 m (v00981's four; a yield from farther out was measured worse on the whole grid, but a yield bounded by the *entrant's* halt time rather than the exiter's position was not). Both, and the corridor round (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds: `n_missed_exit`, `n_exiter_yields`, `n_forced_deferred`, the first standstill's position) that WP-52 and WP-53 named and that has not been run since 20f9fcb, are the next things to measure.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`exiter_yields` = 1, `entrant_yields` = 0, docstrings); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_yield_at_ends` new, the call in `_weave_step`, the two counters in the section state and `_weave_meta`, the docstrings of `_weave_step` and `_weave_meta`); `packages/api/api/schemas.py` (`WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut.n_exiter_yields`, `.n_entrant_yields`); `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` (`WEAVE_FIELDS`); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveYieldsAtTheLaneEnds`: the exiter's yield and its bounds, the entrant's yield and its bounds, the defaults; the fake state and the counters test); `tests/test_api/test_runs_merge_diagnostics.py`, `tests/test_scripts/test_corridor_sweep.py` (the key lists, 35 keys); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the key list, the WP-54 paragraph, the API paragraph); CHANGELOG.md; this section. The T.H.52 tests' markers and pins are unchanged (their runs are byte-identical under the shipped rule); golden `merge_weave` unchanged; `frontend/` untouched (the dashboard lists neither key). Session artifacts (the harness with the pair trace and the yield log, the seven grids, the pair table, the yield-episode traces) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-55, the forming pair): the exiter's yield brought forward to the entrant's halt time reads worse, and the give-ups it was written for have no move at the exiter's `b` — 12 of 39, counted; nothing ships (`exiter_yields_halting` = 0)

**Why.** WP-54 above took the persisting half of the crossing pair (an exiter inside its forced zone stops behind a driven entrant already halted at the auxiliary lane's end) and handed on the forming half with numbers: the pair forms with both vehicles braking abreast for their own lane ends, the entrant arriving at 14–16 m/s onto a lane it cannot stop in at its own `b`, and the exiters that enter the zone at 16–17 m/s cannot stop for a halted entrant within the 70 m the zone offers (v00981's four remaining give-ups); a yield from farther out (the whole section, XY) had measured worse, but a yield bounded by the *entrant's halt time* rather than the exiter's position had not. This package derives that yield, measures it and its parts, counts the give-ups honestly against the exiter's own `b`, and reports the entry-speed distribution of the entrants that head a pair. The ramp throttle and the entrance demand are untouched.

**The derivation.** The WP-54 yield waits for the entrant ahead of the exiter to be *halted* (below the creep speed, 3 m/s) before the exiter is asked to stop behind its rear; the pair forms while both still move, and by the time the entrant has halted the exiter that entered the zone at 16 m/s is inside its own brake distance. The trigger is brought forward to the entrant's halt time: a driven entrant ahead of the exiter's front in the auxiliary lane, still moving, is taken as halting at its lane end when (1) it is **committed** to that end — its brake distance at its own comfortable deceleration reaches the end, `v_E² / (2·b_E) ≥ rem_E`, so it can no longer stop short of the end at `b_E` and either changes lane or halts there (the forming pairs of the WP-54 trace: both braking at 0.9–2.7 m/s², above the entrant's `b` in every case but one) — and (2) it is there **first**: at its speed it reaches the lane end no later than the exiter at its speed reaches the gore, `rem_E / v_E ≤ rem_X / v_X` (an entrant slow and far enough ahead that the exiter passes it before it halts is behind the exiter by then, and the geometry never forms); speeds floored at the creep speed (`microsim.runner._weave_halting_first`). The exiter is then driven, as in WP-54, towards a virtual leader standing one entrant `minGap` behind where the entrant's rear *will* rest — one entrant length short of the lane end, `gap = rem_X − len_E − s0_E`, `room = gap − s0_X` — by the smaller of IDM towards that standing leader and the constant deceleration that stops at the point, only while the stop is feasible at its own `b` (`v_X² / (2·b_X) ≤ room`), re-evaluated every step with nothing to release (an entrant that changes is no longer in the target lane; one that halts falls to the WP-54 case with its actual rear), inside the forced zone, no chain (the entrant is commanded by nobody through this rule), and counted in `n_exiter_yields` like the halted case — no new `weave_sections` key, the contract stays at 35. Behind `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exiter_yields_halting"]` (0 = off, hash-neutral unless set; a switch, not a fitted value). The exiter yielding comes to rest at least `len_E + s0_E + s0_X` ≈ 10 m short of the gore, outside the give-up window (5 m), so it is never given up for having yielded.

**Variants measured (the same 29-run grid as WP-51..54 — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271.4 m window, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness; the 585e588 rows re-run in this session and identical to WP-54's shipped rows, 39 give-ups).** H: the rule as derived, inside the zone (the runner's code; the harness's re-implementation at the same knobs reproduces it). NC: without the commitment test (any moving driven entrant ahead that is there first); NT: without the time-order test; NCT: neither. HS: the halting yield from the whole section, the halted yield kept to the zone. J (harness only, the halting rule off): the WP-54 yield to a *halted* entrant also from outside the zone, but only from the last step at which the stop at `b` is still feasible — just in time — the bound on the exiter's side rather than the entrant's. JH: J with H.

**The honest count (session harness: every in-zone step of every exiter logged with the nearest driven entrant ahead of it in the auxiliary lane — halted, halting by the two conditions above, or neither — and the exiter's stop against it, `need = v_X² / (2·b_X)` against the `room` behind that entrant's rest point; 886 exiters entered a zone over the 28 runs at 585e588).** Of the 39 give-ups: **12** are an exiter with a halted or halting entrant ahead of it on some in-zone step and a stop infeasible at its `b` on *every* such step — no move, at its `b`, for any yield inside the zone; 1 was offered a feasible stop and took it (v00088, Ruth St corridor fleet seed 3, given up as brake-distance after all); 1 was offered one at its zone entry and the command did not bind (v00070: its own car-following towards its real leader asked less already, and the geometry was infeasible on the 11 steps after); and **25** had no halted or halting entrant ahead of their front on any in-zone step — the 5 crossing pairs whose entrant was never halted or halting *ahead* of the exiter's front on an in-zone step — beside it or behind it (v00069, v00508, v00355: the abreast and sliding formings of WP-54's (b) and (c)), or ahead, moving and uncommitted until it halted beside it (v00198's v00956 at 8.2 m/s with 47 m of lane, v00911's v01012 at 15.3 m/s) — which no yield to an entrant ahead reaches, the 10 sliding, the 3 crawling and 7 of the 9 brake-distance give-ups. Read across: the zone offers 60–69 m of room behind a halted entrant at the lane end; an exiter needs 74–90 m from 13–17 m/s at a `b` of 1.0–1.6, and 95–165 m at the corridor fleet's low draws (0.56–0.96 m/s²). The closest cases miss by 1.9, 2.8 and 2.9 m (v00518, v00645, v00523), the rest by 8–15 m or more, on every in-zone step. A yield from outside the zone would have to begin 90–165 m from the gore — on the 136 m Ruth St section at its entry or upstream of it — and there the entrant was not yet halted: v00981 entered the section at t = 671 s at 19.4 m/s with a `b` of 0.99 m/s² (190 m to stop against 134 m of lane) and halted at the end while the three exiters given up beside it (t = 696–720 s) were already inside their own brake distance when they entered the zone. The exiters at zone entry, all 886: 12.5 m/s on average (median 12.8, p90 15.3, max 20.3; 26 % at 14 m/s or more); the 39 given up: 13.9 (median 13.3, 10.8–18.7).

**Reading.**

1. *The halt-time trigger reads worse, and the trace says why.* H: give-ups 39 → 43, exits 6,015 of 6,142 → 6,017 of 6,139 reached, the entrances 5,973 → 5,958, forced changes deferred 4,590 → 4,838, pair releases 169 → 178, lane-1 minutes at or below 5 m/s 12 → 13, no collision, no lock; it binds on 142 vehicle-steps against 89 (53 more, in five runs), the T.H.52 rows and the golden byte-identical. Where it binds, the entrant it yields to is *committed* only through a small drawn `b`: the Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 5 — v00239 at 10.9 m/s is stopped for v00960 ahead at 14.8 m/s with 56 m of lane and `b` = 0.77 (143 m to stop), for 9 steps from t = 335 s, and changes into lane 0 at t = 339 s at 7.2 m/s behind an entrant that changed too: it braked for nothing, and the row reads 5 given up against 4 with lane 1 at 3.3 m/s in one minute against 6.7 in none, 357 deferred against 132; the two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 4 — v00161 at 11.2 m/s for v01684 at 12.4 m/s with 62 m and `b` = 1.22 (63 m to stop, committed by 0.6 m), 4 steps, v00161 changes at t = 205 s and v01684 halts at the lane end 11 s later anyway (the same crossing pair as at 585e588, v00158 given up beside it at t = 216.5 s); seed 5 — v00573 for v01941 (10.6 m/s, 67 m, `b` = 0.69), 7 steps, changes at 8.0 m/s having entered at 10.9. Commitment says the entrant *cannot stop short at its `b`*; it does not say it will halt — with 56–67 m of lane it has 4–6 s to find its gap, and it finds one, or halts and heads the pair regardless. The rule taxes lane 1 for a prediction that is wrong more often than right at the speeds where it fires.
2. *The parts.* NT (no time-order test) is H to the byte: in every binding case the committed entrant is closer to the end and faster than the creep floor, so the order test never decides. NC (no commitment test) binds on 365 vehicle-steps and reads 43 given up with 50 fewer exits (5,965) and 81 fewer entrants (5,892): the Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 3, gives up 6 against 10 with 282 exits against 279 — the one row it helps — and the T.H.52 capacity fixture at seed 5 gives up 2 against 1 with 360 of 466 on the entrance against 373 and lane 1 at 3.7 m/s in three minutes against 5.0 in one; NCT reads 46. HS (the halting yield from the whole section) binds 294 times and reads 43 with 6,009 exits, the two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 3 at 306 of 314 exits against 309 and 4 unfinished against 1. None ships.
3. *The just-in-time yield from outside the zone (J) reads 34 given up, and its gain is not a mechanism.* J binds on 96 vehicle-steps: 30 on the two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 4 (against 14 at 585e588), where it reads 1 given up against 2, 307 of 308 exits against 301 of 304 and 248 deferred against 305 — the one row where it acts and helps — and 12 / 6 on the T.H.52 capacity fixture at seeds 5 / 4 (one exit fewer each; seed 4 with 0 unfinished against 2). Its other movements come from **single vehicle-steps**: on the T.H.52 corridor-demand fixture at seed 3 it binds once (t = 902 s, v00627 to 1.99 m/s) and the row reads 0 given up against 1, lane 1 at 13.1 m/s in no minute against 3.8 in one, the entrance 394 of 470 against 403 and 292 reached against 299; at seed 5 it binds once (t = 125 s, v00093 to 3.55 m/s) and the row reads 1 given up against 4, 301 of 311 exits against 313 of 323, the entrance 390 against 401 and lane 1 at 6.7 m/s in no minute against 4.1 in three; on the two-entrance defaults at seed 4 once, and the row reads 8 unfinished against 1, E1 202 of 360 against 210 and E1's acceleration-lane end at 0.2 m/s in 15 minutes against 0.8 in 13. A one-step speed target on one vehicle re-rolls a 20-minute run: SUMO is deterministic and the section is sensitive, so the T.H.52 corridor-demand rows under J are *other realisations* of the same seed, not the rule at work. On the totals J trades 5 give-ups, 4 lane-1 minutes and 625 deferred steps for 11 exits, 30 entrants, 5 of E1's and 6 more vehicles unfinished — and the entrance losses on the T.H.52 rows (403 → 394, 401 → 390, 388 → 382) are the kind that rejected the priority from zone entry and the abreast patience. Not shipped, and not added to the runner. JH reads 36 with 5,994 exits and 5,918 entrants.
4. *A note on reading the grid.* Two of J's three largest row movements, and H's on the two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 5 (8 steps, the first one at t = 283 s and the other seven at t = 1124–1127 s; 285 reached against 301, the entrance 362 against 374), come from one to eight binding vehicle-steps. Totals over the 28 runs that move by five give-ups or thirty entrants with a rule binding on a handful of steps are the sequence re-rolled, and WP-56 onward should read a variant's binding steps per row beside its totals before calling a direction; the rules that shipped (the speed-aware acceptance, the exiter's yield) showed their effect on rows where they bound tens of times with a traced mechanism.
5. *The entrants that head a pair are the fast mode of the entry-speed distribution.* Over the 28 runs 3,122 driven entrants entered a section (their speed on the first step under control on section lane 0): 9.6 m/s on average, median 8.4, and bimodal — 1,727 between 4 and 10 m/s (the ramp queue's discharge, the sixth derivation's throttle) and 412 between 16 and 18 m/s (19 % at 16 m/s or more, 22 % at 14 or more; p90 17.4, max 22.0); 2,495 had a gap chosen on the ramp; 40 (1 %) could not stop within their auxiliary lane at their own `b`. The 12 entrants that head a crossing pair at 585e588: 16.6 m/s on average, median 17.4, p10 10.9, p90 19.5, max 21.2; `b` 1.34 on average against 1.68; **5 of the 12 cannot stop within their lane at their `b`** (v00981 19.4 m/s at 0.99 → 190 m against 134; v01384 19.0 at 0.80 → 225; v00960 18.3 at 0.77 → 217; v00991 17.2 at 0.91 → 163; v00947 15.7 at 0.59 → 209 m); 10 of the 12 are Ruth St entrants at 15.3–21.2 m/s onto the 136 m auxiliary lane, the two T.H.52 ones (v01684, v01701) at 10.9 and 7.5 m/s onto 305 m; 7 of the 12 had a gap chosen on the ramp before they appeared and lost it. Reported as a finding: the ramp throttle and the entrance demand are as they were.

**What this hands on.** The forming pair is decided at the entrant's section entry, not at the lane ends, and not by the exiter: an entrant arriving at 15–21 m/s onto 130 m of auxiliary lane with a `b` under 1 m/s² cannot stop in it and has about seven seconds to find a gap — the halted entrant that then heads the pair is the fast mode of the distribution, one in five of all entrants. Nothing on the exiter's side inside the zone reaches it (12 of the 39 give-ups have no stop at the exiter's `b` there, three of them by under 3 m), and from outside the zone every trigger measured — the whole section (WP-54), the halt time (H, HS) and just in time (J) — taxes lane 1 more than it returns or moves the grid by re-rolling it. Two things to derive next, neither touched here: (a) the entrant side at section entry — a driver entering a short auxiliary lane at a speed from which it cannot stop within it is an entry-speed choice on the ramp's last metres (the sixth derivation's throttle lets it in at 16–21 m/s when the ramp queue is empty), to be derived as a bound `v ≤ √(2·b·L_aux)`-shaped anticipation and measured against the entrance criterion that rejected every ramp-side brake so far; (b) the exiter's zone is 80 m whatever its `b`, and the corridor fleet draws `b` down to 0.53 m/s² — a zone entry scaled to the exiter's own brake distance for the *yield only* (between XY and XYZ, which the whole-section measurement did not separate) — with, again, the corridor round (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds: `n_missed_exit`, `n_exiter_yields`, `n_forced_deferred`, the first standstill's position) that WP-52..54 named and that has not been run since 20f9fcb.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`exiter_yields_halting` = 0, docstring); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_halting_first` new, the halting branch in `_weave_yield_at_ends` and its docstring); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveYieldsAtTheLaneEnds.test_halting_first`, `test_the_exiter_yields_to_a_halting_entrant_ahead`, `test_the_halting_yield_is_bounded`; the `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` pin); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the WP-55 paragraph; the key list unchanged at 35); CHANGELOG.md; this section. No `weave_sections` key added, so `packages/api/api/schemas.py`, `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` and the key-list tests are untouched; the T.H.52 tests' markers and pins are unchanged (their runs are byte-identical at the default); golden `merge_weave` unchanged (hash 436cd4ec9e5d, byte-identical at the default; 0bbd30d8f034 with the switch set, the metrics unchanged); `frontend/` untouched. Session artifacts (the harness with the in-zone trace, the entry trace and the variant knobs, the eight grids, the honesty tables, the episode traces) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-56, the brake-scaled zone): the exiter's yield asked from its own brake distance binds where the cooperation already brakes it, and every row it moves is the sequence re-rolled — nothing ships (`exiter_yield_lead_s` = 0); and the exiter's yield itself goes off by default (`exiter_yields` = 0) on corridor evidence

**Why.** WP-55 above counted 12 of the 39 fixture give-ups as an exiter that entered the 80 m forced zone at 10.8–16.6 m/s needing 41–165 m to stop at its own `b` against the 60–69 m the zone offers behind an entrant halted at the lane end — the zone is fixed (`force_within_m`) and blind to the exiter's brake distance, and the corridor fleet draws `b` down to 0.53 m/s². Its lead (b): a yield-only zone scaled to that brake distance — from `max(force_within_m, v²/(2·b) + margin)` upstream of the gore an exiter with a halted entrant ahead in the auxiliary lane starts its yield early enough to stop at `b`, while the forced change, the exit priority and the give-up keep the 80 m zone so the entrance numbers are untouched — with the warning to read a variant's binding vehicle-steps per row beside its totals, since the earlier yields from outside the zone (XY in WP-54; J, JH in WP-55) moved the grid rather than the mechanism. This package derives that zone, bounds it, measures it in two forms on the same 29-run grid, and reads every moved row against the steps on which the rule bound.

**The derivation (`microsim.runner._weave_yield_early`, called from `_weave_yield_at_ends`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exiter_yield_lead_s"]` = τ, 0 = off).** The shipped yield of WP-54 stops the exiter one entrant `minGap` behind the halted entrant's rear: `room = gap − s0_X`, feasible while `need = v²/(2·b_X) ≤ room`, asked only inside the fixed zone. The brake-scaled zone asks it *outside* the fixed zone as well, from the step at which the exiter is within τ seconds of travel of the last point at which the stop is still feasible at its `b`: `room − need ≤ max(v, v_creep)·τ`. For an entrant halted at the lane end that is the yield zone `max(force_within_m, v²/(2·b) + v·τ + len_E + s0_E + s0_X)` upstream of the gore — the exiter's own stopping distance, one lead time of travel and the rest-point offset (≈ 10 m at the fleet defaults) — and, unlike the whole-section yield (XY, which braked the exiter gently to the same point from 100–300 m out and held lane 1 for an entrant freed before it arrived), it holds lane 1 for the shortest time a stop at `b` allows: the exiter keeps its speed until the last lead time and then brakes at `a_stop = −v²/(2·room)`, below `b` by the lead. WP-55's just-in-time form J is this rule at τ ≈ 0.7 s (one step of travel and acceleration). The speed in the slack term is floored at the creep speed so that an exiter the rule has brought to rest at the rest point (`room` ≈ 0, `need` = 0) is held there as inside the zone rather than let go by its own model on the next step (the first draft without the floor let it creep past). Three guards against a lock: outside the fixed zone an exiter the rule has held below the creep speed for longer than `pair_release_s` (2 s, the fifth derivation's two reaction times) on consecutive asked steps *lapses* and is not asked again while a halted entrant stands ahead of it — the clock resets on any step the rule does not ask, and the lapse clears once no halted entrant is ahead (the geometry gone); nobody else is commanded through the rule (no chain); it is re-evaluated every step. And the premise, added after the first measurement below: the entrant is halted **at its lane end** — within the fixed zone of the end of its lane, `rem_E ≤ force_within_m` — because without that condition the rule also bound, one to three steps at a time, on exiters crawling in a standing lane-1 queue at the *section start* beside a queued entrant halted 300 m from its lane end (the ramp queue of the sixth derivation, not the crossing pair), and those single steps are what re-rolled the T.H.52 rows. Counted in `n_exiter_yields` like the halted case (no new `weave_sections` key; the contract stays at 35); the harness counts the outside-zone binds separately. A positive τ is one human reaction time or a few (Treiber & Kesting 2013, ch. 12, the figure `pair_release_s` doubles); not a fitted value.

**The exiter's yield goes off by default (`exiter_yields` = 0; VM M).** Corridor evidence arrived during this package: the 20-seed four-hour I-94 battery under WP-54's rule (VM M, runner 585e588, `artifacts/mndot_rounds/weave_2026-09-24/battery_corrected_inputs_exiter_yields_585e588.json`) locks one seed — 6904272788004776631 departs 0.356 of its demand against 0.852 under VM K without the rule, with 18,259 exiter-yield vehicle-steps against 770–3,500 on the other seeds and every on-ramp starved — while the other 19 seeds read 0.843–0.899 (the battery 0.836 departed, speed RMSPE 0.716, GEH 0.077, 19 collisions against VM K's 15, given-up exits 0.6 / 0.9 % against 0.8 / 1.0 %, and VM K's 0.743 seed recovered to 0.865). A rule that locks a seed of the flagship corridor cannot ship on until it is bounded, so `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["exiter_yields"]` is 0 from this commit (the provenance is in the key's docstring); `1` switches it on. The golden `merge_weave` does not move (the rule never bound on `weave.osm`; hash 436cd4ec9e5d, `test_golden_summary_reproduced[merge_weave]` passes byte-identically at the new default); the two pins that assumed the default on (`test_defaults_and_hash`, `TestWeaveYieldsAtTheLaneEnds.test_defaults`) now pin 0 and exercise the rule with the key set, and WP-54's and WP-55's other yield tests already set `exiter_yields=1.0` explicitly; the T.H.52 and Ruth St integration markers were written before WP-54 shipped and untouched by it, and the grid below shows the new default reproduces those rows to the number. The lock's standstill map is being fetched on a VM (the seed index in the spawn, trajectories kept); nothing of the corridor was run locally. The brake-scaled zone is an extension of the exiter's yield and inert without it (the row below: `exiter_yields` 0 with τ = 1 s is the off-baseline byte for byte), so the grid is reported with the exiter's yield **off** (the new default; WP-54's 6e7757e rows) and **on** (WP-55's 585e588 rows, the baseline every WP-56 variant is measured against, `exiter_yields` = 1 set explicitly).

**Variants measured (the same 29-run grid as WP-51..55 — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271.4 m window, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness).** `off`: `exiter_yields` = 0, the new default (identical to WP-54's 6e7757e rows in every fixture row; 44 given up); `off+τ1`: the same with `exiter_yield_lead_s` = 1 (identical to `off`: the lead is inert without the yield); `on`: `exiter_yields` = 1 (identical to WP-55's 585e588 rows, the 0bd4519 default re-run in this session; 39 given up). **Z05 / Z1 / Z2 / Z3**: the rule as it stands in the runner (the entrant at its lane end) at τ = 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3 s on top of `on`; Z1on: Z1 with `exiter_yields` = 1 passed explicitly (identical to Z1); Z1NB: Z1 without the standstill bound (harness; identical to Z1 — the bound never fires in this form). **L05 / L1 / L2 / L3**: the first form measured, without the lane-end condition (any halted driven entrant ahead), at the same τ; L1NB: L1 without the bound; L1H: L1 with `exiter_yields_halting` = 1 (WP-55's halting entrant taken as halted at its rest point). Columns as in WP-55's table; the last one the exiter-yield vehicle-steps (of which outside the fixed zone), the lapses, and the longest standstill the early yield held outside the zone.
