**Reading.**

1. *At the default nothing changes but the default.* `off` is WP-54's 6e7757e grid to the number — 44 given up (24 crossing pairs on 16 halted entrants, 10 sliding, 9 brake-distance, 1 crawling), 5,988 exits of 6,131 reached, 14 lane-1 minutes at or below 5 m/s, 5,439 forced changes deferred, 218 pair releases, 45 unfinished, the entrances 5,944, no collision — and `on` is 585e588's (39; 6,015 of 6,142; 12; 4,590; 169; 41; 5,973). The T.H.52 capacity rows are the same under both (the yield never bound there), so `test_th52_weave_at_capacity_flows` reads as before: seeds 3 / 4 / 5 depart 395 / 401 / 373 of 466 against the 420 the marker requires (25 / 19 / 47 short), lane 1's first 60 m reads 3.9 / 4.3 / 3.3 m/s at the minimum in 2 / 5 / 4 windows against "above 5 in every window" (seed 3: 3.9 and 4.4 in minutes 2–3, then 5.8–11.4; seed 4: 4.7, 4.5, 4.4, 4.3, 4.9; seed 5: 4.9, 4.8, 4.2, 3.3), 4 of 456 / 2 of 487 / 3 of 513 driven unfinished, one exit given up and no collision at each — the entrance and the lane-1 criteria fail at every seed, the unfinished and collision criteria pass, and the strict marker's stated numbers are the run's.
2. *The brake-scaled zone cannot reach the give-ups it was written for, by construction.* The section-wide trace (every driven exiter's step on the section, in or out of the fixed zone, with the nearest halted or halting entrant ahead and the stop's feasibility) says of the 12 honest give-ups at `on` what WP-55 said of their in-zone steps: on **no** section step with a halted or halting entrant ahead was the stop feasible at the exiter's `b` — the entrant was not yet halted when the exiter was at its brake distance (v00981 halted at t ≈ 690 s, the three exiters given up beside it entered the section at 16 m/s after t = 690 s already inside their brake distance; the closest margins 1.9 / 2.8 / 2.9 m are in-zone margins). A yield that begins at the brake distance has nothing to begin from. Whatever the rule moves, it moves through exiters that were not given up: 25 of the 39 have no such entrant ahead on any section step, 12 no move at `b`, 1 offered and taken, 1 offered and not bound — the same four counts under every Z variant (12 / 1 / 1 / 25 at τ = 0.5–2; 13 at τ = 3 with one crossing pair more).
3. *The rule as derived binds on six exiters in four runs, and where it binds the cooperation was already braking the exiter at `b`.* Z1 binds outside the zone on 16 vehicle-steps: v01298 (two-entrance corridor fleet, seed 4; 16.2 m/s, 162 m from the gore, `b` = 1.16, 8 steps from t = 223 s) and v01302 (13.7 m/s, 2 steps) for v01688 halted at the auxiliary lane's end; v00542 (Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, seed 3; 15.5 m/s, 94 m out, 2 steps) for v00981; v00654 (271 m window, seed 5; 23.4 m/s, 135 m out, 1 step) for v00991; v00921 (fleet defaults, seed 3; 19.2 m/s, 130 m out, 1 step) for v01012; and v00580 (Ruth St corridor fleet, seed 3) crawling at 0.6 m/s 91 m out behind v00987 halted 79 m from its lane end, 2 steps. None of the six was given up under either rule. The traced one: at `on` v01298 brakes from t = 224 s at −1.17 m/s² (16.18 → 13.85 → 11.40 m/s over 224–228 s) as the cooperating follower of v01688's chosen gap — v01688 is a driven entrant and v01298 the follower it holds at `−b` — and comes to rest 54.7 m from the gore at t = 236 s; under Z1 the early yield binds from t = 223 s at −1.01 m/s² (16.18 → 12.63 over 3.5 s) and it comes to rest 56.9 m from the gore at t = 236 s; in both v01688 moves at t ≈ 239.5 s and changes into lane 1 ahead of it, and v01298 changes after. The rule's whole effect on its mechanism row is a stop 2 m farther back at a rate 0.16 m/s² gentler; the give-up that disappears on that row (2 → 1) is v01351 at t = 525 s, 300 s later, no overlapping vehicle, and the six more exits are the sequence after it. On the Ruth St corridor fleet at seed 3 the same rule with 4 binding steps reads 11 given up against 10 with 277 exits against 279; at τ = 2 the same row reads 9 and 279 (7 steps), at τ = 3 the same 9 and 279. Two rows with one binding step each (fleet defaults seed 3, the 271 m window seed 5) are byte-identical to `on` in every other column: one step's target below the model on one vehicle changed nothing.
4. *The mechanism row swings with the lead on the same two vehicles.* The two-entrance corridor fleet at seed 4 reads, by τ: 0.5 s — 2 of 303 given up, 291 of 303 exits, the entrance 339 of 470, 3 unfinished (v01298 alone, 6 steps); 1 s — 1 of 308, 307 of 308, 380, 1 (10 steps); 2 s — 3 of 312, 306 of 312, 383, 0 (18 steps); 3 s — 4 of 304, 294 of 304, 347, 4 (23 steps, lane 1 at 3.2 m/s in two minutes). The best and the worst row of the whole grid are the same exiter stopping at a slightly different point behind the same entrant; that is not a mechanism to set a default by. The totals follow: Z05 39 given up with 10 fewer exits and 41 fewer entrants than `on`; Z1 39 with 4 more exits (6,019 of 6,146), the entrances equal, 99 fewer deferred, 1 more unfinished; Z2 39 with 5 more exits and 3 more entrants; Z3 40 with 7 fewer exits and 33 fewer entrants. No collision, no lock, no lapse at any τ (the rest point of an entrant halted at its lane end is inside the fixed zone, where the shipped rule holds the exiter without a bound), Z1NB identical to Z1.
5. *Without the lane-end condition the rule re-rolls the T.H.52 rows from single steps, and they are J's and XY's rows.* The first form measured (L) also binds on exiters crawling in a standing lane-1 queue at the section start — 0.3–2.8 m/s, 285–306 m from the gore, a queued entrant halted 300 m from its lane end 9–15 m ahead, `room` ≈ 0 — for one to three steps each; that is where its totals come from. L1 reads 36 given up (30 early binds on 14 exiters in 9 runs, 17 lapses, no standstill over 2 s) with 17 fewer exits, 30 fewer entrants and 2 more unfinished than `on`; its rows: the T.H.52 corridor demand at seed 3 — 0 of 292 given up against 1 of 299, lane 1 at 13.1 m/s in no minute against 3.8 in one, the entrance 394 against 403 — from **one** binding step (v00627 at 2.53 m/s, t = 902 s, held one step behind v01928 halted 12 m ahead and 304 m from its lane end), WP-55's J row to the number; at seed 5 — 1 of 311 against 4 of 323, 301 of 311 exits against 313 of 323, the entrance 390 against 401 — from one step (v00093 at 4.27 m/s, t = 125 s), J's row again; the T.H.52 capacity fixture at seed 4 — **5 of 375 given up against 1 of 379**, lane 1 at 3.9 m/s in two minutes against 10.7 in none, 339 deferred against 71, the entrance 397 against 401 — from five steps on three crawling exiters at the section start (v00174 one step at t = 229 s at 0.79 m/s, v00177 three at t = 238.5–239.5 s at 2.75 m/s, v00216 one at t = 294 s at 0.41 m/s), the four extra give-ups at t = 432–988 s, and the row is WP-54's XY row (the whole-section yield) to the number, because XY's first divergence from the baseline is the same command on the same vehicle at the same step; the corridor demand at seed 4 — 304 of 316 exits against 308 of 317, 4 unfinished against 2, 383 against 388 — from four steps with two lapses. L05 reads 35 (27 binds, 25 lapses) with 28 fewer exits and 53 fewer entrants; L2 39; L3 41; L1H 42 (the halting entrant re-adds WP-55's H rows); L1NB, without the bound, holds one exiter 11.5 s at rest outside the zone and reads 37 with 29 fewer exits — the bound is what keeps the unrestricted form from standing. The same crawling-queue steps are the kind WP-55's note on reading the grid warned about, and the lane-end condition is now part of the rule; with it none of the T.H.52 rows moves at any τ.
6. *The classification and the halted entrants do not move under the rule as derived.* Z1: 17 crossing pairs on 12 halted entrants (4, 2, 2, 1 × 9), 11 sliding, 10 brake-distance, 1 crawling — `on` reads 17 / 10 / 9 / 3 on the same 12 entrants; v00981's four, v00991's two and v00947's two are as they were. The unrestricted form's lower crossing-pair counts (16 on 11 entrants) are the re-rolled T.H.52 corridor-demand seed 5, whose v01701 pair (one give-up at `on`) is not formed in the other realisation.
7. *The golden is untouched at the default.* Hash 436cd4ec9e5d, byte-identical before and after the flip of `exiter_yields`; with any override set the hash moves and the metrics do not (mean travel time 69.500 s, p90 84.126, σ_v 3.940 / 3.449, VMT 169.649, VHT 1.7492, fuel 90.54, throughput 1680.0, 14 / 19 / 2 / 6, 33 exits, no collision, no yield on `weave.osm`).

**What this hands on.** The brake-scaled zone closes the exiter-side lead of WP-55: the honest give-ups have no feasible stop on any section step (the entrant halts after the exiter is inside its brake distance), and where a stop is feasible early the cooperation already brakes the exiter at `−b` towards the halted entrant as the follower of its gap — the yield from the brake distance is that brake one step earlier and 2 m longer, and every row it moves is a re-roll (the one mechanism row swings from the grid's best to its worst as τ goes 0.5 → 3 s on the same two vehicles). Nothing ships; the key stays as a measured option with its lane-end condition. Two things stand out for what comes next: (a) the exiter's yield itself is off by default now on VM M's lock — one seed with 18,259 binding vehicle-steps against 770–3,500 elsewhere says the in-zone yield chains on the corridor (an exiter held behind a halted entrant that is itself held, in a queue the fixtures do not form); the standstill map on its way from the VM should say where, and a bound on the in-zone yield (the same standstill lapse this package added outside the zone, or a cap on the yields per entrant) is the next derivation before the rule can return; (b) the entrant side at section entry — WP-55's (a), the entry-speed bound `v ≤ √(2·b·L_aux)` on the ramp's last metres — is the only lead left on the forming pair, since neither yield at the lane ends, from the zone, from the brake distance, from the halt time or from the whole section has reached it. The corridor round that WP-52..55 named (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds: `n_missed_exit`, `n_exiter_yields`, `n_forced_deferred`, the first standstill's position) is now VM M's battery, with the rule off.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`exiter_yield_lead_s` = 0 with its docstring; `exiter_yields` 1 → 0 with the VM M provenance in the dict and the docstring); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_yield_early` new; `_weave_yield_at_ends` takes `t`, the early branch, the lapse's reset and the empty-lane reset; the two state fields in `_weave_step`); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveYieldsAtTheLaneEnds.test_yield_early_bounds`, `test_the_exiter_yields_outside_the_zone_within_its_lead`, `test_the_early_yield_lapses_and_clears`; `test_defaults` and the `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` pin at the new defaults); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the WP-54 paragraph's default, the WP-56 paragraph; the key list unchanged at 35); CHANGELOG.md; this section. No `weave_sections` key added, so `packages/api/api/schemas.py`, `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` and the key-list tests are untouched; the T.H.52 tests' markers and pins are unchanged (their rows are the same under the yield on and off); golden `merge_weave` unchanged (hash 436cd4ec9e5d, byte-identical at the default); `frontend/` untouched. Session artifacts (the harness with the section-wide yield trace and the early-bind count, the sixteen grids, the tables, the per-episode traces) not committed.

## 2026-09-24 (block 3, WP-57, the entrant's entry speed): an entrant asked on the ramp's last metres to enter no faster than its own stop at `b` within the auxiliary lane allows — the first ramp-side brake since the sixth derivation — binds on 187 entrants, spares the entrance and the T.H.52 rows, and reads worse on the give-ups, because the pair does not form by momentum; nothing ships (`entry_speed_bound` = 0)

**Why.** WP-54..56 above exhausted the exit side of the crossing pair at the lane ends: the fixture give-ups are 44 at the current defaults, 12–14 of them honest (no feasible stop at the exiter's own `b` on any section step), and the one rule that helped (`exiter_yields`) locked a seed of twenty on the four-hour corridor and is off. The lead left on the *forming* pair was entrant-side (WP-55 §"Entry-speed finding", WP-56's hand-on): entrants enter the section bimodally (the ramp queue's discharge at 4–10 m/s, and 19 % at 16 m/s or more when the queue is empty and the sixth derivation's throttle admits them at 16–21 m/s), the 12 pair-heading entrants averaged 16.6 m/s onto the 136 m Ruth St auxiliary lane and 5 of 12 could not stop within it at their own `b`. This package derives the entrant's own anticipation of a short auxiliary lane as a bound on its entry speed, implements it on the vehicle's commanded speed only (SUMO's safety check on, the ramp throttle's demand and every scenario untouched), measures it on the same 29-run grid in five forms, and reads the give-ups, their classification and honesty, and the entry-speed distribution before and after. It is the first ramp-side brake since the sixth derivation, and the entrance criterion (T.H.52 at capacity: 395 / 401 / 373 of 466 departed against the 420 pinned; the earlier priority forms were rejected at 407 / 416 / 402) is the measure it must not worsen.

**The derivation (`microsim.runner._weave_entry_speed_bound`, `_weave_entry_bound`; `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["entry_speed_bound"]`, 0 = off).** An entrant E with its front `D` metres from the end of the auxiliary lane (the gore: on the ramp, the ramp to the section start plus the section, the `remaining` the anticipation already passes to `_weave_cooperate`) can come to rest at its comfortable deceleration `b_E` with its front `s0_E` (its `minGap`, the accepted gap at rest) short of that end iff `v_E² / (2·b_E) ≤ D − s0_E`; the ceiling is therefore

    v_E ≤ v_bound(D) = √(2 · b_E · (D − s0_E)),

the constant-`b` braking curve that ends one `minGap` before the lane end — at the section start `√(2·b·(L_S − s0))`: 21.1 m/s at the fleet defaults (`b` 1.67 m/s², `s0` 2.5 m) on the 135.7 m Ruth St lane, 16.4 m/s at `b` = 1.0, 11.9 m/s at the corridor fleet's smallest draw (0.53); 31.8 and 17.9 m/s on the 305 m T.H.52 lane. On the ramp the curve is higher (`D` is larger) and descends towards the section start at the rate `b` along the vehicle's path, so an entrant riding it brakes at exactly `b`. The rule: on the ramp within `lookahead_m` (120 m) of the section — the `approaching` set of `_weave_step`, where the entrant already chooses its gap and eases — the entrant's speed on the next step is kept at or under the curve at its next position, `√(2·b·(D − v·Δt − s0))`, as a one-step car-following target through `_weave_command`: recorded only when below the entrant's own model (an entrant braking harder for a real leader is left to it), clipped at `−b` (an entrant already over the curve is asked its comfortable brake, which holds its deficit `v²/(2b) − (D − s0)` where it is — at `−b` the margin is constant, `d/dt = v·(|a|/b − 1)` — rather than letting the free term grow it; it cannot get back under the curve at `b`), the lowest target on the entrant this step winning as for every request. Bounded by construction: not evaluated once the entrant is on the section (release on entry; on the section its own car-following, the easing and the cooperation are as they were), the ceiling on the ramp is never below `√(2·b·(L_S − s0))` (11.9 m/s at the smallest corridor draw on Ruth St), so it can neither hold an entrant below the creep speed nor stand it on the ramp; nobody else is commanded through it (no chain). Counted in `n_entry_bounded` — the **36th `weave_sections` key** (docs/CONTRACTS.md §2, `WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut`, `WEAVE_FIELDS`; the vehicle-steps on which the target bound) — and, as every target, in `n_cooperations`. How it differs from every earlier ramp-side brake: the fourth derivation's easing on the ramp braked an entrant for a lane-1 vehicle that was passing it anyway, and the fifth's "only when needed" condition was the one lead that moved the entrance; this rule reads no other vehicle at all — it is the entrant's own geometry, its `b` and its lane's length — so it cannot be the head of the ramp queue on a T.H.52-length lane (the fleet defaults' `b` never falls under the 31.8 m/s curve, and the ramp's limit is 22.2 m/s), and it did not: the T.H.52 rows are byte-identical under it.

**Variants measured (the same 29-run grid as WP-51..56 — seeds 3 / 4 / 5 on the Ruth St module at both demand points and both fleets, the Ruth St corridor fleet at the 271.4 m window, the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity fixtures, the two two-entrance fixtures, the moderate fixture and the golden; 20 simulated minutes at step 0.5 s, macOS, session harness `wp57_harness.py` = WP-56's with WP-55's entry trace and a per-step trace of the bound).** `off`: the default (re-run in this session; identical to WP-56's `off` and WP-54's 6e7757e rows in every fixture row, 44 given up). **EB: the rule as derived** (`entry_speed_bound` = 1; `EBt`, the same run under the per-step trace, is identical to it in every row). `hold` (harness): asked only while the entrant is still under the curve at its current position — never a `−b` brake on one already over it. `ramp` (harness): the bound from the ramp's start (210 m) as well, not only within `lookahead_m`. `bx05` (harness): the curve at half the entrant's `b` (a margin on the deceleration). `onEB`: EB with the exiter's yield on (`exiter_yields` = 1; WP-54's rule, off by default since WP-56), against WP-56's `on` rows (39 given up). Columns as in WP-56's table; the last one the entry-bound vehicle-steps, the exiter-yield vehicle-steps, and the changer-eased vehicle-steps.

**The bound's trace (EB; every approaching entrant on every ramp step within `lookahead_m`: its speed, `b`, `D`, the curve at its next position, whether the target bound; 4,796 entrants over the 28 runs).** The entrants reach the anticipation zone (120 m before the section start, 330 m from the gore on Ruth St) either in the ramp queue's discharge (2,751 of 4,796 below 8 m/s) or at the ramp's own limit, 22.2 m/s. **187 entrants are bound**, on 7.4 steps each (at most 15; the zone is 120 m, 5.5 s at the ramp's limit), 1,383 vehicle-steps: they reach the zone at 21.5 m/s on average with `b` 1.09 on average and arrive on the section at 18.0 m/s — the rule takes 3.5 m/s off the fast mode and leaves the slow mode alone. **64 entrants are over the curve already at the zone's entry**, 2.6 m/s on average (5.1 at most), `b` 0.76 on average: at 22.2 m/s an entrant needs `b` ≥ 0.75 m/s² to be under the curve 330 m from the gore (`22.2² / (2 · 0.75) = 328` m), and the corridor fleet draws `b` down to 0.53 — for those the rule asks `−b` across the zone (they shed 4.4 m/s on average) and they arrive still over it (71 at the section start, 2.9 m/s over on average, 6.4 at most): these are the 27 Ruth St entrants (of 591) that still cannot stop within the lane at their `b` under EB, against 40 of 597 at `off`. Of the 17 entrants heading a crossing pair under EB, 5 are of this kind (v00947 at `b` 0.59, v01384 0.80, v01407 0.79, v00960 0.77 — 22.1–22.2 m/s at the zone's entry, 17.7–18.3 m/s at the section start after 8–12 binding steps — and v01008 at 1.28, over by the section start) and **12 are under the curve at the section start** — they could stop within the lane at their `b` and halted at its end regardless: v00945 (19.9 m/s at `b` 2.19, 90 m to stop against 128 m of lane, never bound) and v00960 of seed 4 (19.0 m/s at 1.35, 133 against 135, bound 5 steps) head chains of **five and six** give-ups on the exit peak's seed 4 (t = 97–168 s and 349–411 s: each exiter arriving beside the standing auxiliary-lane queue halts at the end and is given up in turn, WP-54's persisting state), v00968 (21.7 m/s, `b` 3.08, 76 m to stop) two on seed 3, v00948 (2.43) two on seed 5.

**Reading.**

1. *The rule does what it was derived to do, on the ramp, and it is bounded.* Under EB the bound entrants arrive at 18.0 m/s instead of 21.5, the Ruth St entrants that cannot stop within their lane at `b` fall 40 → 27 (6.7 → 4.6 %; the 27 are over the curve before the rule can see them, at the ramp's 22.2 m/s limit with `b` under 0.75, and `−b` cannot bring them back under it), the rule asks no target below 11.9 m/s on the ramp, nothing stands, no collision (0 in 28 runs), no lock. It binds nowhere but the seven Ruth St corridor-fleet runs and on 4 / 2 steps of the two-entrance corridor fleet at seeds 3 / 5: the fleet defaults' `b` of 1.67 puts the curve at 21.1 m/s at the Ruth St section start, above every fleet-default arrival (max 18.2 m/s), and on the 305 m T.H.52 lane at 31.8 m/s — so the T.H.52 corridor-demand and capacity rows, the two-entrance fleet defaults, the moderate fixture and the golden are byte-identical, and **the entrance criterion reads exactly as before: 395 / 401 / 373 of 466 at capacity against the 420 pinned, 403 / 388 / 401 of 470 at the corridor's demand**; the two-entrance corridor fleet's entrance moves 356 → 364 and 374 → 389 (with E1 253 → 271, 253 → 254) on 4 and 2 binding steps — the re-roll WP-55's note warned of, not the rule. Every earlier ramp-side brake (the fourth derivation's easing on the ramp, 407 / 416 / 402; the seventh's symmetric resolution) cost the entrance because it braked the entrant for a lane-1 vehicle; this one reads no other vehicle and costs the entrance nothing.

2. *And it reads worse where it binds, by a mechanism, not a re-roll.* Give-ups 44 → 54 (5,988 → 5,995 exits of 6,131 → 6,146 reached; the entrances 5,944 → 5,967; forced changes deferred 5,439 → 6,644; pair releases 218 → 270; unfinished 45 → 48; lane-1 minutes at or below 5 m/s 14 → 15). The rows: the Ruth St corridor fleet at the exit peak, **seed 3 — 9 → 5 given up, 273 → 283 exits, lane 1 at the gore 2.8 m/s in four minutes → 6.1 in none, 1,168 → 300 deferred, 32 → 1 releases, on 122 binding steps** — the best row this fixture has read in the series (v00981's chain of three is gone: bound on 12 steps from 22.1 m/s, it enters at 15.8 instead of 19.3 — 126 m to stop at its `b` of 0.99 against 130 m of lane, where before it needed 188 — and changes; v00947, `b` 0.59, bound on 12 steps from 21.5 and still over the curve at 18.0, halts as before, with one give-up beside it instead of two); **seed 4 — 1 → 12, 279 → 268 exits, lane 1 4.2 (1) → 1.0 m/s in four minutes, 402 → 1,757 deferred, 6 → 72 releases, on 86 steps**; seed 5 — 4 → 8 on 64 steps; the 271 m window — 8 → 4 (265 → 269, lane 1 2.1 (3) → 5.2 (0), 814 → 220 deferred) on 90 steps; the entrance peak: seed 3 — 0 → 1, seed 4 — lane 1 5.9 (0) → 1.0 m/s in four minutes (120 → 688 deferred, 1 → 34 releases) on 324 steps, seed 5 — lane 1 3.6 (1) → 13.6 (0) on 399 steps. Tens to hundreds of binding steps on every moved row, and the sign flips by seed on the same fixture. The mechanism on the rows that worsen is the sixth derivation's, moved: an entrant arriving at 18 m/s beside a lane-1 stream at 20–22 m/s is *slower* than the gap it chooses, so the gap's follower is held at `−b` for longer (seed 4 at the exit peak: 1,086 → 1,919 cooperation vehicle-steps, 292 → 993 eased, the exiters' mean wait 7.7 → 14.0 s) — lane 1 pays for the ramp's brake — and the exiters queued behind that lane-1 hold reach the gore's end beside the next halted entrant. On the rows that improve the same slower arrival drops in behind its gap's leader instead of riding abreast of it. Which happens is the seed's platoon geometry, not the rule.

3. *The pair does not form by momentum.* The chains under EB on seed 4 are headed by v00945 and v00960, entrants that can stop within the lane at their `b` with 38 m and 2 m to spare and were bound 0 and 5 steps; of the 17 pair-heading entrants 12 are under the curve at the section start. `ramp` makes the point from the other side: the bound from the ramp's start removes the fast arrivals almost entirely — 5 of 588 Ruth St entrants cannot stop (0.9 %), 3 of 16 pair-heading entrants — and reads 53 given up with **27 crossing pairs on 16 halted entrants**, 18 lane-1 minutes, 7,365 deferred. WP-55's finding stands as a description (the entrants heading a pair are the fast mode) and fails as a cause: an entrant at 18–20 m/s with a `b` of 1.3–3 halts at the end of a 136 m lane not because it cannot stop but because it finds no gap in 7 s and its forced change is refused by the speed-aware guard on every lane-1 vehicle arriving inside its brake distance — WP-54's persisting state, which the entry speed does not touch.

4. *The other forms.* `hold` (no `−b` brake on an entrant already over the curve; 541 binding steps) reads 59 given up with 27 lane-1 minutes and 8,280 deferred — it binds 108 entrants (`b` 1.31 on average, against EB's 187 at 1.09): the ones it no longer asks are the small-`b` arrivals already over the curve at the zone's entry, which reach the section 3.3 m/s over it on average against 2.9 under EB; worse than asking. `bx05` (half `b`; 9,928 steps on 1,015 entrants, the fleet defaults included) moves 21 rows: the Ruth St entrants arrive at 13.9 m/s on average (≥ 16 m/s: 77 → 13 %), the give-ups read 51 with 36 unfinished (the best) and 14 lane-1 minutes, the entrances 5,937 (the T.H.52 corridor demand at seed 3 403 → 391 on four binding steps — a re-roll; the capacity rows are byte-identical still, no binds), and the golden moves (115 steps on `weave.osm`: mean travel time 69.500 → 69.398 s, p90 84.126 → 87.872, σ_v 3.940 → 4.261, fuel 90.54 → 91.32, 19 / 19 / 2 / 0 against 14 / 19 / 2 / 6). `onEB` (with the exiter's yield on) reads 43 given up against the yield's own 39 (WP-56's `on`), 6,024 exits against 6,015, the entrances 5,996 against 5,973, 5,376 deferred against 4,590 — the yield and the bound do not add.

5. *Nothing ships.* `WEAVE_DEFAULTS["entry_speed_bound"]` = 0: the fixture grid at the default is `off` to the byte, golden `merge_weave` unchanged (hash 436cd4ec9e5d, `test_golden_summary_reproduced[merge_weave]` passes; with the key set the hash is d4c41a6213f6 and the metrics do not move — no entrant on `weave.osm` reaches the curve). The rule stays as a measured option with its counter, so a corridor battery can read where it binds. For the record: any rule of this series that ships on must pass a 20-seed corridor battery on a VM before it stays on — VM M's lesson (WP-56), one seed of twenty locked under a rule the fixtures had passed — and this package did not run the corridor.

**What this hands on.** Both sides of the crossing pair have now been measured from every angle the fixtures offer: the exiter's yield from the zone (helps, locks the corridor), from the brake distance, the halt time and the whole section (worse or a re-roll), the entrant's yield (infeasible in 22 of 24), and now the entrant's entry speed (bounded, cheap on the entrance, worse on the pairs, and the momentum hypothesis falsified — 12 of 17 pair-heading entrants can stop). What is left is the state itself, WP-54's persisting half: an entrant halted at the auxiliary lane's end, refused into lane 1 by the speed-aware guard on every lane-1 vehicle arriving inside its brake distance, heading a standing queue that the next exiters halt beside. Two readings of the numbers point at it: (a) the guard's follower side — a lane-1 vehicle arriving at 4–14 m/s inside its brake distance of a *standing* entrant is refused for ever, where a driver would let a halted vehicle in front of him once he is slow enough (a forced entry from rest accepted when the lane-1 follower's own IDM towards the entrant at its speed asks no more than `b`, re-derived for the halted case, and measured against the two Ruth St collisions the speed-aware acceptance closed); (b) the lane-1 hold that the rows which worsen here show — an entrant slower than its gap holds the follower at `−b` for the length of the section — is the same cooperation VM M's lock read as 18,259 yield steps, and a bound on how long a lane-1 follower may be held for one entrant (the fifth derivation's `pair_release_s` applied to the moving pair) has not been measured. The corridor round (VM, the 35-min I-94 slice, 4 seeds; and the 20-seed battery) reads `n_entry_bounded` now if anyone sets the key.

**Bookkeeping.** `packages/flowstate_core/flowstate_core/config.py` (`entry_speed_bound` = 0 with its provenance comment and docstring); `packages/microsim/microsim/runner.py` (`_weave_entry_speed_bound`, `_weave_entry_bound` new; the call in `_weave_step`'s approaching loop; `n_entry_bounded` in the section state and `_weave_meta`; the docstrings of `_weave_step` and `_weave_meta`); `packages/api/api/schemas.py` (`WeaveSectionDiagnosticsOut.n_entry_bounded`); `scripts/corridor_sweep.py` (`WEAVE_FIELDS`); `tests/test_microsim/test_microsim_merge_managed_meter.py` (`TestWeaveEntrySpeedBound`: the curve, the brake over it, the release under it and on the section, a target above the model not recorded, the default; the `WEAVE_DEFAULTS` pin; the fake state and the counters test); `tests/test_api/test_runs_merge_diagnostics.py`, `tests/test_scripts/test_corridor_sweep.py` (the key lists, 36 keys); docs/CONTRACTS.md §2 (the key list, the WP-57 paragraph, the API paragraph); CHANGELOG.md; this section. The T.H.52 tests' markers and pins are unchanged (their runs are byte-identical at the default and under the rule); golden `merge_weave` unchanged; `frontend/` untouched (the dashboard lists no weave counter). Session artifacts (the harness with the entry trace and the per-step bound trace, the six grids, the tables, the classifier) not committed.
